===== FILE: skills/analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/diagnose-pipeline-gap.md =====

---
title: Diagnose a pipeline gap
type: skill
function: analytics-and-campaign-evaluation
owner: marketing-analyst
reads: [goals-and-kpis, icp-and-segments, company]
you_bring: Your pipeline target, or last quarter's numbers if you have none, plus MQLs, SQLs and pipeline value so far from your CRM.
also_for: [pipeline is behind, pipeline is down, ceo asks why pipeline is down, no pipeline target, why is pipeline low, missing our pipeline target, pipeline shortfall, not enough leads, leads not converting, deal size dropped]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Splits a gap against a pipeline target, or last quarter, into volume, conversion and deal size, using numbers you paste, and names the skill to run next.
judge: ["Does the driver match what sales says is happening and if not which one is wrong?", "Is this a one-quarter dip or has the trend been there for months?", "Would the split look different if you read it again in two weeks?", "What is the cheapest thing you could check this week to test the likely cause?"]
show_to: your sales lead, before the CEO, and ask whether the driver matches what they see in deals
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when pipeline is behind target, or down on last quarter. Do not use it to judge one finished campaign; use [Evaluate a campaign](evaluate-campaign.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: target, SQL goal, definitions, baselines.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority.
- [[context-pack:company]]: which numbers are internal only.

The marketer pastes the read date, then MQLs, SQLs and pipeline value for the period so far, as goals-and-kpis.md defines them. With no target, also last quarter's three numbers. Optional: how SQLs were counted, numbers by segment, MQLs older than 14 days, rejection reasons.

## Procedure

1. Stop and ask if MQLs, SQLs or pipeline value are missing. If periods or stage names do not match, a lead ID repeats, or pipeline shows with zero SQLs, stop and say what to fix. If everything is zero, stop and ask whether the export is right.
2. Set the target line. With no target, use last quarter as the line and say so: its MQLs, conversion, deal size and pipeline become the targets. Otherwise, target deal size is the pipeline target divided by the SQL goal, or the average in the pipeline definition, or [deal size needed]. Target conversion is the marketer's rate, or else the baseline MQL to SQL rate. Target MQLs is the pipeline target divided by target conversion and target deal size. A zero or blank target stops the split: ask.
3. For a read before the period ends, scale target MQLs by days elapsed over days in the period (a starting rule). Use the marketer's phasing if given.
4. Work out the actuals. Conversion is SQLs divided by MQLs. Deal size is pipeline value divided by SQLs, at the value pipeline is counted, not contract value. With zero MQLs, conversion is "not defined". With zero SQLs, conversion is 0% and deal size is "not defined". A not-defined part's effect is $0. Name how SQLs were counted, and call conversion provisional if they come from older MQLs.
5. Split the gap in this order, and show the arithmetic:
   - Volume: (actual MQLs minus target MQLs) x target conversion x target deal size.
   - Conversion: actual MQLs x (actual conversion minus target conversion) x target deal size.
   - Deal size: actual MQLs x actual conversion x (actual deal size minus target deal size).
6. Mark each part Gap or Ahead, then test it. Recompute conversion on MQLs older than 14 days (a starting rule, or the MQL to SQL time in goals-and-kpis.md). If that meets target, mark conversion Lag. If the older count is missing, call the read provisional. Under 30 MQLs or 10 SQLs (a starting rule), mark conversion and deal size Too few to call, and read volume anyway. Zero SQLs from 30 or more MQLs is a conversion Gap.
7. Name the main driver: the largest part still marked Gap. If it is under 60% of the sum of the negative effects (a starting rule) and another part is Gap, name both, call it Mixed and start with the larger. If nothing is marked Gap, recommend no action.
8. If segment numbers are pasted, name the segment holding most of the driver.
9. Name who to ask why, and one skill to run first:
   - Volume: ask sales which sources dried up. Run [Read attribution](read-attribution.md), then [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md), or [Allocate the budget](../budget-and-planning/allocate-budget.md) if money is the limit.
   - Conversion: ask the head of sales for the last 30 rejected MQLs with reasons. Run [Define the lead handoff](../marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md), or [Define the ICP](../segmentation-icp-and-personas/define-icp.md) if they are the wrong companies, or [Write a lead follow-up](../campaign-design/write-lead-follow-up.md) if they wait for first contact.
   - Deal size: ask account executives what changed in recent deals. Run [Analyze wins and losses](../competitive-intelligence/analyze-win-loss.md).
10. Any cause is an Inference or an Unknown until the person in step 9 answers.
11. Write what to tell the CEO, in two or three plain sentences: the gap, where it is, and what you are checking by when.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: definitions, baselines and internal-only numbers. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Read date: <date, and day of the period>
Target: <amount and date, or "No target set. Line used: last quarter, <amount>">
Answer: <gap, main driver and the skill to run first, up to 30 words>
What to tell the CEO: <two or three plain sentences>

## The numbers
| Measure | Target to date | Actual | Difference |
<rows: MQLs, conversion, SQLs, deal size, pipeline>
SQLs counted by: <stage date, MQL date or Unknown>

## The split
| Part | Effect | Share of shortfall | Arithmetic | Read |
<rows: volume, conversion, deal size, total; Read is Gap, Ahead, Lag or Too few to call>
Lag check: <conversion on older MQLs against target, or "Not pasted">

By segment: <where the driver sits, or "Not pasted">

## Find out why, then run next
This split shows where the gap is, not why.
Ask: <who, and what to ask>
First: <skill title>, because <reason>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do the three effects add up exactly to the pipeline difference?
- [ ] Is every actual a number the marketer pasted, used as goals-and-kpis.md defines it?
- [ ] Are zero counts, lag and small samples marked before a driver is named?
- [ ] Is every cause written as an Inference or an Unknown?
- [ ] Does the output name one skill to run first, and why?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Read date:** February 14, 2027, day 45 of 90

**Target:** $1.2 million by March 31, 2027

**Answer:** $180,000 behind to date, mainly conversion. Define the lead handoff first.

**What to tell the CEO:** We are $180,000 behind at the halfway point, mostly because fewer MQLs become SQLs. I am asking Tom Ferrant why, and will report back this week.

**The numbers:** MQLs 150 against 173. Conversion 20% against 26%. SQLs 30 against 45. Deal size $14,000 against $13,333. Pipeline $420,000 against $600,000.

**The split**

| Part | Effect | Share | Arithmetic | Read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Volume | -$80,000 | 40% | (150 - 173.08) x 26% x $13,333 | Gap |
| Conversion | -$120,000 | 60% | 150 x (20% - 26%) x $13,333 | Gap |
| Deal size | +$20,000 | None | 30 x ($14,000 - $13,333) | Ahead |

Lag check: 30 SQLs from 130 older MQLs is 23%, still under 26%.

**Find out why, then run next**

Ask: Tom Ferrant for the rejected MQLs and reasons.

First: [Define the lead handoff](../marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md), because conversion misses target.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all actuals are invented. Target conversion is the 26% baseline.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Diagnose a pipeline gap". Read the context pack.
Task: split the gap against <target and date> into volume, conversion and deal size, using the numbers pasted below.
```


===== FILE: skills/analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/evaluate-campaign.md =====

---
title: Evaluate a campaign
type: skill
function: analytics-and-campaign-evaluation
owner: marketing-analyst
reads: [goals-and-kpis, icp-and-segments, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The campaign brief, its results by channel with the date pulled, and anything that disturbed the numbers.
also_for: [did the campaign work, campaign results, campaign roi, campaign post-mortem, was it worth the money, pipeline from a campaign, what to do after a campaign]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Compares a finished campaign's results with its targets, says what worked and sets what to do next.
judge: ["Would these leads have come anyway without the campaign?", "Are you judging the campaign or the timing and the list it went to?", "Would you run it again if the money were your own?", "Did sales notice this campaign and did it change any conversation?"]
show_to: the salesperson who got the most leads from it, and ask whether those leads were worth the call
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this after a campaign's time window has closed and you have its numbers, and again on any later read date. Do not use it for weekly spend decisions on a live paid campaign, which belong to [Adjust paid campaigns](../paid-demand-gen/adjust-paid-campaigns.md), or for credit across all channels, which belongs to [Read attribution](read-attribution.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL, SQL and pipeline definitions, stage timing, plus KPI baselines for comparison.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: ICP criteria for judging lead quality, and the typical sales cycle.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels used, paid limits and hard rules.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- The campaign brief, or its goal, audience, time window, targets and budget.
- Results by channel: spend, leads, MQLs, SQLs and pipeline, with the date pulled.
- Anything that disturbed the results, such as a tracking gap.

## Procedure

1. If the targets or the results are missing, ask for them before drafting. If only part is missing, mark that row "no data". Never reconstruct a target from the results. Check the numbers: one period, stage definitions as in goals-and-kpis.md, no repeated lead ID, totals that add up. If not, say what to fix, and mark those rows "no data". Ask for a missing pull date.
2. Fill the results table with one row per target in the brief. Difference is actual minus target, as a number and a percentage.
3. Set each row's read date: the date the brief promised. If it promised none, use the end date for leads and MQLs, the end date plus the long end of the stage timing in goals-and-kpis.md for SQLs and pipeline, or the end date plus the long end of the sales cycle for won deals. If the timing is blank, write [stage timing needed] and ask.
4. Set each row's status. Before its read date, use "too early". On or after it, use "hit" at or above target and "missed" below it, with the number at that date. Use "no data" when the number was not supplied. Leads keep maturing after a deadline, so give a missed SQL, pipeline or won-deal row a later read: the end date plus that same time. Report the later result on its own line. It never changes the status.
5. Set the verdict from the status of the campaign's one goal metric, and name its read date if too early or its later read if missed.
6. Calculate cost per MQL for each channel with spend. Show the division. Compare a rate with a baseline only if both use the same definition.
7. Write what worked and what did not. Each bullet needs a number from the table. Under 20 leads in a channel (a starting rule), write "too few to read" and draw no conclusion. Say "coincided with" rather than "caused" unless the marketer supplied a control group.
8. Write next actions. Each one starts with keep, change or stop. Stop a channel only when it missed its target, its later read has passed and it has enough leads to read. Name an owner from the marketer's input, or write [owner needed].
9. Check the draft against the context pack: terms match goals-and-kpis.md, actions respect the limits in channels-and-tools.md, and lead quality uses the ICP criteria. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder and ask in the last section.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section, including any row with no data and the date the numbers were pulled.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Verdict
<Hit, missed or too early, then the goal metric's number. Name its read date if too early, its later read if missed. Max 40 words.>

## Results against targets
| Metric | Target | Read date | Actual | Difference | Status |
<One row per target. Status is hit, missed, too early or no data.>
Later read: <metric, date, and the matured result with its pull date once that date has passed, or "None">

## Cost by channel
| Channel | Spend | Leads | MQLs | Cost per MQL |
<One row per channel with spend. Write "not supplied" for gaps.>

## What worked
<Up to 3 bullets, max 25 words each, each with a number.>

## What did not work
<Up to 3 bullets, max 25 words each, each with a number.>

## Next actions
| Action | Keep, change or stop | Owner | By when |
<Up to 5 rows.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the verdict rest on the campaign's one goal metric?
- [ ] Does every target in the brief have a row, with status set by the rule?
- [ ] Is each row read on its own date, with a missed deadline called missed and maturing leads given a later read?
- [ ] Do MQL, SQL and pipeline match the definitions in goals-and-kpis.md?
- [ ] Does every next action have an owner or a visible placeholder?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: this CogniATion webinar and all its results are illustrative, as if pasted on November 1, 2026, against a brief promising its targets by October 31.

**Verdict**

Missed at October 31: pipeline $80,000 against $125,000. Later read November 14, for leads still qualifying.

**Results against targets**

| Metric | Target | Read date | Actual | Difference | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MQLs | 40 | Oct 31 | 46 | +6, +15% | Hit |
| SQLs | 10 | Oct 31 | 6 | -4, -40% | Missed |
| Pipeline | $125,000 | Oct 31 | $80,000 | -$45,000, -36% | Missed |

Later read: SQLs and pipeline, November 14.

**Cost by channel**

| Channel | Spend | Leads | MQLs | Cost per MQL |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Analyst reasoning webinar | $4,500 | 88 | 46 | $98 |

**What worked**

- Webinar topic drew 46 MQLs against a target of 40.

**What did not work**

- 6 SQLs from 46 MQLs is 13% so far, against the 26% baseline, with 13 days of qualifying left.

**Next actions**

| Action | Keep, change or stop | Owner | By when |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keep the analyst reasoning topic for the next webinar | Keep | Marketer | January 2027 |
| Read SQLs and pipeline again | Keep | Marketer | November 14, 2026 |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the campaign ended October 15, 2026.
- Estimate: $4,500 / 46 = $98. Later read is the end date plus the 30-day MQL to SQL time.
- Unknown: How many of the 42 leads short of MQL missed the ICP?
- Unknown: How many of the 46 MQLs has sales called so far?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Evaluate a campaign". Read the context pack.
Task: <the campaign and its time window>.
<paste the brief and results by channel here>
```


===== FILE: skills/analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/read-attribution.md =====

---
title: Read attribution
type: skill
function: analytics-and-campaign-evaluation
owner: marketing-analyst
reads: [goals-and-kpis, icp-and-segments, personas, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: A CRM export with first and last touch pipeline by channel, the date pulled, and answers to how did you hear about us if you collect them.
also_for: [which channel drives pipeline, what is working, where do deals come from, channel credit, first touch vs last touch, how did you hear about us, ceo asks which channel works]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Reads first and last touch data side by side and says what can and cannot be concluded from it.
judge: ["Would the channel with the credit have worked without the one with none?", "Where do your best customers say they first heard of you and does that match this?", "Which channel has the most money riding on the least reliable credit?", "Is direct traffic hiding something you cannot track such as word of mouth?"]
show_to: a salesperson, and ask what recent buyers said made them first reach out
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you have pipeline or revenue by channel and need to know how far the credit can be trusted. Do not use it to judge one campaign against its targets, which belongs to [Evaluate a campaign](evaluate-campaign.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: attribution model, pipeline definition.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: typical sales cycle, for data maturity.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: where buyers get information.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels in use, tools that record touches.

The marketer pastes:

- A CRM export or summary with first and last touch pipeline and opportunities for each channel.
- Answers to "how did you hear about us", counted by channel, if you collect them.
- The period and the date pulled.
- Tracking changes, and when each campaign started.

## Procedure

1. Confirm the model in goals-and-kpis.md. If it is blank, ask. If the export holds only one of the two touches, say the comparison cannot be made, write "not supplied" in the missing column and limit conclusions to the touch supplied.
2. Fill the credit table with one row per channel, plus a row for direct or unknown and a total row. Show dollars and opportunities for each touch, and the count of "how did you hear about us" answers, or "not collected".
3. Compare the two totals. If they differ, report the gap and ask why. Never add first and last touch together.
4. Label each channel from its share of first touch and last touch dollars. "More first touch" means first touch share is at least 1.5 times last touch share. "More last touch" means the reverse. "Both" means anything in between. "Too few" means fewer than 5 opportunities on both touches, and it overrides the other labels. These cutoffs and the 25% in step 5 are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks.
5. Apply two data warnings. If direct or unknown is above 25% of either total, label that row "warning" and say channel conclusions are weak. If a campaign started fewer days before the pull date than the short end of the sales cycle, mark it "still maturing" and draw no conclusion from it.
6. Write what can be concluded. Use only statements the data shows, in the form "[channel] was first touch on [number] opportunities worth [amount]". Label anything further as an inference and name its inputs.
7. Write what cannot be concluded. Always include these points: the data does not show that a channel caused pipeline, or whether a deal would have happened anyway, and it says nothing about touches between the first and the last. Add return on spend unless spend was supplied.
8. Name the blind spots. List each information source from personas.md that tracking cannot record, and say that credit for those conversations lands on a later touch. Count answers naming those sources. Answers are self-reported, not credit.
9. Write up to 3 actions, each framed as a test with the number to watch and when to read it. Never recommend cutting a channel from a single touch, or from a channel labeled too few or still maturing.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: pipeline and model wording match goals-and-kpis.md, and channel names match channels-and-tools.md. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder such as [pull date needed] and ask in the last section. Never invent.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Bottom line
<What the data supports and how far to trust it. Max 40 words.>

## Credit by channel
| Channel | First touch | Last touch | How did you hear about us | Label |
<Dollars and opportunities in each touch column. Include direct or unknown and a total row. Answer counts or "not collected". Label is more first touch, more last touch, both, too few, still maturing or warning.>

## What can be concluded
<Up to 4 bullets, max 25 words each.>

## What cannot be concluded
<Up to 4 bullets, max 25 words each.>

## Blind spots
<Up to 3 bullets, max 20 words each.>

## Tests to run
| Test | Number to watch | Read on |
<Up to 3 rows.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Are first and last touch shown side by side and never summed?
- [ ] Do the two totals match, or is the gap reported?
- [ ] Does every channel label follow the share rule?
- [ ] Is direct or unknown shown as its own row, with the warning applied?
- [ ] Is causation left out of the conclusions?
- [ ] Are the blind spots taken from personas.md?
- [ ] Is every action framed as a test, with no cut based on one touch?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: the $900,000 total is CogniATion's Q3 2026 pipeline baseline. The split by channel and the answers are illustrative.

**Bottom line**

Webinars and email appear more as last touch, organic search and social ads as first. Unknown touches top 25% of first touch, so channel conclusions are weak.

**Credit by channel**

| Channel | First touch | Last touch | How did you hear about us | Label |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Search ads | $252,000 (19) | $180,000 (14) | 9 | Both |
| Organic search | $198,000 (15) | $126,000 (9) | 6 | More first touch |
| Social network ads | $108,000 (8) | $54,000 (4) | 4 | More first touch |
| Webinars | $72,000 (5) | $216,000 (16) | 6 | More last touch |
| Email | $27,000 (2) | $135,000 (10) | 1 | More last touch |
| Direct or unknown | $243,000 (19) | $189,000 (15) | 24 | Warning |
| Total | $900,000 (68) | $900,000 (68) | 50 | |

**What can be concluded**

- Webinars were last touch on 16 opportunities worth $216,000.

**What cannot be concluded**

- Whether webinars caused those deals, or whether they would have happened anyway.

**Blind spots**

- Peers and learning and development associations leave no tracked touch. 18 of the 24 unknown-row answers name them.

**Tests to run**

| Test | Number to watch | Read on |
|---|---|---|
| Invite search ad leads to a webinar for heads of learning | MQL to SQL rate, baseline 26% | End of Q1 2027 |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Estimate: 68 opportunities is 260 MQLs times the 26% rate, both Q3 2026 baselines.
- Unknown: How much of direct or unknown comes from untagged links?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Read attribution". Read the context pack.
Task: <the period, the date pulled, and any tracking changes>.
<paste the CRM export here>
```


===== FILE: skills/analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/specify-dashboard.md =====

---
title: Specify a dashboard
type: skill
function: analytics-and-campaign-evaluation
owner: marketing-analyst
reads: [goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools, company]
you_bring: Who will look at the dashboard, the decision each person makes from it, and the tool you will build it in.
also_for: [marketing dashboard, kpi dashboard, reporting setup, numbers do not match, what to track, ceo wants a dashboard, pipeline report, metric definitions]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Writes a dashboard spec with metrics, definitions, sources and views, which the marketer builds in their own tool.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before building or rebuilding a marketing dashboard, so the definitions are settled first. Do not use it to read the numbers themselves, which belongs to [Write the monthly report](write-monthly-report.md), or to fix link tagging, which belongs to [Set tracking conventions](../marketing-ops/set-tracking-conventions.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: KPIs, definitions, baselines, targets and review cadence.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: which tool does which job, and who has access.
- [[context-pack:company]]: which numbers are internal only and which must never appear.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- Who will look at the dashboard, and the decision each person makes from it.
- The tool it will be built in.
- Field names or export column headers from each source tool, if available.

## Procedure

1. Write the purpose: one audience and the decisions it supports. If the audiences need different numbers, give each its own view rather than a separate metric list.
2. List metrics. Start with every KPI in goals-and-kpis.md. Add another metric only if the marketer names a decision it changes. Cap the list at 12, and record what you cut.
3. Give each metric a definition, copied from goals-and-kpis.md where one exists. If none exists, write a proposed definition and label it an assumption. Never change a definition silently.
4. Give each metric a source, using the tool by job from channels-and-tools.md: pipeline and SQLs from the CRM, visits from the web analytics tool, and so on. If the marketer supplied field names, name the field. Otherwise write [field to confirm]. If a metric needs data from two tools, mark it "manual join" and name the shared field.
5. Set the refresh from the KPI's review cadence. A KPI reviewed weekly refreshes weekly, and so on.
6. Set the target from goals-and-kpis.md. If a KPI has only a baseline, show the baseline and write "no target set". Do not make one up.
7. Lay out the views. Make one view per review cadence that has KPIs, in order: weekly, monthly, quarterly. Choose the display by this rule: a goal shows as one number against target and pace, a trend shows as a line over time, and a comparison across channels shows as a table or bars.
8. Set access. A number marked internal only in company.md goes only in views for people inside the company. A number marked never to be mentioned stays off the dashboard. Flag any metric whose source tool the viewer cannot open, as listed in channels-and-tools.md.
9. Write the build check: recalculate each KPI for the last closed period and compare it with its baseline in goals-and-kpis.md. A mismatch means the definition or the source is wrong, so fix that before anyone reads the dashboard.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: every KPI is present, definitions are unchanged and tool jobs match. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder such as [target needed] and ask in the last section. Never invent.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section, including each unconfirmed field.

## Output format

The marketer builds the dashboard from it.

```
Status: Draft

## Purpose
<Audience and the decisions it supports. Max 40 words.>

## Metrics
| Metric | Definition | Source | Refresh | Target or baseline |
<Up to 12 rows. Definition max 20 words.>

## Views
<One bullet per view: name, cadence, metrics in order, display for each. Max 40 words each.>

## Access
<Up to 4 bullets, max 25 words each.>

## Build check
<Up to 5 numbered steps, max 25 words each.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is every KPI in goals-and-kpis.md on the list?
- [ ] Are the definitions copied without silent changes?
- [ ] Does every metric have a source, with unconfirmed fields marked?
- [ ] Does each added metric have a named decision behind it?
- [ ] Does each view match a review cadence?
- [ ] Are internal-only numbers limited and never-mention numbers absent?
- [ ] Does the build check reproduce a known baseline?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: CogniATion's marketer asked for one dashboard for the marketer and the head of sales, with no field names supplied.

**Purpose**

Marketer and Tom Ferrant, head of sales: decide each week whether pipeline is on pace and where to shift effort.

**Metrics**

| Metric | Definition | Source | Refresh | Target or baseline |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| New qualified pipeline | Value of opportunities accepted by sales, created this quarter, with marketing as first or last touch | CRM, [field to confirm] | Weekly | $1.2 million by March 31, 2027 |
| MQLs | Fits the ICP and has requested a demo, attended a webinar or downloaded two resources | CRM, [field to confirm] | Weekly | Baseline 260 |
| Website demo requests | No definition in goals-and-kpis.md, assumed completed demo forms | Web analytics tool, [field to confirm] | Weekly | Baseline 58 |
| MQL to SQL rate | SQLs divided by MQLs | CRM, [field to confirm] | Monthly | Baseline 26% |

**Views**

- Weekly: pipeline against pace, then MQLs and demo requests as lines.
- Monthly: MQL to SQL rate as a line.

**Access**

- The web analytics tool is marketer only, so the head of sales sees demo requests through the shared view.
- No view shows an individual learner's conversation or score.
- Runway and investor terms stay off the dashboard.

**Build check**

1. Recalculate Q3 2026 MQLs. The result should be 260.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the demo request and rate definitions above. Sources are mapped from tool jobs. Other KPIs are left out here, condensed for length.
- Unknown: Does the dashboard tool read the web analytics tool directly, or do you paste exports?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Specify a dashboard". Read the context pack.
Task: <specify a dashboard for whom, to support which decisions>.
```


===== FILE: skills/analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/write-monthly-report.md =====

---
title: Write the monthly report
type: skill
function: analytics-and-campaign-evaluation
owner: marketing-analyst
reads: [goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools, company]
you_bring: The month's actual for each KPI, quarter-to-date totals, spend by channel, and who will read the report.
also_for: [monthly report, board update, ceo update, report to leadership, month end numbers, are we on track, pipeline vs goal, marketing performance]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Reports one closed month against goals-and-kpis.md, with short commentary and the decisions it calls for.
judge: ["If the CEO reads only the headline will they know what to do differently?", "Is one month enough to call this a trend or is it noise?", "Which number here are you hoping nobody asks about?", "What decision would change if this report were never written?"]
show_to: your CEO or manager, and ask which decision it helps them make
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this once a month, after the month has closed and you can pull its numbers. Do not use it to judge one campaign, which belongs to [Evaluate a campaign](evaluate-campaign.md), or to set the coming week's work, which belongs to [Set weekly priorities](../budget-and-planning/set-weekly-priorities.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: quarterly goals, definitions, KPI baselines and review cadence.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: planned paid spend by channel.
- [[context-pack:company]]: which numbers are publishable, internal only or never to be mentioned.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- The month being reported, and who will read the report.
- The month's actual for each KPI, plus quarter-to-date totals for each goal.
- Actual spend by channel, and planned spend if it differs from the context pack.
- Events that moved the numbers, such as a tracking gap, a launch or a staffing change.

## Procedure

1. Write one KPI row for every KPI in goals-and-kpis.md. Report KPIs reviewed weekly or monthly as the month's figure. Report a KPI reviewed quarterly only in the last month of a quarter, and otherwise write "next quarterly review". If an actual is missing, write [actual needed]. Never estimate an actual.
2. Convert baselines to a monthly figure. Divide a count or dollar baseline from a full quarter by 3 and label it an estimate. Compare rates directly, without dividing.
3. Write one goal row for every quarterly goal. Pace to date is the goal multiplied by months elapsed, divided by 3. For a dated goal that is not a number, such as a campaign publish date, use the marketer's status or write [status needed].
4. Set each numeric goal's status from actual divided by pace. "On pace" is 100% or more, "at risk" is 80% to 99%, and "off pace" is below 80%. These cutoffs and the 10% in step 7 are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks.
5. Fill the spend table. Difference is actual minus planned.
6. Write the headline last, and put it first in the output. It names the goal furthest behind pace and the number that shows it. If every goal is on pace, it names the goal with the smallest margin.
7. Write commentary only for goals at risk or off pace, and for KPIs that differ from their monthly baseline by more than 10%. Explain a change only from what the marketer supplied. Label any explanation you worked out yourself as an inference, and otherwise write [reason not supplied].
8. Write up to 3 decisions the numbers call for. Each names the choice, your recommendation and the date it must be made by. Write "None this month" if no goal is at risk or off pace and no spend line is over plan.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: definitions match goals-and-kpis.md, and no number marked "never mention" in company.md appears. Mark the report "Internal" at the top if it holds any internal-only number. If it will go outside the company, use only publishable numbers. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder and ask in the last section. Never invent.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section, including each baseline you divided and the date the numbers were pulled.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Headline
<Audience marking, month, the goal furthest behind pace and its number. Max 50 words.>

## Goals
| Goal | Target | To date | Pace to date | Status |
<One row per quarterly goal. Status is on pace, at risk or off pace.>

## KPIs
| KPI | Month actual | Monthly baseline | Difference |
<One row per KPI in goals-and-kpis.md.>

## Spend
| Channel | Planned | Actual | Difference |
<One row per paid channel, then a total.>

## Commentary
<Up to 4 bullets, max 30 words each.>

## Decisions needed
| Decision | Recommendation | Decide by |
<Up to 3 rows, or "None this month".>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every KPI and quarterly goal in goals-and-kpis.md have a row?
- [ ] Does the headline name the goal furthest behind pace, with its number?
- [ ] Is every status set by the pace rule rather than by feel?
- [ ] Is every divided baseline labeled an estimate?
- [ ] Is every explanation either supplied by the marketer or labeled an inference?
- [ ] Is every number's audience correct under company.md?
- [ ] Does each decision have a recommendation and a date?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: all of CogniATion's January 2027 actuals are illustrative, as if pasted by the marketer. Marked Internal because company.md does not list pipeline or SQLs as publishable.

**Headline**

Internal. January 2027: pipeline is the goal furthest behind, at $352,000 against a pace of $400,000.

**Goals**

| Goal | Target | To date | Pace to date | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pipeline | $1.2 million | $352,000 | $400,000 | At risk (88%) |
| SQLs | 90 | 27 | 30 | At risk (90%) |
| Professional services campaign | February 15, 2027 | [status needed] | n/a | [status needed] |

**KPIs**

| KPI | Month actual | Monthly baseline | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| MQLs | 97 | 87 | +10 |
| MQL to SQL rate | 28% | 26% | +2 points |
| Website demo requests | 21 | 19 | +2 |

**Spend**

| Channel | Planned | Actual | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Search ads | $5,000 | $5,200 | +$200 |
| Social network ads | $4,000 | $4,000 | $0 |
| Total | $9,000 | $9,200 | +$200 |

**Commentary**

- MQLs beat the baseline by 10, yet 90 SQLs still needs about 107 MQLs a month at this rate.

**Decisions needed**

| Decision | Recommendation | Decide by |
|---|---|---|
| Add a late March webinar for heads of learning? | Yes, MQL volume is the gap | February 5, 2027 |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: other KPIs are left out here, condensed for length.
- Estimate: monthly baselines are Q3 2026 figures divided by 3. Pace is the goal divided by 3. MQL need is 90 / 0.28 / 3.
- Unknown: Why did search ads run $200 over plan?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write the monthly report". Read the context pack.
Task: <the month, who will read it, and what moved the numbers>.
<paste the KPI actuals and spend here>
```


===== FILE: skills/brand-and-design-briefs/codify-brand-voice.md =====

---
title: Codify the brand voice
type: skill
function: brand-and-design-briefs
owner: brand-and-creative-director
reads: [company, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice]
you_bring: At least five writing samples, each marked with its channel and date, or with none, three lines the founders would publish and three they never would.
also_for: [tone of voice, style guide, we all sound different, copy sounds generic, how should we sound, writing guidelines, ai sounds robotic]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Builds or updates brand-voice.md from writing samples the marketer pastes, with evidence for every rule.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when brand-voice.md is blank, or when the company's writing has changed and the file no longer describes it. Do not use it to check one piece of writing against the voice; use [Review any output](../marketing-ops/review-any-output.md) instead. Do not use it to change what the company claims; use [Build a messaging framework](../positioning-and-messaging/build-messaging-framework.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: the current file, if filled, to compare against the samples.
- [[context-pack:company]]: public name.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who the writing is for, to set the reading level.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: which quotes belong to customers, not the company.

The marketer supplies: at least five samples the company has published or would publish, each marked with its channel and date, plus any lines it would never publish and any visual rules.

## Procedure

1. Sort the samples. Remove customer quotes, including any in proof-points.md, legal text and other companies' writing. Mark each remaining sample with its channel. If fewer than five remain, ask for more. If the marketer wants a draft anyway, mark every trait "low evidence". With no samples, ask the founders to write three lines they would publish and three they never would, and work from those, low evidence.
2. Find what repeats. For each sample, note sentence length, point of view, use of jargon, use of numbers, how headlines are written, and what the writing does with the reader. Keep only a pattern seen in at least three samples. Five samples and three repeats are starting rules the marketer can change.
3. Choose three traits. Each trait must be one adjective plus what it means in practice, must come from a pattern in step 2, and must rule something out. Reject any trait that nearly every company would claim, such as "professional" or "friendly".
4. Attach evidence. For each trait, quote one short line from a sample. If you cannot quote one, drop the trait.
5. Set the reading level from the personas.md reader the samples speak to most, as one line naming the reader and where they read.
6. Set the point of view from what the samples do. If they disagree, use the one in the majority and flag the rest.
7. Write three to five Do rules and three to five Do not rules. Each must be something a writer can check on a sentence. Refer to words to avoid in messaging.md, without repeating the list.
8. Write style details from the samples: spelling, capitalization, product name. Where the samples are inconsistent, pick the most frequent form and flag the choice.
9. Choose "sounds like us" from the samples, word for word. Choose "does not sound like us" from lines the marketer supplied. If there are none, write one and mark it "written by the model" under assumptions.
10. Fill visual basics only from the prompt or the current file. Writing samples say nothing about color or logos, so otherwise leave [visual rules needed]. If any other field is blank and the samples do not settle it, leave a visible placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.
11. If brand-voice.md is already filled, list each change and the sample that supports it. Keep a rule the samples do not contradict. Remove a rule only if the marketer agrees.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: the voice must not contradict messaging.md, and no customer's words may be used as the company's.
13. List everything you assumed, and each low-evidence rule, in the last section.

The marketer copies the file section into brand-voice.md.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Brand voice file
Copy this into brand-voice.md.

### Voice
Voice in three traits:
- <Trait>: <what it means in practice, 15 words maximum>
Reading level: <one line>
Point of view: <one line>

### Rules
Do:
- <3 to 5 habits, one line each>
Do not:
- <3 to 5 habits, one line each>
Style details: <spelling, capitalization, product name>

### Examples
Sounds like us: <one or two sentences, word for word from a sample>
Does not sound like us: <one or two sentences>

### Visual basics
Colors, type and logo rules: <from the prompt or the current file, or [visual rules needed]>

## Evidence
| Trait or rule | Quoted line, 15 words maximum | Sample channel and date |
<One row for each trait, and for each rule resting on a sample>

## Changes from the current file
- <Change, and the sample behind it. Write "New file" if brand-voice.md was blank>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Could a writer check each Do and Do not rule on a single sentence?
- [ ] Does each trait rule something out, and have a quoted line behind it?
- [ ] Is every quoted line from the company's own writing, with no customer's words?
- [ ] Is the "sounds like us" line word for word from a sample?
- [ ] Are the visual rules from the marketer or the current file, not guessed?
- [ ] Does the file agree with words to avoid in messaging.md?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Brand voice file**

**Voice**

Voice in three traits:

- Plain: short words, no learning jargon.
- Curious: asks a real question where others would make a claim.
- Respectful: assumes the reader is capable, and never talks down about AI users.

Reading level: a busy head of learning reading between meetings.

Point of view: "we" for CogniATion, "you" for the reader.

**Rules**

Do: name the work (the memo, the decision, the client call). Use numbers only from proof-points.md or the publishable list in company.md.

Do not: no exclamation points. No warnings that AI is making people stupid.

Style details: CogniATion, never Cogniation or COGNIATION. The Coach on second mention.

**Examples**

Sounds like us: Your analysts have answers. Can they defend them?

Does not sound like us: Unlock your team's cognitive potential with next-generation AI.

**Visual basics**

Primary deep blue, secondary warm gray, one sans-serif typeface. Logo on plain backgrounds only, never on photos. No images of glowing brains or robots.

**Evidence**

| Trait | Quoted line | Sample |
|---|---|---|
| Plain | "It asks, it does not answer" | messaging.md, channel unknown |
| Curious | "Your analysts have answers. Can they defend them?" | brand-voice.md, channel unknown |

**Changes from the current file**

None.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: lines from messaging.md and brand-voice.md stand in for pasted samples, so all traits are low evidence.
- Unknown: can you paste five published samples?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Codify the brand voice". Read the context pack.
Task: <any lines the company would never publish, and any visual rules>.
<paste five or more samples here, each with channel and date>
```


===== FILE: skills/brand-and-design-briefs/write-design-brief.md =====

---
title: Write a design brief
type: skill
function: brand-and-design-briefs
owner: brand-and-creative-director
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: What you need made, where it will appear, the sizes and file formats, the due date, and the final copy.
also_for: [brief a designer, freelancer brief, booth design, ad creative, banner sizes, social images, landing page design, need graphics]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a brief with final copy, specifications and dates that a designer can execute without a meeting.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you need a designer to make something specific: a booth panel, an ad set, a set of social images. Do not use it when the words are still undecided; write them first with the matching skill, such as [Write a web page](../content/write-web-page.md). If the campaign itself is not defined, use [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: visual basics, voice and style details.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: the main message and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: what may appear in public.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does and is not, for the images.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who will see the piece.
- [[context-pack:company]]: the designer's time and public name.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: campaign and event dates this quarter.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: the approver and typical time.

The marketer supplies: what is needed, where it will appear, the exact sizes and file formats, the due date, the copy if it already exists, and any files on hand.

## Procedure

1. State the job in one sentence: what the piece must get one named audience to notice or do. If the prompt asks for several unrelated jobs, write one brief for the first and list the others as open questions.
2. List each piece with its size, file format, quantity and where it will appear. Take sizes and formats only from the prompt. Otherwise leave [size needed] or [format needed].
3. Write the copy word for word, with its order on the piece. Mark each line "final" or "[copy needed]". The designer must never have to write or edit a line.
4. Choose the one thing the reader should see first, then rank everything else behind it, with no more than three levels. Cut anything that will not fit the piece, and say what you cut.
5. Add proof only from proof-points.md marked public, with its date or sample, and copy any quote word for word. Permission for a name or a logo does not cover photos of that customer's staff or offices. Ask for that separately, and leave [photo permission needed].
6. Copy the visual rules from brand-voice.md as written. If they are blank, leave [visual rules needed] and ask in the last section. Do not suggest named typefaces or color codes that the file does not give.
7. Write the "do not show" list: everything brand-voice.md rules out, anything the product does not do according to product.md, and any competitor name.
8. List the files the designer will receive and what is still missing, with who owes it and by when.
9. Set dates working back from the due date: first version, marketer's comments, final files. Allow for the approver's time from channels-and-tools.md. Allow two rounds of changes, a starting rule the marketer can change. If the dates do not fit, say which piece to cut.
10. Compare the work with the designer's time in company.md. Use the designer's own estimate if the marketer supplies it, otherwise leave [designer's estimate needed].
11. If any other needed context pack field is blank, leave a visible placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: every name, number, quote and image rule against its source, and all the copy against words to avoid.
13. List everything you assumed, and everything a designer would still have to ask, in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Job: <one sentence, 25 words maximum>
Audience: <who sees it, where, in what setting, one line>
Sees first: <the one thing, one line>

## Pieces
| Piece | Size | File format | Quantity | Where it appears |
<One row per piece, or placeholders>

## Copy, word for word
<Piece name>
1. <Line, marked final or [copy needed]>
<Lines in reading order, three levels of importance at most>

## Visual rules
- <Copied from brand-voice.md>

## Do not show
- <Up to 6 items>

## Files provided and files missing
- <File, who has it, or who owes it and by when>

## Dates
| Step | Date | Who |
| First version | <date> | Designer |
| Comments | <date> | Marketer |
| Final files | <date> | Designer |
| Approval | <date, allowing for typical time> | <approver> |
Rounds of changes: <number>
Designer's time: <estimate or [designer's estimate needed], against available time>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Could the designer start without asking a single question?
- [ ] Is every line of copy word for word, or a visible placeholder?
- [ ] Does each piece have a size, file format and quantity, or a visible placeholder?
- [ ] Is it clear what the reader should see first?
- [ ] Is every customer name, number and quote public in proof-points.md, with its date or sample?
- [ ] Do the visual rules match brand-voice.md exactly?
- [ ] Do the dates allow for the approver's time and the designer's available days?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Job: get heads of learning to stop and talk about showing that training worked.

Audience: learning leaders at the regional learning and development conference, March 9 to 11, 2027.

Sees first: the headline.

**Pieces**

| Piece | Size | File format | Quantity | Where it appears |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Booth back panel | [size needed] | [format needed] | 1 | Booth |

**Copy, word for word**

Booth back panel

1. Your analysts have answers. Can they defend them? Final.
2. CogniATion Coach is an AI chatbot that won't give you the answer, so you learn to find it. Final.
3. "My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory. Final.

**Visual rules**

- Primary deep blue, secondary warm gray, one sans-serif typeface.
- Logo on plain backgrounds only, never on photos.
- No images of glowing brains or robots.

**Do not show**

- Any learner's conversation or score, even mocked up.
- The Coach writing a memo or giving the answer.
- Any competitor name.

**Files provided and files missing**

- Photos of Brightmere staff: [photo permission needed].

**Dates**

| Step | Date | Who |
|---|---|---|
| First version | [date needed] | Designer |
| Final files | [organizer's deadline needed] | Designer |
| Approval | [date needed] | The marketer, same day |

Designer's time: [designer's estimate needed], against about four days a month.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: CogniATion has a booth, because goals-and-kpis.md lists only the dates.
- Unknown: what is the organizer's file deadline?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a design brief". Read the context pack.
Task: <what is needed, where it will appear, sizes, file formats and due date>.
<paste the copy here>
```


===== FILE: skills/budget-and-planning/allocate-budget.md =====

---
title: Allocate the budget
type: skill
function: budget-and-planning
owner: head-of-marketing
reads: [goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools, icp-and-segments, personas]
you_bring: What the budget covers, your committed costs, and last period's results by channel if you have any.
also_for: [budget was cut, where to spend, marketing budget split, channel mix, no past results, how much for ads, new budget, pipeline per dollar]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Splits program budget across channels with a stated rationale and cut order.
judge: ["Is the largest line there because it earned it or because it was largest last quarter?", "Is any line too small to produce a result you could even measure?", "What would you need to see in 60 days to move money between lines?", "Which line are you funding only because stopping it would be awkward?"]
show_to: whoever approves spend, often the CEO, and ask which line they would question first
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this to split a period's program budget across channels, with a reason for each line and a cut order. Do not use it to choose goals and bets; use [Write a quarterly plan](write-quarterly-plan.md) first. For the plan inside one paid channel, use [Plan a paid campaign](../paid-demand-gen/plan-paid-campaign.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: budget, goals, dated commitments, other priorities.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: paid spend, limits, hard rules, approval threshold.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority order, which decides ties.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: where buyers get information.

The marketer pastes:

- What the budget covers, such as salaries, tools and freelancers.
- Committed costs: contracts, deposits and quotes.
- Last period's results by channel: spend, leads and pipeline, if available.
- The quarterly plan, if one exists.

## Procedure

1. Set the total. Take the budget from goals-and-kpis.md or the prompt. If it is blank, ask for it and stop. If the marketer has not said what the budget covers, assume program spend only, and record that.
2. Place committed lines first. A line is committed if a contract or deposit exists, or if it is existing paid spend carried at its current rate for the period. Mark committed spend that could still be stopped as "Stoppable".
3. Remove anything barred. Apply the limits and hard rules in channels-and-tools.md. Any ad line needing tracking that is not in place gets the note "no spend until tracking is live", and its money stays in the reserve.
4. Set aside a reserve of 10% of the total. This and the 10% and 40% caps in step 6 are starting rules, not benchmarks.
5. Rate the remaining candidate lines. "Dated" means a goal or event in goals-and-kpis.md depends on it. "Proven" means pasted results show pipeline from it. "Untested" means neither.
6. Fund in this order: Dated lines, then Proven lines from highest pipeline per dollar, then Untested lines, capped together at 10% of the total. Cap any line at 40% of the total. Flag a signed contract above that. Break ties toward the higher segment in the priority order. Do not use outside benchmarks to rank channels.

   No history: if no results were pasted and over half the total is still unfunded after Committed and Dated lines, drop the 10% cap. Fund channels reaching where personas.md says buyers get information, most-named first. Rate them Hypothesis: a bet to test, not evidence.

7. Price each line. Use a quote or supplied cost if any. If not, give a holding amount, label it an estimate and show how you reached it, or write [cost needed].
8. Check the arithmetic. Lines plus reserve must equal the total exactly, and shares must add up to 100%. If an amount is left, add it to the reserve. If you are over, cut in the cut order.
9. Write the cut order. Cut the reserve first, then Untested and Hypothesis lines, then Proven lines, then Stoppable lines, then Dated lines, then Committed lines. Within a group, cut the lowest pipeline per dollar first, or the smallest line if there are no results. Say what each cut gives up, and show what happens at 10% and 25% less budget.
10. Flag approvals: the approver and lead time for each line above the threshold in channels-and-tools.md.
11. Write the decision check from the office manual for the largest line you could still move.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: total matches the budget, hard rules respected, every dated commitment funded or flagged as unfunded.
13. List everything you assumed or estimated in the last section, including every holding amount.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Period: <period>
Total: <amount> | Covers: <what the budget includes, up to 20 words>

## Allocation
| Line | Amount | Share | Rating | Rationale | Cut rank |
<one row per line, up to 10 rows, reserve included, then a total row; rating is Committed, Stoppable, Dated, Proven, Untested, Hypothesis or Reserve; rationale up to 20 words; cut rank 1 is cut first>

## Cut plan
- At 10% less (<amount>): <what is cut and what it gives up, up to 30 words>
- At 25% less (<amount>): <what is cut and what it gives up, up to 30 words>

## Approvals needed
| Line | Approver | Lead time |
<one row per line above the threshold, or "None">

## Not funded
- <item and reason, up to 20 words each, or "None">

## Decision check
<five lines, as the office manual sets out>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do the lines and the reserve add up to the exact total?
- [ ] Does every line have a rating and a rationale tied to a goal, pasted results or, for Hypothesis, personas.md?
- [ ] Is every line at or under 40% of the total, or flagged?
- [ ] Is every dated commitment funded, or flagged as unfunded?
- [ ] Does the cut plan add up at both 10% and 25% less?
- [ ] Is each line above the approval threshold flagged with its approver?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Period:** Q1 2027

**Total:** $70,000 | **Covers:** program spend, see assumptions

**Allocation**

| Line | Amount | Share | Rating | Rationale | Cut rank |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Search ads | $15,000 | 21% | Stoppable | $5,000 a month | 3 |
| Professional social network ads | $12,000 | 17% | Stoppable | $4,000 a month | 2 |
| Professional services campaign | $14,000 | 20% | Dated | Goal due February 15 | 4 |
| Regional conference, March 9 to 11 | $18,000 | 26% | Dated | Named priority | 5 |
| Freelance designer | $4,000 | 6% | Committed | Existing design support | 6 |
| Reserve | $7,000 | 10% | Reserve | Starting rule | 1 |
| Total | $70,000 | 100% | | | |

**Cut plan**

- At 10% less ($7,000): the reserve goes.
- At 25% less ($17,500): the reserve and $10,500 of the $12,000 in social ads.

**Approvals needed:** All lines over $5,000: Marta Ilves, CEO, 3 days.

**Not funded:** Display retargeting, barred by limits.

**Decision check (campaign line):** Constraint: too few relevant buyers. Why this: a dated goal depends on it. Evidence: [past results needed]. Test: minimum campaign first. Displaces: social ads.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the budget excludes salaries and tools, includes the designer, and tracking is live on both ad channels.
- Estimate: campaign, conference and designer are holding amounts, splitting the $36,000 left after ads and reserve.
- Unknown: what is the conference quote, and the designer's rate?
- Unknown: can you paste pipeline by channel?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Allocate the budget". Read the context pack.
Task: <the period and what the budget covers>.
<paste committed costs and results by channel here>
```


===== FILE: skills/budget-and-planning/propose-quarterly-goals.md =====

---
title: Propose quarterly goals
type: skill
function: budget-and-planning
owner: head-of-marketing
reads: [goals-and-kpis, company, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: Whatever numbers you have from last quarter, such as pipeline, leads, deals and a revenue goal, or none at all.
also_for: [no goals, no quarterly goal, ceo wants goals, set a target, how much pipeline to commit to, goal setting, talk to the ceo about goals, no numbers yet, get marketing set up]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Turns whatever numbers you have into hold, grow and stretch goal options and a script for the CEO conversation, or into a learning goal if you have no numbers.
judge: ["If the CEO picks grow what stops being done to make room for it?", "Which lever in these options can marketing move alone and which one depends on sales?", "Is the step from hold to grow something you can explain or just a round number?", "Does any option connect to a revenue number the CEO already cares about?"]
show_to: your sales lead, before the CEO sees it, and ask which option they would put their own name on
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use. Here the CEO decides which goal to commit to.

## When to use

Use this when goals-and-kpis.md has no marketing goals, whether you have all of last quarter's numbers or none. Do not use it to plan the work once goals exist; use [Write a quarterly plan](write-quarterly-plan.md) instead. For the conversation itself, see [Three hard situations](../../guides/hard-situations.md#you-have-no-goals-yet).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: any goals, definitions and baselines already filled in.
- [[context-pack:company]]: who the CEO is, the size of the marketing and sales teams, and which numbers are internal only.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: current channels, spend and limits.

The marketer pastes into the chat whatever exists. Blanks are fine.

- Last quarter's pipeline, leads, MQLs, SQLs and deals won.
- Any revenue goal someone has stated, and who stated it.
- Budget and days available next quarter, if known.

## Procedure

1. Collect the numbers from goals-and-kpis.md and the paste. Never fill a blank with an outside rate, a benchmark or a guess. If marketing goals are already filled in, ask whether to replace them.
2. Choose the route. With any number for last quarter, take the options route, steps 3 to 7. With no numbers at all, go to step 8.
3. Build the chain from what exists: MQLs x MQL to SQL rate = SQLs, then SQLs x value per SQL = pipeline, then SQLs x SQL to won rate = deals. Work out each rate from the marketer's numbers and label it an estimate. Write [number needed] for a missing link, and build only on what holds.
4. Write three options. Hold repeats last quarter. Grow moves one lever. Stretch moves that lever and a second one. Size a move from the marketer's best past quarter, or else use 15% (a starting rule, to show the arithmetic, not a forecast).
5. For each option, show:
   - The arithmetic, line by line.
   - What it would take: extra volume per week, the channel it comes from, and the money and days, or [budget needed] and [days needed].
   - What would have to be true: up to 3 conditions a named person could confirm.
6. If there is a revenue goal, work backward using only the marketer's rates: revenue goal divided by deal value is deals, divided by SQL to won rate is SQLs, divided by MQL to SQL rate is MQLs. Say which option it matches, or how far above stretch it sits, which the CEO needs to hear first.
7. Write the CEO script: a two-sentence opening, 3 to 5 questions, and the decision to request: which option, by what date, and what gets dropped. Do not choose for the CEO. Once decided, the marketer copies the goal into goals-and-kpis.md and runs the quarterly plan.
8. With no numbers, write no targets. Instead write:
   - Up to 8 questions to get the numbers, each with who to ask and where the answer lives.
   - A first-quarter learning goal: by a date, know the baselines for leads, qualified opportunities and pipeline, using definitions sales agrees. It holds no target number.
   - A short CEO script asking for that goal and saying when real options will follow.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: numbers match, internal-only numbers are marked, and a blank definition reads [definition needed].
10. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Decision owner: <the CEO's name>. These are options, not goals, until the CEO picks one.
Route: <Options, or Learning goal>

## Numbers used
<each number and its source, or [number needed]>

## Options
| Option | Goal as worded | Arithmetic | What it would take | What would have to be true |
<Hold, Grow and Stretch, or "Not enough numbers yet">
Revenue goal check: <what it requires and which option it matches, or "None stated">

## If you have no numbers
| Question | Who to ask | Where it lives |
<up to 8 rows, or "Not needed">
Learning goal: <what you will know by when, with no target number, or "Not needed">

## CEO conversation
Opening: <two sentences>
Questions:
- <3 to 5 questions>
Decision to request: <one sentence, with a date>
Next step: <once the CEO decides>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every number trace to goals-and-kpis.md or the paste, with nothing from outside?
- [ ] Do the three options differ by named levers, with each move labeled?
- [ ] Is the script asking the CEO to decide, with no option chosen for them?
- [ ] With no numbers, is the output free of any target number?
- [ ] Are derived numbers labeled estimates, and internal-only numbers marked?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Decision owner:** Marta Ilves, CEO. These are options, not goals, until she picks one.

**Route:** Options

Assumption: this example treats goals-and-kpis.md as blank, with only the Q3 2026 baselines known.

**Numbers used:** 260 MQLs, 26% MQL to SQL, 24% SQL to won, $900,000 pipeline, all Q3 2026. Estimate: 260 x 26% = about 68 SQLs, and $900,000 / 68 = about $13,200 per SQL.

**Options**

| Option | Goal | Arithmetic | What it would take | What would have to be true |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hold | $900,000 pipeline | 68 x $13,200 | Same channels and spend | Q3 was not a one-off |
| Grow | About $1.03 million | 300 x 26% = 78 SQLs x $13,200 | 3 more MQLs a week, from a named channel | One sales development representative can work 23 MQLs a week |
| Stretch | About $1.19 million | 300 x 30% = 90 SQLs x $13,200 | Grow, plus fewer rejected leads | Tom Ferrant agrees the rejections can be fixed this quarter |

**CEO conversation**

Opening: "Here are three options from our Q3 numbers. They are arithmetic, not forecasts, and I need you to choose one."

Questions:

- What revenue does the company need next year, and how much of it should marketing source?
- Would you rather beat hold or miss stretch?
- What would you drop to make room for grow?

Decision to request: which option we commit to, by the end of this week.

Next step: copy the choice into goals-and-kpis.md, then plan.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: 300 MQLs and 30% are Q3 plus the 15% starting rule, rounded, and are illustrative.
- Estimate: at 24%, about 16, 19 and 22 deals.
- Unknown: what would grow cost?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Propose quarterly goals". Read the context pack.
Task: <next quarter, and who decides>.
<paste whatever numbers you have, or say you have none>
```


===== FILE: skills/budget-and-planning/set-weekly-priorities.md =====

---
title: Set weekly priorities
type: skill
function: budget-and-planning
owner: head-of-marketing
reads: [goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools, company]
you_bring: Last week's numbers, what is due in the next two weeks, what slipped, and the days you have this week, counted as in the Weekly playbook.
also_for: [what should i do this week, too much to do, weekly plan, what to drop, overloaded, pipeline behind, monday planning, to do list]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Top three priorities and a do-not-do list from last week's numbers and plan.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill at the start of each week to pick up to three priorities and a do-not-do list, from last week's numbers and the plan. Do not use it to set the quarter's direction; use [Write a quarterly plan](write-quarterly-plan.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: marketing goals, KPI baselines, which KPIs are reviewed weekly, and other priorities.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: limits, hard rules, approvers and approval times.
- [[context-pack:company]]: the size of the team.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- Last week's numbers and the total to date for each weekly KPI.
- The quarterly plan, or the items due in the next two weeks.
- What slipped last week, and any new requests.
- Days available this week, as the office manual counts capacity.

## Procedure

1. Check the inputs. If no numbers were pasted, write [number needed] in each row, base the priorities on dates alone and ask in the last section. If periods or definitions do not match, say what to fix.
2. Work out each KPI's target pace: goal minus total to date, divided by weeks left. With no goal, derive it if a goal works back to the KPI, such as MQLs from an SQL goal and the conversion rate. Otherwise write "No target". Baseline pace is the baseline divided by the weeks in its period. Show the math and label each result as an estimate.
3. Rate last week's number against each pace separately. Against target: "Ahead" is 100% or more, "Close" is 80% to 99%, "Behind" is under 80%, or "No target". Against baseline: "Above", "Level" or "Below" on the same cutoffs. These cutoffs and the 14-day window in step 5 are starting rules, not benchmarks.
4. Do not call a trend from one week. A KPI that is Behind for one week gets a check, such as "find out why". Behind for two weeks or more gets a fix.
5. List candidates: plan items due within 14 days, items whose approval lead time means they must start this week, the fix or check for each Behind KPI, slipped items and new requests.
6. Rank the candidates. Hard deadlines within 14 days come first, counting approval time from channels-and-tools.md. Next come fixes for a Behind KPI tied to a marketing goal. Next come items slipped twice, which must either be done this week or dropped. New requests that serve no goal do not qualify.
7. Pick up to three priorities. For each one, write what done looks like by Friday, the owner and the goal served. Use only the marketer or people named in the context pack as owners.
8. Run the capacity check. Estimate days for each priority. If days available are unknown, write "Not assessed". If the total exceeds days available, drop the third priority, then the second. Never add a fourth.
9. Write the do-not-do list: 3 to 5 things the marketer might be tempted to do, each with the reason and when to look at it again. Include candidates that lost out in step 6. Add anything barred by limits or hard rules only if someone asked for it or the plan implies it.
10. Write up to 2 decisions for the marketer, each with your recommendation.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: goals and definitions match goals-and-kpis.md, approval times are respected, no hard rule is broken.
12. List everything you assumed or estimated in the last section, including each pace calculation.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Week of: <date>

## Priorities
| # | Priority | Done by Friday means | Owner | Goal served | Days |
<1 to 3 rows; priority up to 12 words; done means up to 20 words>

## Do not do this week
- <item, reason, when to revisit, up to 25 words each, 3 to 5 items>

## Numbers against pace
| KPI | Last week | To date | Target pace | Against target | Baseline pace | Against baseline |
<one row per weekly KPI>

## Capacity
Days needed: <number> | Days available: <number> | Result: <fits, Not assessed, or what was dropped, up to 20 words>

## Decisions for the marketer
- <decision and recommendation, up to 30 words each, up to 2 items, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Are there three priorities or fewer, each with a test for done by Friday?
- [ ] Does each priority serve a goal in the context pack?
- [ ] Do approval lead times still allow each deadline?
- [ ] Do the priorities fit the days available?
- [ ] Is each item on the do-not-do list something you would really be tempted to do?
- [ ] Is every pace calculation shown and labeled as an estimate?
- [ ] Is every trend backed by two or more weeks of numbers?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Week of:** January 25, 2027

**Priorities**

| # | Priority | Done by Friday means | Owner | Goal served | Days |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Finish professional services campaign page and emails | Drafts ready for the February 15 launch | The marketer | Campaign by February 15 | 2.5 |
| 2 | Find out why pipeline is behind, with [Diagnose a pipeline gap](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/diagnose-pipeline-gap.md) | Search ads export reviewed, likely cause named | The marketer | $1.2 million pipeline | 1 |
| 3 | Send conference budget for approval | Request with Marta Ilves, CEO, who needs 3 days | The marketer | Pipeline, SQLs | 0.5 |

**Do not do this week**

- Outbound to software companies: inbound only. Revisit next quarter.
- A third blog post this month: the campaign comes first. Revisit in February.
- Manager view in campaign copy: internal only. Revisit once cleared.

**Numbers against pace**

| KPI | Last week | To date | Target pace | Against target | Baseline pace | Against baseline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New qualified pipeline | $70,000 | $270,000 | $93,000 | Behind | $69,000 | Above |
| MQLs | 24 | 72 | About 27 | Close | 20 | Above |
| Demo requests | 4 | 16 | No target | No target | About 4.5 | Level |

**Capacity**

Days needed 4, available 4: fits.

**Decisions for the marketer:** None.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: numbers, days and the 10 weeks left are invented, and the conference is over the $5,000 approval line.
- Estimate: pipeline target pace is ($1.2 million minus $270,000) over 10 weeks. MQL target pace is (90 SQLs / 26% minus 72) over 10 weeks. Baseline paces are Q3 over 13 weeks.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Set weekly priorities". Read the context pack.
Task: <the week's start date and the days you have>.
<paste last week's numbers, what is due, what slipped and new requests here>
```


===== FILE: skills/budget-and-planning/write-quarterly-plan.md =====

---
title: Write a quarterly plan
type: skill
function: budget-and-planning
owner: head-of-marketing
reads: [goals-and-kpis, company, icp-and-segments, channels-and-tools, product]
you_bring: Last quarter's results, the days you can spend on this plan, and any hard dates not in the context pack.
also_for: [quarterly plan, marketing plan, 90-day plan, stop doing, what to stop, next quarter, what to focus on, plan for the ceo, goals for the quarter, hit the pipeline goal, one person team]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Goals, bets, calendar, owners and a capacity check for one quarter.
judge: ["If you could only finish one bet this quarter which would it be and is it ranked first?", "Which bet would you cut if the CEO added a project next month?", "Did you reason the expected effect from your numbers or is it what you hope for?", "Would these bets close the gap even if they all went well?"]
show_to: your CEO, before you start, and ask which bet they would cut first
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill once a quarter to turn goals into a small set of bets, with dates, owners and a check that one marketer can actually do them. Do not use it to split money across channels; use [Allocate the budget](allocate-budget.md) instead. For the week ahead, use [Set weekly priorities](set-weekly-priorities.md). With no goals yet, run [Propose quarterly goals](propose-quarterly-goals.md) first.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: quarter, goals, budget, definitions, baselines, priorities.
- [[context-pack:company]]: the size of the marketing team and who can be named.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority order.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, limits, approvers and approval times.
- [[context-pack:product]]: limits, and what may be said about the roadmap.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- Last quarter's results against goals, if available.
- Days in the quarter after time off and fixed meetings.
- Hard dates and commitments not in the context pack.
- Everything now running.

## Procedure

1. Copy the quarter, company goal and marketing goals word for word from goals-and-kpis.md. If the marketing goals are blank, stop and point to [Propose quarterly goals](propose-quarterly-goals.md).
2. Work out each gap. Show goal, baseline and difference. Where goals chain, work backward and show the math, such as SQL goal divided by MQL to SQL rate. Label each result as an estimate. If a baseline is blank, write [baseline needed] and mark the gap unknown.
