3. Write 1 to 5 bets. A bet is one coordinated effort with one goal, one segment and one window. Any goal with a date in goals-and-kpis.md becomes a bet automatically. Every goal needs at least one bet, and any idea or running work that serves no goal goes to "Not doing".
4. Rank the bets. Dated goals come first, then bets for the top segment in the priority order, then the rest. State each bet's expected effect as an estimate, with its reasoning in a few words, worked from your baselines. Write unknown only if no baseline helps.
5. Build the calendar. Place hard dates first, then count back from each one, using the approval times in channels-and-tools.md. Add the first draft date for each item needing approval.
6. Assign one owner to each bet and each calendar item. Use only people named in the context pack, or "the marketer". Mark anyone outside marketing "needs to agree".
7. Run the capacity check, as the office manual counts it. Estimate days per bet from its pieces, add rows for routine and for the interruption block, and compare the total with the marketer's days. Keep freelance days separate. If effort or days are unknown, write Not assessed. If demand exceeds supply, shrink or cut the lowest ranked bet until it fits, and record the cut under "Not doing".
8. Check the money. If the estimated cost of the bets exceeds the budget, say by how much and cut the lowest ranked bet.
9. Write the decision check from the office manual for the top bet.
10. Write up to 3 risks, each with its signal and response.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: goals word for word, limits and hard rules respected, nothing planned that the product cannot do, internal-only items marked.
12. List everything you assumed or estimated in the last section, including every day count.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Quarter: <quarter>
Company goal: <word for word, one line>

## Goals and gaps
| Goal | Baseline | Gap | Math |
<one row per marketing goal, plus any derived number labeled Estimate>

## Bets
| Rank | Bet | Goal served | Segment | Window | Owner | Expected effect |
<1 to 5 rows; bet up to 12 words; effect is an estimate with its reasoning>

## Calendar
| Date | Item | Bet | Owner | Approver and lead time |
<sorted by date, up to 15 rows>

## Capacity check
| Work | Marketer days and reasoning | Freelance days |
<one row per bet, routine, interruption block, total, available>
Result: <fits, Not assessed, or what was cut>

## Not doing
- <item and reason, up to 20 words each, 2 to 6 items>

## Decision check
<five lines for the top bet, as the office manual sets out>

## Risks
- <risk, signal, response, up to 30 words each, up to 3 items>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Are the goals copied word for word, with no new goals added?
- [ ] Does every goal have a bet, and does every bet serve a goal?
- [ ] Does each calendar date allow for the approval time of its approver?
- [ ] Does each item have one owner named in the context pack, or the marketer?
- [ ] Does the capacity check fit, after cuts, with routine and interruptions counted?
- [ ] Are all derived numbers labeled as estimates, with the math shown?
- [ ] Are internal-only items marked?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Quarter:** Q1 2027

**Company goal:** Reach $7 million in annual recurring revenue by the end of 2027

**Goals and gaps**

| Goal | Baseline | Gap | Math |
|---|---|---|---|
| Create $1.2 million in new qualified pipeline by March 31, 2027 | $900,000 | $300,000 | Q3 2026 baseline |
| Produce 90 sales-qualified leads by March 31, 2027 | About 68 | About 22 | Estimate: 260 MQLs x 26% |

**Bets**

| Rank | Bet | Goal served | Segment | Window | Owner | Expected effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Professional services campaign | Campaign | Professional services | February 15 | Marketer | Estimate: 40 of its 116 MQLs are new |
| 2 | Regional conference | SQLs | Professional services | March 9–11 | Marketer | Estimate: 30 MQLs, 10 demo requests a day, 3 days |
| 3 | Manager view release (internal only) | Pipeline | All | February | Marketer | Estimate: 15 MQLs, helps calls more than leads |

**Calendar:** January 29 first drafts, February 15 live, March 9–11 conference.

**Capacity check (estimates):** Campaign 20 days (5 assets x 4). Conference 10 (3 on site). Release 15. Routine 13 (1 day a week). Interruptions 3 (2 hours a week). Total 61, 60 available (13 weeks, 5 off). Result: bet 3 shrinks by 1.

**Not doing:** outbound for software companies.

**Decision check (bet 1):** Constraint: too few relevant buyers. Why this: nothing reaches this segment. Evidence: [needed]. Test: minimum campaign first. Displaces: release scope.

**Risks:** Campaign slips. Signal: no draft by January 29. Response: drop bet 3.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Estimate: 90 SQLs / 26% = 346 MQLs, 86 above 260. The bets total 85, no margin.
- Assumption: effects and day counts are illustrative.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a quarterly plan". Read the context pack.
Task: <the quarter, the days you have, and any hard dates>.
<paste last quarter's results here>
```


===== FILE: skills/campaign-design/design-nurture-sequence.md =====

---
title: Design a nurture sequence
type: skill
function: campaign-design
owner: demand-generation-manager
reads: [icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: Who enters and from which source, what counts as success, and which resources already exist.
also_for: [drip campaign, email sequence, leads not ready, cold leads, lead nurturing, warm up leads, automated emails, old demo requests]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Designs a multi-email nurture with entry and exit rules, one purpose per email and a copy outline for each.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for a series of emails to leads who are not ready to talk to sales. Do not use it for one standalone email or newsletter, which belongs in [Write a marketing email](../content/write-marketing-email.md). For the campaign that feeds the sequence, use [Write a campaign brief](write-campaign-brief.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment, disqualifiers, triggers, sales cycle length.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who reads the emails, their objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: proof per email and its permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: tone and rules for the copy.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions, which set the exit rules.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: email list size, tools, approvers.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: who enters and from which source, what action counts as success, and which resources already exist.

## Procedure

1. Write the entry rule as a yes/no test on data the marketer's tools can see: source, segment, and action taken. If a lead already meets the MQL definition or has hit a trigger, keep them out and send them to sales.
2. Write the exit rules: met the MQL definition, requested a demo, accepted by sales, unsubscribed, matched a disqualifier, or finished the sequence. Name what happens next at each exit.
3. Set the length from the sales cycle in icp-and-segments.md: 3 emails if under 30 days, 5 if 30 to 90 days, 7 if longer. If the cycle is blank, use 5, label it an assumption and ask in the last section. These lengths and the spacing in step 4 are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks.
4. Set the spacing: 3 days between the first two emails, then 7 days, unless the marketer's list rules differ. Count each send day from entry.
5. Choose one persona as the reader: the marketer's choice, or the first in personas.md (a starting rule). Skip any role personas.md says does not research software.
6. Give each email one job, in this order: name the trigger or problem, answer the top objection, show proof, answer the next objection, ask for the next step. If personas.md lists only one objection, use a pillar for the second. For 3 emails, keep the first, third and last jobs. For 7, add a use-case email and a how-it-works email before the last.
7. For each email, name one objection or pillar, one proof item with status, and one call to action. Use only Public proof, since email is not one-to-one sales material. If no proof fits, write [proof needed]. If a resource is not in the marketer's list, write [resource needed].
8. Write an outline for each email, not finished copy, following brand-voice.md.
9. Name the measures: share of leads reaching the success exit, unsubscribes per email, clicks per email. Leave targets as [target needed] unless the marketer gave them.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: disqualifiers, words to avoid, proof status, roadmap, voice rules. Fix what contradicts it.
11. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels.

## Output format

Repeat the outline block for every email.

```
Status: Draft

## Sequence summary
Name: <short name>
Reader: <one persona>
Success: <one action>

## Entry and exit rules
Entry: <yes/no test, up to 30 words>
Keep out: <who never enters>
Exit: <event> – <what happens next>

## Sequence map
| Email | Send day | Job | Objection or pillar | Proof and status | Call to action |

## Copy outlines
Email <number>
Subject: <up to 60 characters>
Preview: <up to 90 characters>
Points: <up to 3, each up to 20 words>
Call to action: <up to 8 words>

## Measures
| Measure | Target | Where the marketer reads it |

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Can the entry rule be answered yes or no from data the marketer has?
- [ ] Does every exit say what happens to the lead next?
- [ ] Is a lead who meets the MQL definition removed and sent to sales?
- [ ] Does each email have exactly one job and one call to action?
- [ ] Is every proof item Public in proof-points.md?
- [ ] Do the subject lines follow brand-voice.md and avoid the listed words?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Sequence summary.** Name: Client memo nurture. Reader: head of learning and development. Success: demo request.

**Entry and exit rules.** Entry: professional services lead, fits the ICP, downloaded one resource, not yet an MQL. Keep out: fewer than 100 learners, wants a course library, wants scores on individuals for performance reviews. Exit: second download, webinar or demo request – goes to sales as an MQL. Unsubscribed – removed. Finished – back to the monthly newsletter.

**Sequence map**

| Email | Day | Job | Objection or pillar | Proof and status | Call to action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0 | Problem | Finished, but no change shown | None | Read the guide |
| 2 | 3 | Objection | "People won't make time for another training tool." | 72% weekly session result, Public | Watch a scenario |
| 3 | 10 | Proof | It asks, it does not answer | Lena Marsh quote, Public | See how it works |
| 4 | 17 | Objection | "How would I show the leadership team that it worked?" | Brightmere Advisory 22% result, Public | See a team report |
| 5 | 24 | Next step | People keep practicing | 9-day median, Public | Request a demo |

**Copy outlines.** Email 2. Subject: Will your people make time for one more tool? Preview: What learners did in Q3 2026. Points: practice on real work problems; 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week in Q3 2026. Call to action: Watch a scenario.

**Measures.** Demo requests from the sequence: [target needed].

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the guide and a team-level sample report exist.
- Unknown: what target do you want for demo requests from this sequence?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Design a nurture sequence". Read the context pack.
Task: <who enters and from which source, what counts as success, and which resources exist>.
```


===== FILE: skills/campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md =====

---
title: Write a campaign brief
type: skill
function: campaign-design
owner: demand-generation-manager
reads: [icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The campaign goal, the time window and the budget.
also_for: [plan a campaign, need more leads, demand campaign, generate demand, campaign idea, pipeline campaign, campaign plan, more demo requests]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a single-goal campaign brief with audience, offer, channels, assets, timeline and targets.
judge: ["Why would this audience stop what they are doing for this offer?", "Is the offer something they want or something you want them to download?", "If this works what would a salesperson notice the following month?", "Could you run this same campaign for another segment by changing the name?"]
show_to: a salesperson who talks to this segment, and ask whether they would send this offer to a prospect
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before any multi-channel campaign with one goal, one audience and one time window. Do not use it to promote a product release, which belongs in [Plan a product launch](../go-to-market-and-launches/plan-product-launch.md). For the paid part in detail, use the brief as input to [Plan a paid campaign](../paid-demand-gen/plan-paid-campaign.md) instead of expanding it here.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, priority order, disqualifiers, triggers, sales cycle.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who the campaign speaks to, their objections, where they get information.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: proof available and its permission status.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, budget, baselines, metric definitions.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: owned and paid channels, limits, approvers, typical approval times.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the campaign goal, time window and budget. Optional: an offer idea, days available, and the result and lesson of the last similar campaign.

## Procedure

1. Fix one goal. If the marketer gives two, ask which wins. If they want a draft anyway, use the one named in goals-and-kpis.md and list the other as an open question.
2. Pick one segment and one primary persona. Use the marketer's choice. If none is given, take the top segment in the priority order and the persona that starts the purchase. Name one secondary persona at most.
3. Choose the offer by readiness: a useful resource for people who have not felt the problem yet, a demo or pilot for people who have hit a trigger from icp-and-segments.md. If the offer does not exist yet, add it to the assets table and label it as an assumption.
4. Choose one pillar from messaging.md as the lead message: the first with Public proof that fits the segment's reason to buy (a starting rule). Attach proof from proof-points.md with its status. Use only Public proof in anything the public sees. If a proof item appears in messaging.md but not in proof-points.md, do not treat it as cleared, and ask about it in the last section.
5. Choose channels from channels-and-tools.md only, unless the marketer adds one. Rank them by where the persona gets information. Give each one job: reach, conversion or follow-up. Drop any channel that breaks a limit in that file, and say why.
6. List the assets each channel needs and the skill that produces each one, or [skill title needed] if it is not in your files. If capacity was given and the list exceeds it, cut the lowest-ranked channel first.
7. Set targets using the metric definitions in goals-and-kpis.md. Work each one back from the quarter's goal and show the arithmetic as an estimate. With no share given, use the segment's share of customers (a starting rule). If no baseline exists, write [baseline needed]. Give each target a read date: the date promised, or the window's end plus that stage's timing in goals-and-kpis.md.
8. Build the timeline backward from the start date. Subtract each approver's typical time from channels-and-tools.md, then the production time. If production time is unknown, write [production time needed].
9. If the budget is blank, write [budget needed]. Flag any line that crosses an approval limit in channels-and-tools.md.
10. Write the decision check from the office manual. State which lesson this brief applies.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: segment, words to avoid, proof status, internal-only numbers, channel limits, approvers. Fix what contradicts it.
12. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Campaign summary
Name: <short name>
Goal: <one goal, with number and date>
Window: <start date to end date>
Lesson applied: <from which campaign, or "None yet">

## Audience
Segment: <one>
Primary persona: <role, and the trigger to reach them at>
Secondary persona: <role or "None">
Excluded: <disqualifiers>

## Offer
<What they get and what they do, up to 40 words>

## Message
Lead pillar: <one>
Line for the primary persona: <up to 20 words>
Proof: <item, permission status>

## Channels
| Channel | Job | Why this one (up to 15 words) |

## Assets
| Asset | Skill that produces it | Owner | Due |

## Timeline
| Date | Milestone | Approver, if any |

## Targets
| Metric | Baseline | Target | Read on | How worked out |

## Budget
<Total, by line, and any approval needed>

## Decision check
<five lines, as the office manual sets out>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the campaign have exactly one goal, one segment and one time window?
- [ ] Is every target defined the way goals-and-kpis.md defines it, with the arithmetic shown?
- [ ] Does each proof item used in public carry Public status in proof-points.md?
- [ ] Does each channel appear in channels-and-tools.md and respect its limits?
- [ ] Does each asset name a skill or a placeholder, an owner and a due date?
- [ ] Do the dates leave room for each approver's typical time?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Campaign summary.** Name: Defend the memo. Goal: 30 SQLs by March 31, 2027. Window: February 15 to March 31, 2027. Lesson applied: None yet.

**Audience.** Segment: professional services firms. Primary persona: head of learning and development, after a company-wide AI assistant rollout or a client complaint about poor reasoning. Secondary persona: managing partner. Excluded: fewer than 100 learners, wants a course library.

**Offer.** A guide to checking the reasoning in client memos, then a demo of CogniATion Coach.

**Message.** Lead pillar: you can see reasoning improve. Line: show that training changed how people reason. Proof: Brightmere Advisory's 22% result on client memo reasoning, Public.

| Channel | Job | Why this one |
|---|---|---|
| Professional social network ads | Reach | Finds heads of learning by job title |
| Search ads | Conversion | Reaches firms already looking |
| Email list | Follow-up | 5,400 contacts to start from |

**Assets.** Guide, then landing page: [Write a web page](../content/write-web-page.md), marketer, February 8.

**Timeline.** February 15: campaign live, the date in goals-and-kpis.md.

**Targets.** SQLs: baseline [needed]. Target 30, a third of the 90 goal, read March 31. MQLs: baseline 260 for all campaigns. Target 116, read March 31.

**Budget.** [budget needed]. Any line over $5,000 goes to Marta Ilves, 3 days.

**Decision check.** Constraint: too few relevant buyers. Why this: no campaign reaches this segment yet. Evidence: [needed]; under 58 MQLs at mid-window would disprove it. Test: page and email first. Displaces: a third blog post.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the marketer gave this campaign a third of the SQL goal.
- Estimate: 116 MQLs is 30 SQLs divided by the 26% MQL to SQL rate, rounded up.
- Unknown: how much of the $70,000 quarter budget goes to this campaign?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a campaign brief". Read the context pack.
Task: <the goal, the time window, the budget, and any offer idea>.
```


===== FILE: skills/campaign-design/write-lead-follow-up.md =====

---
title: Write a lead follow-up
type: skill
function: campaign-design
owner: demand-generation-manager
reads: [icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, product, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The leads from your form, download or demo request, or the source you want rules for, and how sales routes them.
also_for: [demo request follow up, follow up on leads, inbound leads, contact form leads, download leads, leads going cold, how fast to call leads, what to send new leads]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Sorts leads from a form, a download or a demo request into tiers and writes the timing, the messages and the handoff to sales.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when leads arrive from a form, a download or a demo request, not from an event. Do not use it for an event or webinar list; use [Follow up after an event](../events-and-webinars/follow-up-after-event.md) instead. It stops after three messages, so for a longer series use [Design a nurture sequence](design-nurture-sequence.md). For the permanent rules with sales, use [Define the lead handoff](../marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: fit, disqualifiers, triggers, segment priority.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who is writing in.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what can be promised, and any pilot.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for all messages.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies the source (which form, resource or demo page) and how leads reach sellers. To sort actual leads, paste a list with only: name, company, title, email, ICP fields, what the person did and when, and consent status.

## Procedure

1. If no source is named, ask. With a list, count rows, merge duplicates and list missing fields. With no list, write the rules and messages, with [n] and [lead] as placeholders. Mark fit you cannot confirm as Fit unknown.
2. Remove, and say why: existing customers (send to their account owner), competitors, vendors, students, anyone outside the ICP region, anyone matching a disqualifier. A lead whose consent is no or missing gets no marketing email, and the marketer decides.
3. Assign one tier to each remaining lead. A Tier B or C lead who replies with an ask moves to Tier A.
   - Tier A: asked for a demo, pricing or a call. Personal messages from sales, on business days 1, 3 and 7.
   - Tier B: fits the ICP, filled in a form or downloaded, with no ask. Marketing emails on business days 2 and 7.
   - Tier C: Fit unknown or partial fit, with no ask. One resource email on business day 5. No sales contact.
   These windows and message counts are starting rules the marketer can change.
4. Set the stop rules. A reply or an opt-out stops all messages. After the last message, a lead that fits the ICP goes to [Design a nurture sequence](design-nurture-sequence.md), and any other lead closes.
5. Mark MQL status separately from tier, using the exact definition in goals-and-kpis.md. Never mark anyone SQL, since sales decides that. If the definition is blank, write [MQL definition needed].
6. Draft each message: subject of 50 characters or fewer, body of 90 words or fewer, one ask. Refer only to what the person did, using [resource name] or [personal detail] for the rest. Take product statements from product.md. Use Public proof only, except that a Tier A message from one seller to one person may use Sales only proof. Never use internal numbers.
7. Write the sales handoff for Tier A, one row per lead, with [seller needed] if routing is missing. Rank by segment priority, then a named trigger, then date. If Tier A may exceed what sales can take that week, ask the marketer.
8. Set a check with sales 14 days after the first handoff (a starting rule). If more than a third of leads are rejected (a starting rule), recommend [Define the lead handoff](../marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md).
9. Check the draft against the context pack: every claim, permission, word to avoid, internal number, roadmap item and hard rule. Add approval time from channels-and-tools.md before the first send.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Source: <form, download or demo request, by name>
Rows in: <n>. Duplicates: <n>. Removed: <n, with reasons>.

## Lead tiers
| Tier | Count | MQLs | Sender | After the last message |

## Timing
| Tier | Message | Business day | Date | Approver |

## Messages
Tier A, message 1
Subject: <50 characters at most>
Body: <90 words at most, one ask>
<same fields for each message in the timing table>

## Sales handoff
| Rank | Lead, title and company | Action and date | Fit and trigger | MQL | Seller | Due |

## Check with sales
Date: <date, or "14 days after first handoff">
Read: <accepted, rejected with reason, not contacted>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do tier counts add up to rows in, minus duplicates and removals?
- [ ] Is MQL status marked by your definition, with nobody marked SQL?
- [ ] Does each message refer only to what the person did, with one ask?
- [ ] Do leads without consent stay off marketing email?
- [ ] Does every message use only proof with the right permission and no internal number?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Source:** Website demo page and guide downloads, week of January 11, 2027

Rows in: 14. Duplicates: 1. Removed: 2 (one customer, one under 100 learners).

**Lead tiers**

| Tier | Count | MQLs | Sender | After the last message |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 5 | 4 | [seller needed] | Nurture if fit |
| B | 4 | 1 | Marketer | Nurture |
| C | 2 | 0 | Marketer | Closes |

**Timing** (Tier A shown): message 1 by January 12, message 2 by January 14, message 3 by January 20. Approver: Tom Ferrant.

**Messages** (Tier A, message 1 shown)

Subject: Your demo request for CogniATion Coach

Body: [First name], you asked for a demo of CogniATion Coach. It questions employees about a work problem instead of answering it, then scores their reasoning. Which work problem would you want to try it on? [Seller name] can show you on a call this week.

**Sales handoff**

| Rank | Lead, title and company | Action and date | Fit and trigger | MQL | Seller | Due |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [lead name], head of learning, [accounting firm] | Demo request, Jan 11 | 150 people to train, AI assistant rollout | Yes | [seller needed] | Jan 12 |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all counts, dates and the sample lead are invented.
- Assumption: Tier A text counts as sales material, approved in advance.
- Inference: one Tier A lead is Fit unknown, so it is not yet an MQL.
- Unknown: how are leads routed across the three account executives?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a lead follow-up". Read the context pack.
Task: sort the leads pasted below from <form, download or demo request> and write the tiers, timing, messages and sales handoff.
```


===== FILE: skills/competitive-intelligence/analyze-win-loss.md =====

---
title: Analyze wins and losses
type: skill
function: competitive-intelligence
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [competitors, icp-and-segments, product, messaging, goals-and-kpis]
you_bring: A CRM export of closed deals with one row per deal, plus any call notes marked with the deal ID.
also_for: [why we lose deals, why we win, lost deals, sales says we lose on price, losing to a competitor, closed lost reasons, deal review, win rate dropped]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Finds patterns across won and lost deals from a pasted CRM export and call notes, and tests the context pack against them.
judge: ["Are these reasons what buyers said or what the salesperson typed into the CRM afterward?", "Would you hear the same reason if you called three of these buyers yourself?", "Is the pattern about your product or about deals you should never have been in?", "Which loss reason could marketing change and which belongs to product or price?"]
show_to: your sales lead, and ask which pattern they disagree with and why
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this at the quarterly review, or after a run of unexpected losses, when you can paste a CRM export of closed deals. Do not use it for interviews or surveys with no deal outcomes; use [Synthesize customer research](../segmentation-icp-and-personas/synthesize-customer-research.md) instead. For one competitor's facts, use [Write a competitor profile](write-competitor-profile.md).

## Inputs

From the context pack:

- [[context-pack:competitors]] – where each wins and loses, the status quo
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]] – fit criteria, disqualifiers, segments
- [[context-pack:product]] – limits and weak spots, to sort product gaps
- [[context-pack:messaging]] – the pillars to test
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]] – the SQL definition and the SQL to won baseline

The marketer supplies in the prompt: a CRM export pasted as text, with one row per deal, and call notes marked with the deal ID. The export needs deal ID, outcome and close date. Segment, competitor, stated reason and amount make the analysis better. Remove contact names first if your company's rules require it.

## Procedure

1. Check the export. If deal ID, outcome or close date is missing, ask for it and stop. List any other missing column as an unknown and skip the cuts that need it. Never fill in a value.
2. Clean the data: remove duplicates and open deals, and keep only deals that meet the SQL definition in goals-and-kpis.md. If the export cannot show that, keep all closed deals and record it as an assumption.
3. Sort every deal into Won, Lost to a named competitor, or No decision. A deal lost to the buyer's current approach counts as No decision, with the status quo noted.
4. Code each lost and no-decision deal with one reason: Product gap, Price, Implementation effort, Competitor strength, Timing, Status quo, Not ICP or Unknown. Code a blank or vague reason as Unknown.
5. Read the call notes. Where they contradict the CRM reason, use the reason in the notes and count the disagreement in the data summary.
6. Mark each deal as ICP fit or not, using the criteria and disqualifiers in icp-and-segments.md. If the data cannot show it, mark it Unknown.
7. Build the cuts, in this order, with what the data allows: by segment, by competitor, by reason, by ICP fit, by deal size. Report counts. Show a percentage only for a group of 20 or more closed deals.
8. Write patterns. A pattern has at least 3 deals, with a call note on at least one. Anything less is an observation. The 3-deal and 20-deal cutoffs are starting rules the marketer can change. State each as "N of M deals", and name the deal IDs. Say "because" only if a buyer said so. Otherwise label it an inference.
9. Test the context pack: for each pillar and each competitor's "Where they win" and "Where they lose", write Holds, Challenged or No data, with the pattern or deals behind it.
10. Recommend up to 5 actions, based on patterns only. Each names the change, the agent role that owns it and the skill to run. Observations get one action: what data to collect next.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: competitor statements match competitors.md or a cited call note, and definitions match goals-and-kpis.md. Compare with the SQL to won baseline only if the denominators match. Mark deal names and quotes internal.
12. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not find in the last section, each with its label.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Bottom line
<60 words at most: what the deals say and the single most important action>

## Data summary
Period: <dates>. Closed deals: <n>. Won: <n>. Lost to a named competitor: <n>. No decision: <n>.
Reason unknown: <n>. CRM reason contradicted by notes: <n>. Not ICP: <n>.

## Cuts
| Cut | Won | Lost | No decision |
(one table, one row per group, percentages only for groups of 20 or more)

## Patterns
1. <Pattern, N of M deals, deal IDs, evidence. 40 words at most. 5 patterns at most>

## Observations
- <Fewer than 3 deals. 20 words at most each. 5 items at most, or None>

## Context pack test
| Item | Holds, Challenged or No data | Evidence |

## Recommended actions
1. <Change, owning agent role, skill to run. 25 words at most. 5 actions at most>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do the deal counts add up to the closed total?
- [ ] Are open deals out, and no-decision deals apart from losses?
- [ ] Are blank reasons coded Unknown, not guessed?
- [ ] Does every pattern have at least 3 deals and named deal IDs?
- [ ] Are percentages limited to groups of 20 or more?
- [ ] Is each causal claim backed by a buyer's words, or labeled an inference?
- [ ] Does each action trace to a pattern and name a skill?
- [ ] Are deal names and quotes marked internal?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Bottom line**

In this invented deal set, most losses went to Arguvia on price at software companies, or to Elenchly from buyers wanting a course library. Screen for that disqualifier earlier.

**Data summary**

Assumption: all counts are invented, because the context pack holds no CRM export.

Period: Q3 2026. Closed deals: 24. Won: 9. Lost to a named competitor: 10. No decision: 5. Reason unknown: 3. CRM reason contradicted by notes: 2. Not ICP: 3.

**Cuts**

| Cut | Won | Lost | No decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Professional services | 5 | 3 | 1 |
| Financial services | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| Software companies | 1 | 5 | 3 |

**Patterns**

1. 4 of 6 losses to Arguvia were software company deals citing price (D3, D7, D12, D19).
2. 3 of 4 losses to Elenchly wanted a course library, an ICP disqualifier (D5, D14, D21).

**Observations**

- 2 no-decision deals kept an annual workshop, saying earlier tools went unused.
- 1 financial services deal ended with no decision during its security review.

**Context pack test**

| Item | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Elenchly "Where they win" | Holds | Pattern 2 |
| Pillar 3 | No data | No deal mentions team reports |

**Recommended actions**

1. Screen for the course library disqualifier before SQL, marketing operations manager, Define the lead handoff.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all deals, IDs and counts above are invented.
- Inference: both patterns rest on that set and competitors.md.
- Unknown: Do call notes exist for the won deals?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Analyze wins and losses". Read the context pack.
Task: <the period the deals cover>.
<paste the CRM export and call notes here>
```


===== FILE: skills/competitive-intelligence/write-competitor-profile.md =====

---
title: Write a competitor profile
type: skill
function: competitive-intelligence
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [competitors, product, icp-and-segments, proof-points, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The competitor's name, today's date, and pasted source text with where it came from and the date checked.
also_for: [competitor research, competitor analysis, what does the rival do, rival pricing, new competitor, update competitor file, rival product changes]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Creates or updates a sourced profile of one competitor from what the marketer pastes.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this to create a profile of one competitor, or to update one at the monthly refresh, from sources you paste. Do not use it for a sales sheet; use [Write a battlecard](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-battlecard.md) instead. For patterns across many deals, use [Analyze wins and losses](analyze-win-loss.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:competitors]] – the current entry and its dated facts
- [[context-pack:product]] – features, what it is not, known weak spots
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]] – the target, to judge where the competitor matters
- [[context-pack:proof-points]] – proof for the answer, with permission status
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]] – hard rules on naming competitors

The marketer supplies: the competitor's name, today's date, and pasted source text such as pricing page copy, help center pages, product pages, review site excerpts or call notes. Each paste needs its source and the date it was checked. Work only from what is pasted.

## Procedure

1. Find the competitor in competitors.md. If it is there, the mode is Update. If not, the mode is New. If nothing is pasted and competitors.md has no entry, ask for sources and stop.
2. Give each pasted item an ID (E1, E2) and sort it by source type: Own for the competitor's published pages, Buyer for call notes and lost-deal notes, Third party for review sites, articles and anything else.
3. Use nothing from memory, even if you recognize the name. An item with no source or no date goes under "Unverified" and into the unknowns.
4. Write each fact in one sentence that shows where it came from: "lists", "per its help center", "three prospects reported". Write an absence as "not found in [source]", never as "does not have". Report a buyer's statement as a report, with the count of buyers.
5. Mark each fact in Update mode: New, Confirmed by a new source, Changed, Contradicted, or Unchanged if no new source touches it. Never overwrite silently. A newer Own source replaces an older Own source. A Buyer or Third party item does not replace an Own source: keep both, with dates, and mark them Contradicted.
6. Mark any fact checked more than 6 months before today's date as Stale. Six months is a starting rule the marketer can change. If the marketer gave no date, skip Stale marks and ask in the last section.
7. Write "Where they win" and "Where they lose", at most 3 lines each, with the IDs or the existing entry behind each line. A line with one source is an inference, and gets that label. Keep existing lines with no new evidence as they are.
8. Fill in the side-by-side table, 6 rows at most, using capabilities buyers in the ICP ask about. "Us" comes only from product.md, including "Known weak spots" and "What it is not". Set "Stronger" to Them, Us or Cannot tell. Use Cannot tell when the two sides measure different things. Show every row where they are stronger.
9. Write "Our answer": one sentence on when the competitor is a fair choice, one factual sentence on the difference, and one question the buyer can ask them. Use proof only if its status fits one-to-one sales use. No criticism beyond the known facts.
10. Write "Use limits": the profile is internal by default. Copy any hard rule on competitor names from channels-and-tools.md, and name the approver for sales material.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: every fact has a source and date, "Us" matches product.md, no Pending or None proof appears, and no roadmap item is used. Fix what fails.
12. List what to check next, by source, and list everything you assumed, inferred or could not find in the last section.

## Output format

Field names through "Our answer" match competitors.md, so the marketer can paste them in by hand.

```
Status: Draft
Mode: <New or Update>

## Name and category
<Name: how buyers see them, 12 words at most>

## Where they win
- <3 lines at most, 20 words each, with evidence IDs>

## Where they lose
- <3 lines at most, 20 words each, with evidence IDs>

## Known facts, with source and date
- <Fact> (<source>, checked <date>) <New, Confirmed, Changed, Contradicted, Unchanged or Stale>
(10 items at most)

## Our answer
<3 sentences at most>

## Unverified
- <Item, and what source would settle it; write None if empty>

## Side by side
| Capability | Them | Us | Stronger |
(6 rows at most, cells 12 words at most)

## Use limits
<30 words at most>

## Check next
- <Source to check, and why; 3 items at most>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every known fact have a source and a checked date?
- [ ] Is anything from memory, with no pasted source?
- [ ] Are absences written as "not found", not as facts?
- [ ] Are buyer reports labeled as reports, with a count?
- [ ] Does the table show where the competitor is stronger?
- [ ] Do "Us" cells match the product file, weak spots included?
- [ ] Were changes marked, with no fact silently overwritten?
- [ ] Is the use limit stated?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Mode: Update

**Name and category**

Elenchly: established course library on critical thinking and decision making.

**Where they win**

- Large companies wanting one library through their learning management system (existing entry).

**Where they lose**

- Buyers who want to see behavior change, not course completions (existing entry).

**Known facts, with source and date**

- Three prospects described its reports as completion data and quiz scores (calls, 2026). Unchanged.
- More than 200 video courses listed on its product pages (checked October 2026). Unchanged.

**Our answer**

A fair choice if you want a library. CogniATion Coach is practice that reports progress by team. Ask how the last course changed the work.

**Unverified**

None.

**Side by side**

| Capability | Them | Us | Stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| Course library | More than 200 video courses, per product pages | Not a course library | Them |
| Reports for learning leaders | Completion data and quiz scores, reported by three prospects | Team-level progress on six thinking skills | Cannot tell, different measures |

**Use limits**

Internal. No competitor names in ads. Tom Ferrant approves sales material.

**Check next**

- Its product pages, for changes to its reports.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: no new source pasted, so all facts come from competitors.md.
- Unknown: Do Elenchly's reports show how people work after a course?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a competitor profile". Read the context pack.
Task: <the competitor's name and today's date>.
<paste each source here, with its origin and date checked>
```


===== FILE: skills/content/plan-content-calendar.md =====

---
title: Plan a content calendar
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, messaging, proof-points, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The period, any campaign briefs, items already scheduled, dates to avoid, hours per item and the hours you have for content.
also_for: [content plan, editorial calendar, what to publish, blog schedule, social schedule, posting schedule, content ideas, content for demand]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a dated content calendar for a set period, with every item tied to a goal or campaign and checked against capacity and approval times.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this to turn goals and campaigns into a dated list of what gets published, where and by whom, for a month or a quarter. Do not use it to choose the quarter's goals and bets; use [Write a quarterly plan](../budget-and-planning/write-quarterly-plan.md) instead. For the audience, offer and channels of one campaign, use [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: marketing goals, deadlines and other priorities with dates.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, publishing cadence, approvers and their typical times.
- [[context-pack:company]]: size of the marketing team and any freelance time.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority order.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid, for working titles.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: which customers and numbers may appear in public.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what may and may not be said about the roadmap.

The marketer supplies the period, any campaign briefs, items already scheduled, dates to avoid and, if known, hours per item and hours available, as the office manual counts capacity.

## Procedure

1. Set the period from the request. If none is given, ask. Treat Monday to Friday as working days unless told otherwise.
2. List the fixed dates: deadlines from goals-and-kpis.md, dated priorities, and anything the marketer supplied. Give each goal in the period a one-letter tag (A, B).
3. Count the slots. For each channel in channels-and-tools.md, multiply its stated cadence by the length of the period, rounding up. That count is a ceiling, not a quota. Do not add slots beyond the cadence unless the marketer asks. If a channel has no stated cadence, write [cadence needed], leave that channel out of the calendar, except for fixed-date items, and ask in the last section.
4. Fill the slots in this order. First, items tied to a fixed date, published at least two working days before a hard deadline. Second, items supporting a campaign in the four weeks before its date. Third, remaining slots that can serve a goal, rotated through the segments in priority order, with the top segment getting the most. Leave the rest empty. The two-day and four-week windows are starting rules the marketer can change.
5. Give every item a goal tag. An item that serves no goal goes under "Cut or held", not on the calendar.
6. Name the skill that produces each item. If no skill fits, write "no skill".
7. Set the draft due date: publish date, minus the approver's typical time, minus one working day. A same-day approver counts as zero. Item types with no listed approver get [approver needed], and the draft due date is worked out as if the approver were same-day.
8. Move to "Cut or held" any item that depends on a roadmap item not cleared in product.md, an internal-only fact, or a proof point with status pending, sales only, internal only or none. Give held items no public date.
9. Run the capacity check. Count items per week and items needing design work. Count an item already in a campaign or launch plan once. If the marketer gave hours per item and hours available, show both as Estimates. If not, write [hours per item needed] or [hours available needed], give the verdict "Not assessed" and do not guess. If the load exceeds the hours, remove items from the bottom of the order in step 4 and list them under "Cut or held".
10. Check the draft against the context pack: every date, cadence, approver and title. Remove words to avoid, and fix anything that contradicts a file.
11. List everything assumed, including publishing days you chose, under "Assumptions and open questions". If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer copies the calendar into their own planning tool. This skill schedules and publishes nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Period: <start date to end date>
Goals: <1 to 4 goals, each with a tag, copied from goals-and-kpis.md>
Fixed dates: <dated items, up to 8, or "None">

## Calendar
| Date | Channel | Working title | Goal | Segment | Skill | Approver, draft due |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
<one row per item, in date order; working title up to 10 words>

## Capacity check
Items per week: <count by week>
Marketer: <hours needed and available with method, or the placeholder>
Design: <items needing design against available days>
Verdict: <fits, tight, over or Not assessed, in one sentence>

## Cut or held
<each item with its reason in up to 15 words, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every item have a date, a channel, a goal tag and a skill?
- [ ] Does each channel stay within the cadence in channels-and-tools.md?
- [ ] Are hard deadlines met at least two working days early?
- [ ] Is every draft due date worked out from the approver's typical time?
- [ ] Are roadmap items and unapproved proof points off the calendar?
- [ ] Is the verdict Not assessed whenever hours are unknown, with none made up?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Period:** February 8 to 15, 2027 (one week shown, condensed)

**Goals:** A: publish the campaign for professional services firms by February 15, 2027

**Fixed dates:** February 15, campaign deadline

**Calendar**

| Date | Channel | Working title | Goal | Segment | Skill | Approver, draft due |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Feb 8 | Social | Did your last course change how anyone works? | A | Professional services | write-social-posts | Marketer, Feb 5 |
| Feb 9 | Blog | Three questions that show whether training changed the work | A | Professional services | write-blog-post | Marketer, Feb 8 |
| Feb 10 | Social | What to ask about one client memo | A | Professional services | write-social-posts | Marketer, Feb 9 |
| Feb 11 | Web | Professional services landing page update | A | Professional services | write-web-page | Marketer, Feb 8 |
| Feb 12 | Email | February newsletter: defending the reasoning in client memos | A | Professional services | write-marketing-email | Marketer, Feb 11 |
| Feb 12 | Social | The professional services page is live | A | Professional services | write-social-posts | Marketer, Feb 11 |

**Capacity check**

Items per week: six. Marketer: [hours per item needed]. Design: one page against about four designer days a month. Verdict: Not assessed until the hours are known.

**Cut or held**

CogniATion Coach manager view release: internal only, no public date.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: social posts go out Monday, Wednesday and Friday.
- Assumption: the landing page is a fixed-date item, on top of the blog cadence.
- Unknown: how many hours does each item take?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan a content calendar". Read the context pack.
Task: <the period, the goals or campaigns to cover, and anything already scheduled>.
```


===== FILE: skills/content/write-blog-post.md =====

---
title: Write a blog post
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: An angle or SEO page brief, plus notes or sources for anything about practice, standards or regulation.
also_for: [blog article, thought leadership, website traffic, article idea, content for seo, write a post, organic traffic, blog draft]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces one blog post from an angle or SEO page brief, built on material the marketer supplied and proof cleared for public use.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for one post, from an angle you already have or a brief from [Write an SEO page brief](../seo/write-seo-page-brief.md). Do not use it for a page that asks the reader to act on an offer; use [Write a web page](write-web-page.md) instead. For a customer story, use [Write a case study](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-case-study.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:personas]]: the reader's daily problem, objections and language.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, messages by persona, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does, what it is not, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes with permission status.
- [[context-pack:company]]: which company numbers may be published.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: why buyers stay with what they have today.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, point of view, rules.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves blog posts and pricing mentions.

The marketer supplies the angle or SEO page brief, plus notes or pasted sources for anything about practice, standards or regulation.

## Procedure

1. Find the source. If the marketer pasted an SEO page brief, take its topic, search intent, primary keyword and headings as given. If they gave an angle, use it. If they gave only a topic, write three one-sentence angles, choose the first that names a daily problem from personas.md (a starting rule), and record the choice as an assumption.
2. Fix one reader, the persona whose daily problem the angle names, or the first listed on a tie (a starting rule), and one takeaway: a single sentence saying what the reader can do after reading, even if they never buy.
3. Sort the material into three piles: facts from the context pack, what the marketer supplied, and what is missing. Claims about practice, standards or regulation come only from the marketer's notes or pasted sources. For each gap, write [source needed: what is missing] in the post and do not fill it from memory.
4. Write the outline: title, opening, three to five sections, closing action. Each section heading says what the reader finds out. Use time order for a sequence, otherwise put the most useful section first.
5. Draft to 600 to 1,000 words, unless the SEO page brief sets another length. Name the problem in the first two sentences, with no background first. End each section with something the reader can do or check.
6. Keep the product in its place: at most two mentions in the body, plus the closing action. Each mention describes something in product.md.
7. Add proof only where it backs a claim. Use a customer name, number or quote only if proof-points.md marks it public, and a company number only if company.md marks it publishable. State the sample and date. If a claim in messaging.md has no entry in proof-points.md, leave it out and ask.
8. Do not name competitors. Write about the alternative in general terms. For a named comparison, use [Write a web page](write-web-page.md) with the comparison page type.
9. If a primary keyword was supplied, put it in the title, the first 100 words and one section heading. State no search volumes unless supplied. With no keyword, write "none supplied".
10. Check the draft against the context pack, line by line: facts, permissions, what the product is not, words to avoid, voice rules. Add any pricing mention to Approvals needed, with the listed approver.
11. List everything assumed under "Assumptions and open questions", with each [source needed] item as an Unknown. If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer places the post in their own CMS. This skill publishes nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Reader: <one persona, up to 8 words>
Angle: <one sentence, up to 25 words>
Takeaway: <what the reader can do, up to 20 words>
Primary keyword: <from the brief, or "none supplied">
Title: <up to 65 characters, sentence case>
Meta description: <up to 155 characters>

## Post
<opening, up to 60 words>

### <section heading, up to 10 words>
<up to 200 words, ending in something to do or check>

<3 to 5 sections in total; the length in step 5 governs if the field limits add up to more>

### <closing heading, up to 10 words>
<up to 60 words, with one action and [link needed] unless a link was supplied>

## Proof and sources used
| Item | From | Permission status or date |
|---|---|---|
<one row per item, or "None">

## Approvals needed
<approver and typical time from channels-and-tools.md>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Could a reader act on the takeaway without buying the product?
- [ ] Does each section end in something to do or check?
- [ ] Is every claim about practice, standards or regulation sourced or shown as a placeholder?
- [ ] Is the product mentioned at most twice in the body, within its limits in product.md?
- [ ] Does every customer, number and quote have public or publishable status?
- [ ] Are competitors unnamed and the words to avoid absent?
- [ ] If a keyword was supplied, does it appear in the title, the first 100 words and one heading?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Reader:** Head of learning, professional services firm

**Angle:** Three questions show whether training changed how people reason, before the leadership team asks.

**Takeaway:** Ask the three questions about one client memo this week.

**Primary keyword:** none supplied

**Title:** Three questions that show whether training changed the work

**Meta description:** Test one client memo with three questions to see whether reasoning changed.

**Post (condensed)**

Everyone finished the course. Can you show that it changed how they work? Test one client memo this week.

**What does it assume?** Ask the author to name the assumptions behind the recommendation. Note any they can't name.

**What evidence backs it?** Ask where each key claim came from, and check one. [source needed: how firms review client memos]

**What else was considered?** Ask for one alternative and why it lost. CogniATion Coach asks for all three in practice sessions of about 15 minutes, and declines to give the answer.

**Where to start.** "My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory. Request a demo: [link needed]

**Proof and sources used:** Marsh quote, proof-points.md, public, approved June 2026.

**Approvals needed:** The marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the post targets professional services firms.
- Unknown: what do firms check in a client memo, and where is the source?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a blog post". Read the context pack.
Task: <the angle, if there is no SEO page brief>.
<paste the brief, notes and sources here>
```


===== FILE: skills/content/write-marketing-email.md =====

---
title: Write a marketing email
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The purpose, who receives it, the send date, the action and the link.
also_for: [newsletter, email blast, webinar invite, announcement email, email to customers, email to the list, low open rates, demo invite]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces one standalone marketing email or newsletter issue, with subject lines, body, one main action and send notes.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for one email: an announcement, an invitation or a newsletter issue. Do not use it for a series of emails; use [Design a nurture sequence](../campaign-design/design-nurture-sequence.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: the email list, its cadence, approvers and hard rules.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the reader's daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: messages by persona, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does, prices, what it is not, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes with permission status.
- [[context-pack:company]]: public name, publishable numbers.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, point of view, rules.

The marketer supplies the purpose, the list or part of the list receiving it, the send date, the action and link, and the items for a newsletter issue. Past results, if any, come as pasted numbers.

## Procedure

1. Set the email type: standalone or newsletter. If the request needs more than one email in a planned order, stop and point the marketer to [Design a nurture sequence](../campaign-design/design-nurture-sequence.md).
2. Fix the audience, one reader from personas.md and one main action. If the marketer does not say which part of the list receives it, ask. Do not assume the list can be split by segment or persona.
3. Write three subject lines of up to 50 characters, one of each kind: a question, a plain statement of fact, and a line naming the task, decision or document. Recommend the one closest to the reader's daily problem, or the question on a tie (a starting rule). Use no exclamation points and no all caps, and give no deadline or scarcity unless the marketer supplied it.
4. Write a preheader of up to 90 characters that adds something the subject line does not say.
5. Write the body. For a standalone email, up to 150 words: open on the reader's problem in the first sentence, give what you know that helps, then the action, in the point of view set in brand-voice.md. For a newsletter, up to 250 words: up to three items, each a heading and up to 60 words, with the first item carrying the main action and the others carrying a plain link.
6. Use a customer name, number or quote only if proof-points.md marks it public, and a company number only if company.md marks it publishable. State the sample and date. If a claim in messaging.md has no entry in proof-points.md, leave it out and ask. Do not name competitors.
7. Write links as the marketer's URL, or [link needed]. Add [tracking parameters needed] unless the marketer supplied them, following the skill [Set tracking conventions](../marketing-ops/set-tracking-conventions.md) once it has been run.
8. Write the footer fields as placeholders unless the marketer supplied them: [postal address needed], [unsubscribe link needed]. Do not give legal advice. Add an open question asking the marketer to confirm the email meets the rules that apply to their list.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: facts, permissions, what the product is not, words to avoid, voice rules. Add any pricing mention to Approvals needed with the listed approver.
10. List everything assumed under "Assumptions and open questions". If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer loads the email into their own email tool and sends a test first. This skill sends nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Email type: <standalone or newsletter>
Audience: <list or part of list, up to 15 words>
Reader: <one persona, up to 8 words>
Main action: <up to 8 words>
Send date: <supplied date, or [send date needed]>

## Subject lines
Question: <up to 50 characters>
Fact: <up to 50 characters>
Task, decision or document: <up to 50 characters>
Recommended: <which one, and why in up to 15 words>
Preheader: <up to 90 characters>
From name: <from the context pack or the marketer>

## Body
<standalone: up to 150 words. Newsletter: up to 250 words, up to 3 items>
Button or link text: <up to 5 words>
Link: <URL with tracking, or [link needed] [tracking parameters needed]>

## Footer
<[postal address needed] [unsubscribe link needed], or supplied values>

## Proof used
<each item and its permission status, or "None">

## Approvals needed
<approver and typical time from channels-and-tools.md>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is this one email, with one main action?
- [ ] Are the subject lines within 50 characters, with no invented urgency?
- [ ] Does the body open on the reader's problem and stay inside its word limit?
- [ ] Does every customer, number and quote have public or publishable status, with sample and date?
- [ ] Are competitors unnamed, and are the words to avoid and exclamation points absent?
- [ ] Are the link, tracking and footer fields filled or shown as placeholders?
- [ ] Has the marketer sent a test to themselves before it goes out?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Email type:** Standalone. **Audience:** [part of list needed]. **Reader:** Head of learning. **Main action:** Request a demo. **Send date:** [send date needed]

**Subject lines**

Question: Did your last course change how anyone works?

Fact: Practice reasoning in about 15 minutes a session

Task, decision or document: Test the reasoning in one client memo

Recommended: Question, names the proof the reader is asked for.

Preheader: Completion data won't show it. Team-level progress on reasoning can.

From name: CogniATion

**Body**

When leadership asks whether your last course changed how people work, what do you show them?

CogniATion Coach is a chatbot that questions employees about a work problem instead of answering it. Sessions take about 15 minutes. The Coach scores reasoning against a rubric, and you see progress by team, never an individual's conversation.

People keep practicing. Across all customers in Q3 2026, 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week.

"My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory.

Button: Request a demo. Link: [link needed] [tracking parameters needed]

**Footer:** [postal address needed] [unsubscribe link needed]

**Proof used:** 72% figure and Marsh quote, public.

**Approvals needed:** The marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: CogniATion is the from name.
- Unknown: which part of the 5,400-contact list receives this?
- Unknown: does this meet the rules that apply to your list?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a marketing email". Read the context pack.
Task: <the purpose, who receives it, the send date, the action and the link>.
```


===== FILE: skills/content/write-social-posts.md =====

---
title: Write social posts
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: One source idea or pasted text, the link to use, and each channel's character limit.
also_for: [social media posts, professional network post, promote a blog post, post ideas, company page posts, repurpose content, organic social]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a set of social posts from one source idea, by channel, each with a different angle and one action.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this to turn one source idea, such as a blog post, a page, a result or a note, into a set of organic posts. Do not use it for paid copy; use [Write ad variations](../paid-demand-gen/write-ad-variations.md) instead. For deciding what to post across a month, use [Plan a content calendar](plan-content-calendar.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: which social accounts exist, their cadence, approvers and hard rules.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the reader's daily problem, objections and where they get information.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: messages by persona, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does, what it is not, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes with permission status.
- [[context-pack:company]]: which company numbers may be published.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, rules and visual basics.

The marketer supplies the source idea or pasted source text, the link to use, the number of posts if not the default, and the character limit for each channel.

## Procedure

1. Reduce the source to one sentence, the source idea. If the marketer supplied no source, ask. Do not choose a topic on your own. The reader is the persona whose daily problem is closest to it, or the first listed on a tie (a starting rule).
2. Pick the channels. Write only for the accounts listed in channels-and-tools.md, unless the marketer names another. If that field is blank, ask.
3. Set the count: three posts per channel by default, or the marketer's number, up to six.
4. Set the limit for each channel. Use the character limit the marketer supplied. If none was supplied, keep the post to the template's word limits and record the missing limit as an Unknown. Do not state a channel's limits from memory.
5. Give each post a different angle, in this order. Post 1, problem: a daily problem from personas.md. Post 2, how-to: one action the reader can take today without buying. Post 3, proof: one public proof point. If no public proof point fits, use Post 3 for an objection answer instead and say so. Posts 4 to 6, in order: objection answer, one product fact, one question drawn from the source.
6. Write each post. The first line stands alone and makes sense without the rest. The body carries one claim. The post has one action. Use the marketer's link, or write [link needed]. Add no hashtags, tags or mentions unless brand-voice.md or the marketer asks for them.
7. Adapt rather than copy. If two channels are in the set, rewrite each post for that channel's limit and reader, and do not paste the same text twice.
8. Use a customer name, number or quote only if proof-points.md marks it public, and a company number only if company.md marks it publishable. Include the sample and date with each number. If a claim in messaging.md has no entry in proof-points.md, leave it out and ask. Do not name competitors. Tag a customer only if the marketer confirms that the customer agreed.
9. Write a visual note for each post, following the visual basics in brand-voice.md. If no real image exists, write [image needed] or "no image".
10. Check every post against the context pack: facts, permissions, what the product is not, words to avoid, voice rules. Add any pricing mention to Approvals needed with the listed approver.
11. List everything assumed under "Assumptions and open questions". If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer publishes the posts themselves. This skill schedules and posts nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Source idea: <one sentence, up to 25 words>
Reader: <one persona, up to 8 words>
Link: <supplied link, or [link needed]>

## <Channel name>
Limit: <characters supplied by the marketer, or "not supplied">

### Post 1: <angle>
First line: <up to 15 words>
Body: <up to 60 words>
Action: <up to 10 words>
Visual note: <up to 15 words>
Proof used: <item and permission status, or "None">

### Post 2: <angle>
<same five fields>

### Post 3: <angle>
<same five fields>

<repeat the section for each further channel>

## Approvals needed
<approver and typical time from channels-and-tools.md>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do all posts come from the one source idea?
- [ ] Does each post use a different angle, in the set order?
- [ ] Does each first line make sense on its own?
- [ ] Does each post carry one claim and one action?
- [ ] Is the how-to post useful to someone who never buys?
- [ ] Does every customer, number and quote have public or publishable status, with sample and date?
- [ ] Are competitors unnamed, and are the words to avoid and exclamation points absent?
- [ ] Does each post fit the limit the marketer supplied?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Source idea:** Show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it.

**Reader:** Head of learning and development

**Link:** [link needed]

**Professional social network**

Limit: not supplied

**Post 1: problem**

First line: Everyone finished the course. Can you show it changed how they work?

Body: CogniATion Coach questions your people on work problems and shows you progress by team. You never see an individual's conversation.

Action: See how it works.

Visual note: Team-level report screenshot with no individual names, or [image needed].

Proof used: None

**Post 2: how-to**

First line: Try this with one client memo this week.

Body: Pick one recommendation. Ask the author for the assumption behind it, the evidence for it and one alternative they rejected. Note what they can't answer.

Action: Read the full checklist.

Visual note: No image.

Proof used: None

**Post 3: proof**

First line: "My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own."

Body: Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory. Brightmere's analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners.

Action: Request a demo.

Visual note: [image needed]

Proof used: Marsh quote and 22% result, public.

**Approvals needed:** The marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: a blog post with a checklist exists for Post 2 to link to.
- Unknown: what is the character limit for this channel?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write social posts". Read the context pack.
Task: <the source idea or pasted source, the link, and each channel's character limit>.
```


===== FILE: skills/content/write-video-script.md =====

---
title: Write a video script
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The topic, the video type and length, the footage you have, and who will speak.
also_for: [product explainer, demo video, customer clip, short video, event promo, how-to clip, what to say on camera]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a script for one short video, up to 120 seconds, with time, shot, spoken line and on-screen text in columns.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for one short video of up to 120 seconds: a how-to clip, a product explainer, a customer clip or an event promo. Do not use it for a live session; use [Plan a webinar](../events-and-webinars/plan-webinar.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:personas]]: the viewer's daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what can be shown, what it is not, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes with permission status.
- [[context-pack:company]]: leaders who can be named or quoted, publishable numbers.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, rules and visual basics.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: the video channel, approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies the topic, video type, length, available footage and who will speak.

## Procedure

1. Fix the video type, one viewer from personas.md, one goal and the length from the request. If the type or goal is missing, ask. If no length is given, use 30 seconds for a how-to clip or promo and 60 seconds for an explainer or customer clip. Past 120 seconds, ask the marketer to split it or cut it. These lengths are starting rules the marketer can change.
2. Set the spoken word budget: length in seconds times 2 to 2.5 words a second (typical range, estimate). Show the arithmetic.
3. Choose one point. If the source holds more, keep the one closest to the viewer's daily problem or objection, or the first on a tie (a starting rule), and list the rest as cut.
4. Write three to eight rows, in time order. The first row runs up to 5 seconds and states the problem or the job. The middle rows show it being done. The last row gives the one action.
5. Write each shot as something the marketer can film or record: a named scenario, a task, a screen recording of a named feature. Follow the visual basics in brand-voice.md. If the scenario is not known, write [scenario needed]. List footage that does not exist yet under Footage needed.
6. No row's spoken line may run above the word rate in step 2.
7. Write on-screen text so the point still lands with the sound off, with no claim the spoken line does not support.
8. Name speakers only if company.md lists them as people who can be named or quoted, or proof-points.md has a public quote from them. Write a named person's line as [approved line needed] unless it is an approved quote. Otherwise use "narrator".
9. Use a customer name, number or quote only if proof-points.md marks it public, and a company number only if company.md marks it publishable. If a claim in messaging.md has no entry in proof-points.md, leave it out and ask. Do not name competitors. Filming at a customer's site needs that customer's permission, so list it under Approvals needed.
10. Show only features that exist today in product.md. Check the script against the context pack: facts, permissions, words to avoid, voice rules. Count the spoken words and enter the count.
11. List everything assumed under "Assumptions and open questions". If a needed context pack field is blank, including the approver for video, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer films and edits from the script. This skill produces and publishes nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Title: <up to 60 characters, sentence case>
Video type: <how-to, explainer, customer clip or promo>
Channel: <from channels-and-tools.md>
Viewer: <one persona, up to 8 words>
Goal: <one sentence, up to 15 words>
Length: <seconds, up to 120>
Word budget: <Estimate: seconds times 2 to 2.5 words a second>
Spoken word count: <actual count>

## Script
| Time | Shot | Spoken line | On-screen text |
|---|---|---|---|
<3 to 8 rows; shot up to 15 words; on-screen text up to 7 words>
```
