```
## Production notes
Speakers: <narrator or named person, with basis>
Footage needed: <each item, or "None">
End card: <text, up to 10 words, and logo rule from brand-voice.md>
Proof used: <each item and its permission status, or "None">
Cut from this script: <other points left out, or "None">

## Approvals needed
<approver and typical time from channels-and-tools.md, or [approver needed]>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the video make one point to one viewer, with one action?
- [ ] Is the spoken word count within the word budget, with the arithmetic shown?
- [ ] Does the first row state the problem or job within 5 seconds?
- [ ] Can every shot be filmed or recorded, or is it listed under Footage needed?
- [ ] Are all speakers, customers, numbers and quotes cleared in the context pack?
- [ ] Do the visuals follow brand-voice.md, with no stock images it rules out?
- [ ] Does every feature shown exist today?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Title:** Answer "why?" before your manager asks

**Video type:** How-to. **Channel:** Video channel. **Viewer:** Analyst or new manager.

**Goal:** Show how to practice answering "why?" in a session

**Length:** 30 seconds. **Word budget:** 60 to 75. **Spoken word count:** 61.

**Script**

| Time | Shot | Spoken line | On-screen text |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0–5 | Printed draft with "why?" in the margin | Your draft came back with "why?" on it. Now what? | "Why?" on your draft |
| 5–11 | Scripted demo: [scenario needed] opens | Open a work scenario and state your recommendation, in your own words. | Start from a work scenario |
| 11–18 | Scripted demo: CogniATion Coach asks for an assumption | CogniATion Coach won't write it for you. It asks for your assumptions and evidence. | It asks, it does not answer |
| 18–24 | Scripted demo: the Coach asks for an alternative | Then it asks what else you considered, and why that lost. | What else did you consider? |
| 24–30 | Logo on plain background | The Coach takes about 15 minutes a session. Share this with your learning team. | CogniATion Coach |

**Production notes**

Speakers: narrator. Footage needed: printed draft, scripted demo recording. End card: cogniation.example, logo on plain background. Proof used: None. Cut from this script: team reports.

**Approvals needed:** The marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the demo is illustrative, with no real learner's conversation or score.
- Estimate: word budget is 30 seconds times 2 to 2.5 words a second.
- Inference: analysts do not research training software, so the clip asks them to share it (personas.md).
- Unknown: which scenario should the clip show?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a video script". Read the context pack.
Task: <the topic, video type, length, and the footage and speakers you have>.
```


===== FILE: skills/content/write-web-page.md =====

---
title: Write a web page
type: skill
function: content
owner: content-lead
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The page type, the reader, the one action the page asks for, and any SEO page brief.
also_for: [website copy, landing page, homepage, website rewrite, pricing page, website not converting, demo request page, product page]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces section-by-section copy for one web or landing page, with one reader, one action and every proof item checked for permission.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for copy on one web or landing page. Do not use it for an article; use [Write a blog post](write-blog-post.md) instead. For a handout that sales sends or leaves behind, use [Write a sales one-pager](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-sales-one-pager.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:messaging]]: positioning, pillars, messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:product]]: features, prices, what it is not, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the reader's daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: why each segment buys.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes with permission status.
- [[context-pack:company]]: publishable company numbers.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: dated facts, for comparison pages only.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, rules and visual basics.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies the page type, the reader, the one action the page asks for, and any SEO page brief.

## Procedure

1. Fix the page type, one reader and one action from the request. If the reader or action is missing, ask. If the marketer wants a draft anyway, write [reader needed] or [action needed].
2. Pick the sections by page type and omit the rest of the template. Landing or segment page: Hero, Problem, How it works, Proof, Objections, Final action. Product page: the same plus Features. Pricing page: Hero, Price, Objections, Final action. Comparison page: Hero, Comparison, Proof, Final action. Any other type: ask which of these is closest.
3. If an SEO page brief was pasted, take its topic, search intent and headings, and put the primary keyword in the SEO title and hero. State no search volumes unless supplied.
4. Write the headline first, from the pillar that best answers the reader's daily problem. If two fit, take the first in messaging.md, a starting rule. It says what happens to the reader, not how the company feels.
5. Write each section to do one job. Problem uses the reader's situation and the segment's reason to buy. How it works has exactly three steps from product.md. Objections answers up to three objections from personas.md, each with a fact from the context pack.
6. Use a customer name, number or quote only if proof-points.md marks it public, and a company number only if company.md marks it publishable. State the sample and date of each number. If a claim in messaging.md has no entry in proof-points.md, leave it out and ask.
7. Claim nothing listed under what the product is not, and mention no roadmap items unless product.md clears them.
8. Include prices only on pricing pages or when asked, using the exact price points, and add the pricing approver to Approvals needed.
9. Name competitors only on comparison pages, using only facts in competitors.md with their source and date.
10. Write the hero's visual note from the visual basics in brand-voice.md. If no real image exists, write [image needed].
11. Check the draft against the context pack, line by line: facts, permissions, words to avoid, voice rules. Fix anything that contradicts a file.
12. List everything assumed under "Assumptions and open questions". If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.

The marketer places the copy in their own CMS. This skill publishes nothing.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Page type: <landing, segment, product, pricing or comparison>
Reader: <segment and persona, up to 12 words>
Action: <the one action, up to 8 words>
SEO title: <up to 60 characters>
Meta description: <up to 155 characters>

## Hero
Headline: <up to 10 words, sentence case>
Subhead: <up to 25 words>
Button: <up to 4 words>
Visual note: <up to 20 words>

## Problem
<up to 60 words>

## How it works
<exactly 3 steps, each up to 20 words>

## Features
<product pages only: 3 to 6 items, up to 15 words each>

## Price
<pricing pages only: price points, what is included, up to 60 words>

## Comparison
<comparison pages only: up to 6 rows, each fact with source and date>

## Proof
<1 to 3 quotes or numbers, attributed, with sample and date>

## Objections
<up to 3: objection in the reader's words, answer up to 30 words>

## Final action
<one line, up to 15 words, and the hero's button>

## Proof used
<each item and its permission status>

## Approvals needed
<approver and typical time from channels-and-tools.md, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the page have one reader and one action, with the same button at top and bottom?
- [ ] Does every proof item show public or publishable status in Proof used?
- [ ] Does each number state its sample and date?
- [ ] Are competitors unnamed unless this is a comparison page?
- [ ] Is every claim inside the limits in product.md, with no roadmap items?
- [ ] Are the words to avoid absent and the brand voice rules followed?
- [ ] Is every pricing mention listed under Approvals needed?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Page type:** Landing. **Reader:** Professional services head of learning. **Action:** Request a demo.

**SEO title:** Critical thinking training for analysts | CogniATion

**Meta description:** CogniATion Coach questions your analysts on work problems and won't give the answer.

**Hero**

Headline: See reasoning improve, team by team

Subhead: CogniATion Coach is an AI chatbot that won't give your people the answer. They practice reasoning on work problems.

Button: Request a demo

Visual note: Team-level report screenshot, no individual names or scores.

**Problem**

Everyone finishes the course, and nobody can show it changed how they work. In your firm, their reasoning reaches clients.

**How it works**

1. Choose from 180 work scenarios, or build ones from your documents.
2. In about 15 minutes, the Coach asks for assumptions, evidence and alternatives, and won't give the answer.
3. The Coach scores reasoning against a rubric. You see progress by team, never an individual's conversation.

**Proof**

Brightmere Advisory's analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners.

"My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory.

**Objections**

"People won't make time for another training tool." 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week (all customers, Q3 2026).

**Final action**

Show that training changed how people reason. Button: Request a demo

**Proof used:** Brightmere result, Marsh quote, 72% figure, public.

**Approvals needed:** The marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: a demo request is the action.
- Unknown: which report screenshot can the hero use?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a web page". Read the context pack.
Task: <the page type, the reader, the one action, and any SEO page brief>.
```


===== FILE: skills/events-and-webinars/follow-up-after-event.md =====

---
title: Follow up after an event
type: skill
function: events-and-webinars
owner: events-and-webinar-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The lead list with consent status, what was promised on site, and how leads route to sellers.
also_for: [trade show leads, webinar leads, badge scans, event leads, conference follow up, leads after the event, no-shows, hand leads to sales]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Sorts event or webinar leads into tiers, drafts follow-up messages and writes the handoff to sales.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this right after a trade show, in-person event or webinar, once you have the lead list. Do not use it for a long series of emails; use [Design a nurture sequence](../campaign-design/design-nurture-sequence.md) instead. To set the permanent rules between marketing and sales, use [Define the lead handoff](../marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: the sales team.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what can be promised and what it is not.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: fit criteria, disqualifiers, triggers, segment priority.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for all messages.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions, and how pipeline is credited.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer pastes the lead list with only the fields this skill needs: name, company, title, email, ICP fields, what the person did (demo request, conversation, scan, attended, no-show), staff notes, tier if captured at the event, and consent status. Also the event name and dates, what was promised on site, and the routing of leads to sellers. Remove anything else before pasting.

## Procedure

1. Stop and ask if no lead list is pasted. Count rows, merge duplicates and list missing fields. Do not guess fit from the company name: mark it Fit unknown.
2. Remove from follow-up, and say why: existing customers (send to their account owner), competitors, vendors, students, anyone outside the ICP region, and anyone matching a disqualifier. A lead whose consent status is no or missing gets no marketing email. Flag those for the marketer to decide.
3. Assign one tier to each remaining lead, keeping any event tier that the data supports:
   - Tier A: fits the ICP, and asked for a demo, pricing or a call, or named a trigger from icp-and-segments.md. A personal message from sales, within 1 business day.
   - Tier B: fits the ICP, had a conversation or attended, with no ask. A marketing email within 2 business days, then nurture.
   - Tier C: Fit unknown, scan only, or no-show. A resource email within 5 business days, then nurture. No sales contact.
   These windows are starting rules the marketer can change.
4. Mark MQL status separately from tier, using the exact definition in goals-and-kpis.md. Never mark anyone SQL, since sales decides that. If the definition is blank, write [MQL definition needed].
5. Draft one message per tier, plus a no-show version for webinars: subject of 50 characters or fewer, body of 120 words or fewer, one ask. Refer only to conversations in the notes, and mark where the Tier A sender adds a detail with [personal detail from notes]. Use proof marked public, or one-to-one proof only in Tier A messages that one seller sends to one person.
6. Write the sales handoff for Tier A, one row per lead. Use [seller needed] if routing is missing. Rank by segment priority, then by whether a trigger was named. If Tier A may exceed what sales can take that week, ask the marketer, and say which leads to contact first.
7. Build the calendar with real dates: Tier A, Tier B, Tier C, then a check with sales 14 days after the event on accepted and rejected leads. Add approval time before sending from channels-and-tools.md, and route sales messages to the approver listed there.
8. Set the report: leads, tiers, MQLs, SQLs, pipeline credited under the attribution model in goals-and-kpis.md. Compare with event targets if the marketer pastes the plan. Leave anything not yet known as [figure needed], and pass results to [Evaluate a campaign](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/evaluate-campaign.md).
9. Check the draft against the context pack: every claim, permission, word to avoid, internal number and roadmap item.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Event follow-up: <event>, <dates>

Rows in: <n>. Duplicates: <n>. Removed: <n, with reasons>.

## Lead tiers
| Tier | Count | MQLs | Action | Sender | Due date |

## Messages
Tier A
Subject: <50 characters at most>
Body: <120 words at most, one ask>
Tier B
<same fields>
Tier C
<same fields>
No-show, webinars only
<same fields>

## Sales handoff
| Rank | Lead | Title and company | Fit evidence | Trigger | Next step | Seller | Due |

## Calendar
| Date | Step | Owner | Approver |

## Report
| Measure | Count | Target if supplied | Next read date |

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Do tier counts add up to the rows in, minus duplicates and removals?
- [ ] Is MQL status marked by your definition, with nobody marked SQL?
- [ ] Does each message refer only to what happened, with one ask and no invented talk?
- [ ] Do leads without consent stay off marketing email?
- [ ] Does every message use only proof with the right permission and no internal number?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Event follow-up: regional learning and development conference, March 9 to 11, 2027**

Rows in: 62. Duplicates: 2. Removed: 4 (two customers, two under 100 learners).

**Lead tiers**

| Tier | Count | MQLs | Action | Sender | Due date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 9 | 6 | Personal message | [seller needed] | Mar 12 |
| B | 19 | 0 | Marketing email | Marketer | Mar 15 |
| C | 28 | 0 | Resource email | Marketer | Mar 18 |

**Messages** (Tier A shown)

Tier A
Subject: Showing your training changed the work
Body: [First name], you asked how to show your leadership team that training changed how people reason. [personal detail from notes]. CogniATion Coach questions employees about a work problem and scores their reasoning against a rubric. You see progress by team, and never an individual's conversation. Can I show you on a call this week?

**Sales handoff**

| Rank | Lead | Title and company | Fit evidence | Trigger | Next step | Seller | Due |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [lead name] | Head of learning, [accounting firm] | 150 people to train | AI assistant rollout | Demo | [seller needed] | Mar 12 |

**Calendar**

| Date | Step | Owner | Approver |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mar 5 | Approve Tier A text | Marketer | Tom Ferrant |

**Report:** 6 MQLs against a target of 4. Next read Mar 25.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all counts and the sample lead are invented.
- Assumption: Tier A text counts as sales material, Tom Ferrant, 2 days.
- Inference: 6 Tier A leads requested demos, so they meet the MQL definition.
- Unknown: how are leads routed across the three account executives?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Follow up after an event". Read the context pack.
Task: <the event and dates, what was promised, and how leads route to sellers>.
<paste the lead list here>
```


===== FILE: skills/events-and-webinars/plan-event.md =====

---
title: Plan an event
type: skill
function: events-and-webinars
owner: events-and-webinar-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The event name, dates and location, the organizer's attendee information, the quotes, and who can staff it.
also_for: [trade show, conference booth, should we sponsor, event roi, sponsorship decision, booth planning, in-person event, leads from events]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a trade show or in-person event plan with a decision, targets, budget, booth or session, lead capture and timeline.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you are deciding on or preparing for a trade show or other in-person event. Do not use it for an online session; use [Plan a webinar](plan-webinar.md) instead. Once the event is over, use [Follow up after an event](follow-up-after-event.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: sales team, nameable people, internal numbers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what can be shown in a demo.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: fit criteria, disqualifiers, segment priority.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who to talk to, their objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for booth copy.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions, conversion rates, quarter budget.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, approvers, spending limits.

The marketer supplies the event name, dates and location; the organizer's attendee information; quotes for the booth or session, travel and materials; and who can staff it.

## Procedure

1. Make the attendance decision from the organizer's attendee information, which is the organizer's claim. Go: attendees include ICP companies in the first or second priority segment. Go smaller: only a lower segment or part of the ICP. Do not attend: no match. No information: Unknown, and state what would make it Go.
2. Set one goal and work out the targets. Use: break-even SQLs = total cost ÷ (average contract value × SQL to won rate), and MQLs needed = SQLs ÷ MQL to SQL rate. Round up and show the math as an estimate. If a value is blank, write [contract value needed] or [rate needed].
3. If a booth conversation would not count under the MQL definition in goals-and-kpis.md, say so and name the step that makes it count, such as logging a demo request.
4. Build the budget. List fee, travel, materials, giveaways and lead capture. Each amount comes from a quote the marketer supplied, or reads [quote needed]. Show the total and its share of the quarter budget. Flag each line that passes the spending limit in channels-and-tools.md, with the approver and approval time.
5. Write the presence. Default to a booth, and add a session only if the organizer offers a slot and the topic works without a pitch. Write a headline that follows brand-voice.md, proof to show, one demo, and up to three qualifying questions drawn from the ICP situation and triggers. Anything visible to the public uses only proof marked public, and sales only proof stays one-to-one. Leave out roadmap items and anything marked internal.
6. Plan staffing. Each open hour needs two people, and one of them sells. Use names the marketer gave, or [person needed].
7. Set lead capture. Fields: name, company, title, email, ICP fields, current method, trigger, tier, next step, consent status. Tiers: A asked for a demo or named a trigger, and fits; B fits, with no ask; C everything else. Staff tier leads on the spot. Have legal confirm the consent wording.
8. Build the timeline by counting back from the first event day, with real dates: 8 weeks, approvals and booking; 6 weeks, design brief; 4 weeks, invitations; 2 weeks, personal outreach by sales; 1 week, staff briefing; next business day after the event, Tier A follow-up. If fewer than 8 weeks remain, compress and say what is at risk. Add approval time from channels-and-tools.md. Use only channels listed there. These times and the staffing rule are starting rules the marketer can change.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: fit rules, permissions, words to avoid, approvers, spending limits.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
## Event plan: <event>, <dates>, <location>

Decision: <Go, Go smaller, Do not attend or Unknown>, <reason, 25 words at most>
Status: Draft, <internal only, if it holds internal numbers>

## Goal and targets
Goal: <one sentence>
| Measure | Target | How worked out |

## Budget
| Line | Amount or [quote needed] | Approver if over limit |
Total: <amount, share of quarter budget>

## Booth or session
Format: <booth, session or both>
Headline: <10 words at most>
Proof shown: <items, each marked public>
Demo: <25 words at most>
Qualifying questions: <up to 3>
Do not say: <up to 4 items>

## Staffing
<who covers what, 3 lines at most>

## Lead capture
Fields: <list>
Tiers: <A, B and C rules, one line each>
MQL rule: <how a booth lead counts, 25 words at most>

## Timeline
| Date | Step | Owner | Skill or approver |

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the decision rest on attendee information you supplied, or say Unknown?
- [ ] Is every budget amount from a quote, or marked as [quote needed]?
- [ ] Does the plan say how a booth lead counts under your MQL definition?
- [ ] Is everything visible at the booth limited to proof marked public?
- [ ] Are the lead tiers the same ones your follow-up will use?

## Example (fictional company)

**Event plan: regional learning and development conference, March 9 to 11, 2027, [location needed]**

Decision: Unknown. Go if attendees include ICP firms in the top two segments.
Status: Draft, internal only.

**Goal and targets**

Goal: book demos with heads of learning.

| Measure | Target | How worked out |
|---|---|---|
| SQLs | 1 | $18,000 ÷ ($81,000 × 24%) = 0.9 |
| MQLs | 4 | 1 ÷ 26% = 3.8 |

**Budget**

Booth fee and travel [quote needed]. Total: $18,000 assumed, about 26% of $70,000. Lines over $5,000: Marta Ilves, 3 days.

**Booth or session**

Format: booth
Headline: Your analysts have answers. Can they defend them?
Proof shown: median of 9 days to first live session (public); Lena Marsh quote (public).
Demo: try a client memo scenario, see CogniATion Coach decline to answer, view a sample team report.
Qualifying questions: Have you rolled out a general AI assistant? How many people would you train?
Do not say: roadmap items, the February release, individual scores.

**Staffing**

Marketer and [account executive needed].

**Lead capture**

Fields: contact details, employees, people to train, current method, trigger, tier, next step, consent.
Tiers: A asked for a demo or named a trigger. B fits, no ask. C the rest.
MQL rule: log demo requests on the spot. A scan alone is not an MQL.

**Timeline**

| Date | Step | Owner | Skill or approver |
|---|---|---|---|
| Feb 1 | Book booth | Marketer | Over $5,000: Marta Ilves |
| Mar 12 | Tier A follow-up | Sales | Follow up after an event |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: total cost of $18,000.
- Estimate: targets use internal contract value and Q3 2026 rates, rounded up.
- Unknown: what are the organizer's attendee list and booth price?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan an event". Read the context pack.
Task: <the event, dates, location and who can staff it>.
<paste attendee information and quotes here>
```


===== FILE: skills/events-and-webinars/plan-webinar.md =====

---
title: Plan a webinar
type: skill
function: events-and-webinars
owner: events-and-webinar-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The date or window, available speakers, any topic idea, and past registration and attendance numbers if you have them.
also_for: [no webinar history, how many sign-ups, online event, virtual event, webinar topic, webinar promotion, run of show, low webinar attendance, webinar for leads, live demo session]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a webinar plan with goal, topic, promotion timeline, run of show, roles and a failure plan.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you are planning one live online session. Do not use it for an in-person event; use [Plan an event](plan-event.md) instead. For the wider campaign around it, use [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md), and once the session is over, use [Follow up after an event](follow-up-after-event.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: people who can be named.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what can be shown live.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority and triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: daily problems and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for all copy.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL definition and current goals.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: list size, channels, approvers, limits.

The marketer supplies the date or window, speakers, any topic idea, and any past registration and attendance numbers.

## Procedure

1. Set the goal. Name the KPI in goals-and-kpis.md the webinar serves. Read the MQL definition and say which attendees count under it. If fit data is needed to count them, add those fields to the registration form.
2. Set targets: sign-ups needed = attendee target ÷ past attendance rate, shown as an estimate. With no history, use 30% to 50% of sign-ups attending (typical range, estimate), show sign-ups as a range, and say to replace it with the marketer's own numbers after the first event. With no attendee target, use one week's MQLs from the baseline (a starting rule), or ask.
3. Write three topic candidates. Each one states the persona, the daily problem from personas.md, the pillar and whether public proof exists. Reject any candidate that needs a roadmap item, a feature product.md does not list, or proof that is not marked public. Choose by these tests in order, including any topic the marketer gave: higher-priority segment, public proof available, fit with a current campaign in goals-and-kpis.md.
4. Write the title, saying what the attendee will be able to do, the abstract and three takeaways. Follow brand-voice.md. The session teaches first, and the product appears only as the way to do what was taught.
5. Assign roles: host, presenter, producer, chat moderator. The presenter cannot also produce. One person can hold producer and moderator, or host and moderator. Use only names from company.md or the marketer. Anyone else is [speaker needed]. A customer guest needs public permission in proof-points.md and the customer's agreement, so allow for approval time.
6. Build the promotion timeline by counting back from the session, with real dates: 4 weeks, registration page live; 3 weeks, first invitation; 2 weeks, social posts; 1 week, second invitation and personal invitations from sales; 1 day and 1 hour, reminders. If fewer than 3 weeks remain, say so and name what gets cut. Use only channels in channels-and-tools.md. Add paid spend only if the marketer asks and tracking is in place. Name the skill for each piece of copy. These times and the 45-minute default are starting rules the marketer can change.
7. Write the run of show by the minute: start time, what happens, who, what is on screen. Default to 45 minutes: 3 opening, 27 teaching and demo, 12 questions, 3 next step. Put one next step at the close and prepare two starter questions.
8. Write the failure plan: a dry run two business days before, a recorded demo if the live one fails, a named backup if the presenter drops out, what to do if the webinar tool goes down, and the follow-up date.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: claims, permissions, words to avoid and limits. For any blank field, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Webinar plan: <title>

Date and length: <date, time, minutes>
Goal: <KPI served, 25 words at most>
Who counts: <how attendees become MQLs, 25 words at most>
Targets: <attendees, sign-ups, with math; with no history, a range marked "typical range, estimate", to replace after the first event>
Form fields: <up to 8>

## Topic
| Topic | Persona | Segment | Pillar | Public proof | Result |
<3 rows>

## Copy
Title: <70 characters at most>
Abstract: <80 words at most>
Takeaways: <3 lines>
Proof used: <items, each marked public>

## Roles
Host: <name>. Presenter: <name>. Producer: <name>. Moderator: <name>

## Promotion timeline
| Date | Channel | Message | Owner | Skill or approver |

## Run of show
| Minute | What happens | Who | On screen |

## Failure plan
<up to 5 lines>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the plan say which attendees count as MQLs, and does the form capture what that takes?
- [ ] Does the topic pass the rejection rules, with no roadmap item or feature outside product.md?
- [ ] Is every proof item marked public?
- [ ] Does each role have a real name or a visible placeholder, with the presenter not producing?
- [ ] Is there a plan for a failed demo and a missing presenter?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Webinar plan: How to show that training changed reasoning**

Date and length: February 24, 2027, 45 minutes
Goal: MQLs for the professional services campaign.
Who counts: attendees who fit the ICP.
Targets: 20 attendees who fit the ICP, one week of MQLs (260 / 13, a starting rule). 40 to 67 sign-ups: 30% to 50% attend (typical range, estimate). Replace with your own numbers after this event.
Form fields: name, email, company, employees, people to train

**Topic**

| Topic | Persona | Segment | Pillar | Public proof | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Show that training changed reasoning | Head of learning | Professional services | You can see reasoning improve | Brightmere Advisory 22% result | Chosen |

**Copy**

Title: How to show that training changed reasoning
Abstract: What completion data cannot show, how to score reasoning with a rubric, and how to report by team.
Proof used: Brightmere Advisory 22% result, public.

**Roles**

Host: the marketer. Presenter: [speaker needed]. Producer and moderator: [second person needed].

**Promotion timeline**

| Date | Channel | Message | Owner | Skill or approver |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Jan 27 | Website | Registration page | Marketer | Write a web page |
| Feb 3 | Email list | Invitation | Marketer | Write a marketing email |

**Run of show**

| Minute | What happens | Who | On screen |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Scoring and demo | Presenter | Sample scenario, team report |
| 42 | Request a demo | Host | Link |

**Failure plan**

Dry run February 22. Recorded demo as backup.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the date, after the February 15 campaign.
- Unknown: who presents?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan a webinar". Read the context pack.
Task: <the date or window, speakers, and topic or ask for options>.
```


===== FILE: skills/go-to-market-and-launches/plan-product-launch.md =====

---
title: Plan a product launch
type: skill
function: go-to-market-and-launches
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, proof-points, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: What the release does in plain words, who confirmed it, and the planned date.
also_for: [release in eight weeks, product launch, new feature, launch plan, launch timeline, announce a release, tell sales about the release, launch dates]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Sets the launch tier, timeline, owners and assets for one release, worked back from the launch date and the approval times.
judge: ["Would a customer notice this release if you told them nothing?", "Does the tier reflect what buyers will care about or what the team is proud of?", "What will sales do differently the week after launch?", "What is the one thing you want a buyer to do because of this launch?"]
show_to: your product lead, and ask which promise in it the release could fail to keep
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for one release with a known scope and a planned date. Do not use it to enter a new segment or market or to bring a new product to market for the first time; use [Write a go-to-market plan](write-gtm-plan.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: team size and capacity.
- [[context-pack:product]]: current features, known weak spots, what it is not, roadmap rules.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who the release changes things for.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, messages by persona, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, baselines, other dated priorities.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, approvers and typical times, hard rules.

The marketer supplies: what the release does in plain words, who confirmed it, the planned date, and any early customer feedback or sales requests. No release facts come from anywhere else.

## Procedure

1. Write down what ships, in at most 40 words, from the marketer's description only. If the scope is blank, ask before drafting. If the date or another field is blank, use a placeholder such as [launch date needed], count days back from launch day and ask in the last section.
2. Set the launch tier with these rules, and stop at the first that fits:
   - Tier 1: the release changes who can buy, changes the price, adds a new product, or makes a new claim under a messaging pillar.
   - Tier 2: the release gives current customers or one persona a new capability, or fixes a known weak spot in product.md.
   - Tier 3: a fix or minor improvement.
3. State the one fact that would move the release to another tier.
4. Pick one primary persona, the one the release changes most, or the first in personas.md on a tie (a starting rule), and at most two others. Write one message for each, tied to a pillar and using only what the release does.
5. Build the asset list from the tier, in the order below, which runs from most to least needed. Each asset names the skill that produces it, an owner and a due date.
   - Tier 1: [web page](../content/write-web-page.md), [sales one-pager](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-sales-one-pager.md), [battlecard update](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-battlecard.md), [launch email](../content/write-marketing-email.md), [press release](../pr-and-comms/write-press-release.md), [media pitch](../pr-and-comms/write-media-pitch.md), [social posts](../content/write-social-posts.md), [video](../content/write-video-script.md) or [webinar](../events-and-webinars/plan-webinar.md).
   - Tier 2: sales one-pager or update, web page update, launch email, social posts.
   - Tier 3: a mention in the next newsletter, and a release note as a "No skill, ad hoc output".
6. Map proof to each message, using only items whose permission fits the channel. If no proof exists for this release, write a placeholder such as [customer quote needed]. Do not stretch older proof to cover it.
7. Build the timeline backward from launch day, counting L minus working days: launch and after-launch tasks, sales briefed and sales material delivered before anything public, approvals using the typical times in channels-and-tools.md, then production and scope freeze. Plan no public mention before launch day unless product.md clears that roadmap item.
8. Run the capacity check, as the office manual counts it, against company.md and the other dated priorities in goals-and-kpis.md. If effort or days are unknown, write Not assessed. If it does not fit, cut or move assets from the end of the tier's list. Never shorten an approver's typical time. List every cut.
9. Set one or two launch measures from goals-and-kpis.md, with baselines and a date to read them. Leave targets without a basis as placeholders.
10. Write what happens if the release slips: which assets hold, and who tells sales.
11. Write the decision check from the office manual.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: product limits, words to avoid, permission status, hard rules, roadmap rules. Fix anything that contradicts it.
13. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section. Schedule [Review launch readiness](review-launch-readiness.md) on the timeline before the first public date.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Release: <max 40 words>
Launch date: <date or placeholder>
Launch tier: <1, 2 or 3, plus one-sentence reason>
What would change the tier: <one sentence>

## Audience and messages
| Persona | Message (max 20 words) | Pillar | Proof and permission |

## Assets
| Asset | Skill | Owner | Due | Approver |

## Timeline
| Date or L minus | Task | Owner |

## Capacity
<Who, how much time, what was cut or moved, max 50 words>

## Launch measures
| Measure | Baseline | Target | Read on |

## If the release slips
<Max 40 words>

## Decision check
<five lines, as the office manual sets out>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the tier follow from the written rules, with a reason?
- [ ] Is every message free of anything product.md says the product is not?
- [ ] Does every asset have an owner, a due date and the right approver?
- [ ] Do the timeline dates allow each approver's typical time?
- [ ] Is sales briefed before anything goes public?
- [ ] Does the plan fit the team's capacity, with cuts listed?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft, internal only.

**Release:** CogniATion Coach manager view. [Release scope needed from Anika Sole.]

**Launch date:** February 2027 [date needed].

**Launch tier:** 2. It gives managers a new capability and changes neither price nor buyer.

**What would change the tier:** If it changes who can buy, it moves to 1.

**Audience and messages**

| Persona | Message | Pillar | Proof and permission |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head of learning | Give managers a team view of reasoning, no individual's conversation shown. | You can see reasoning improve | [Customer quote needed] |

**Assets**

| Asset | Skill | Owner | Due | Approver |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Web page update | Write a web page | Marketer | L-7 | Anika Sole, 2 days |
| Launch email | Write a marketing email | Marketer | L-5 | Anika Sole, 2 days |
| Sales one-pager | Write a sales one-pager | Marketer | L-7 | Tom Ferrant, 2 days |

**Timeline:** L-10 scope freeze. L-5 sales briefed. L-3 readiness review. L launch, nothing public before.

**Capacity:** One marketer, designer four days a month. Professional services campaign due February 15, so social posts move after launch.

**Launch measures:** Website demo requests, baseline 58 in Q3 2026. Target: [needed]. Read on L+30.

**If the release slips:** Hold public assets. Marketer tells Tom Ferrant.

**Decision check:** Constraint: too little proof. Why this: managers can see progress. Evidence: [needed]. Test: tier 2, not tier 1. Displaces: social posts, after launch.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: no price change, view stays at team level, L-minus dates are provisional.
- Unknown: Which February date, is it confirmed, and does the view go below team level?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan a product launch". Read the context pack.
Task: <what ships, the planned date and who confirmed it>.
```


===== FILE: skills/go-to-market-and-launches/review-launch-readiness.md =====

---
title: Review launch readiness
type: skill
function: go-to-market-and-launches
owner: marketing-reviewer
reads: [company, product, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The launch plan, the full text of each asset, the launch date, and which approvals are in.
also_for: [are we ready to launch, go or no-go, launch checklist, launch next week, launch review, should we delay, launch risks, final check before launch]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Returns a go, no-go or fix-first verdict on a launch plan and its assets, with blockers, fixes and approval status.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this once a launch plan and its assets exist, before the first public date. Do not use it to build the plan; use [Plan a product launch](plan-product-launch.md) instead. For one output outside a launch, use [Review any output](../marketing-ops/review-any-output.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: which numbers may be published.
- [[context-pack:product]]: features, what it is not, roadmap rules, prices.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: sourced facts, to check every competitor claim.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: permission status for each customer, number and quote.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for public copy.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: definitions and baselines, to check launch measures.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers, typical times, channel limits, hard rules.

The marketer supplies: the launch plan, the full text of each asset, today's date, the launch date, who confirmed the release is on track, and which approvals are in.

## Procedure

1. List every asset in the plan and mark it Reviewed, Not supplied or Not finished. Review only what was pasted.
2. Check the release. If nobody named has confirmed that the release and the date are on track, record a blocker.
3. Check each claim in each asset against the context pack, in this order: product.md for what the product does and does not do, including roadmap rules; company.md for numbers; proof-points.md for customer names, numbers and quotes, against where the asset will appear; competitors.md for competitor claims, which need a source and a date; hard rules in channels-and-tools.md. Quote the words at fault and name the file.
4. Check public assets against brand-voice.md and the words to avoid in messaging.md.
5. Check approvals. For each asset, find its approver in channels-and-tools.md. Mark it Received, Requested or Not requested. Only the marketer's word counts as Received. Count the working days left and compare them with the typical time. If the time left is shorter, it cannot be fixed in time.
6. Check sales readiness: sales material is finished, sales is briefed before the first public date, and the plan says who answers buyer questions.
7. Check measurement and fallback: tracked links or tracking in place for anything paid, a launch measure with a baseline that matches goals-and-kpis.md, and a written plan if the release slips.
8. If a context pack file needed for a check is blank, mark that check Unknown and ask the marketer. Do not pass it, and do not fill the gap.
9. Rate each finding:
   - Blocker: a false claim, a claim the product cannot meet, proof without permission, a broken hard rule, a missing required approval, an unconfirmed release, or a planned asset that the launch cannot run without.
   - Fix: wrong voice, weak proof, a missing measure or fallback, or a late or unbriefed sales team.
   - Note: anything else worth a look.
10. Set the verdict with these rules, and stop at the first that fits:
    - No-go: a blocker cannot be fixed before the launch date, or the product cannot do what the main message claims.
    - Fix first: there is at least one blocker or an unknown, and all can be fixed before the launch date.
    - Go: no blockers and no unknowns. Fix items can remain, listed with owners.
11. For each blocker and fix, say what must change and who does it. Do not rewrite the asset unless asked.
12. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section. Say that the verdict is a recommendation and that the marketer decides.

## Output format

```
Verdict: <Go, No-go or Fix first>
Why: <max 30 words>
Latest date to fix: <date, from the launch date minus the longest approval still needed>
Status: Draft, recommendation only

## Blockers
| Asset | Words or issue | Breaks | What must change | Owner |

## Fixes
| Asset | Issue | What must change | Owner |

## Notes
<Max 3, one line each, or None>

## Asset status
| Asset | Reviewed, Not supplied or Not finished | Approver | Approval status | Time needed |

## Checks not run
<Each check not run, and why, or None>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the verdict come first, and does it follow from the written rules?
- [ ] Does each blocker quote the words at fault and name the file they break?
- [ ] Was each claim checked against the permission status for the place it will appear?
- [ ] Is each approval checked against the typical time and the days left?
- [ ] Are assets not supplied listed, instead of being passed?
- [ ] Does every blocker and fix have an owner?
- [ ] Is the review free of rewritten copy, unless the marketer asked for it?

## Example (fictional company)

**Verdict:** Fix first

**Why:** Two claims on the page are wrong and the date is unconfirmed, but all can be fixed and approved before launch.

**Latest date to fix:** L-2, two days for Anika Sole.

**Status:** Draft, recommendation only

**Blockers**

| Asset | Words or issue | Breaks | What must change | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch page | "See each analyst's score" | Hard rule, channels-and-tools.md; product.md: no reports below team level | Remove the claim | Marketer |
| Launch page | "Makes new managers smarter" | Words to avoid, messaging.md; hard rule on intelligence claims | Remove the claim | Marketer |
| Launch page, launch email | No product claims approval | channels-and-tools.md | Send to Anika Sole, 2 days | Marketer |
| Release | No named person has confirmed the date | Unconfirmed release | Get a named confirmation | Marketer |

**Fixes**

| Asset | Issue | What must change | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Launch email | Exclamation point in the subject line | Remove it, per brand-voice.md | Marketer |

**Notes:** None

**Asset status**

| Asset | Status | Approver | Approval status | Time needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch page | Reviewed | Anika Sole | Not requested | 2 days |
| Launch email | Reviewed | Anika Sole | Not requested | 2 days |
| Sales one-pager | Not supplied | Tom Ferrant | Unknown | 2 days |

**Checks not run:** Sales one-pager, not supplied.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the reviewed plan and copy are illustrative, supplied by the marketer for the manager view release.
- Unknown: Who can confirm the release date, and can you paste the one-pager?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Review launch readiness". Read the context pack.
Task: <today's date, the launch date, who confirmed it, and which approvals are in>.
<paste the launch plan and each asset here>
```


===== FILE: skills/go-to-market-and-launches/write-gtm-plan.md =====

---
title: Write a go-to-market plan
type: skill
function: go-to-market-and-launches
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: What the plan is for, the start date, the budget, and any research on the target.
also_for: [go to market, gtm strategy, new segment, new market, new product, enter a market, sell to a new audience, market entry]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a go-to-market plan for one new product, new segment or new market, with a fit decision, route to market, phases, targets and a capacity check.
judge: ["Why would a buyer in this market choose you over doing nothing?", "Who is the first customer you could name and how would you reach them this month?", "Does sales have the time and a reason to sell this next to what they already sell?", "What result would make you stop and is anyone willing to actually stop?"]
show_to: your sales lead and your product lead, and ask what would make them pull out of this plan
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this for a new product, a new segment or a new market. Do not use it for one release of an existing product; use [Plan a product launch](plan-product-launch.md) instead. For one campaign inside an existing plan, use [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: team and publishable numbers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what it is and is not, price, weak spots.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: ICP, disqualifiers, segment priority, sales cycle, triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: roles, objections, where they get information.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: positioning, pillars, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: sourced facts and what buyers do today.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, budget, baselines.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, limits, approvers, hard rules.

The marketer supplies: what the plan is for, the start date, the budget, and any research on the target, such as notes or market data with its source.

## Procedure

1. Classify the plan as new product, new segment or new market. If it fits more than one, plan for the one that changes most and list the others under Unknown.
2. Run the fit check: compare the target with the ICP, the disqualifiers and "what it is not" in product.md, plus language, region and integration limits for a new market. If the target meets a disqualifier or needs something the product does not do, write "Recommend against as written" on the decision line, name what would have to change, and still complete the plan.
3. If a blank changes the target or the decision, ask all such questions together before drafting. If it affects a detail, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent a market size, competitor move, customer or price.
4. Write the target: segment, personas, trigger. Use only the context pack and the marketer's research.
5. Restate the positioning for this target in at most 40 words. Keep the existing pillars. If the research contradicts one, keep it and ask in the last section.
6. Map proof to each pillar, using only items whose permission status fits the use. Mark any pillar with no public proof from this target as a gap, and make closing it a task in phase 1.
7. Pick one primary route: sales-led, inbound or partner. Choose inbound if icp-and-segments.md says the segment is served through inbound only, or if the marketer says sales has no capacity for it. Choose partner only if the marketer names a partner. Otherwise choose sales-led. Name at most three channels for phase 1, taken from channels-and-tools.md and from where the personas get information.
8. Write three phases: prepare, first test, expand. Each has dates, tasks, an owner and one exit test. The first test runs at the smallest size that can pass or fail its exit test. A phase starts only when the last one's exit test is met.
9. Set targets from goals-and-kpis.md, using its definitions and baselines. Label any split or backward calculation as an estimate and show the math. With no baseline, leave a placeholder.
10. Run the capacity check: count the team in company.md against the tasks, cut tasks until they fit, and list the cuts. List each approval and its typical time from channels-and-tools.md.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: product limits, words to avoid, permission status, hard rules. Fix anything that contradicts it.
12. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Decision: <Proceed, Proceed with conditions, or Recommend against as written, plus one sentence why>
Plan type: <New product, new segment or new market>

## Target
- Segment: <one line>
- Personas: <champion, approver, signer, one line each>
- Trigger: <one line>
- Fit check: <max 40 words, including disqualifiers checked>

## Positioning for this target
<Max 40 words>

## Proof by pillar
| Pillar | Proof | Permission | Gap |

## Route to market
- Primary route: <sales-led, inbound or partner, with one-sentence reason>
- Phase 1 channels: <max 3>
- Competitor to expect: <one line per competitor, sourced, max 3>

## Phases
| Phase | Dates | Tasks (max 5) | Owner | Exit test |

## Targets
| Metric | Baseline | Target | Date | Basis |

## Budget and capacity
- Budget: <amount or placeholder>
- Capacity: <who, how much, what was cut>
- Approvals: <item, approver, typical time>

## Risks
<Max 3, each with the response>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the decision line appear first, and does it follow from the fit check?
- [ ] Does each pillar show proof with a permission status, or a named gap?
- [ ] Does each phase have an owner and an exit test that can fail?
- [ ] Is every split or calculated target labeled as an estimate with the math shown?
- [ ] Does the work fit the team, with cuts and approvers listed?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Decision:** Proceed with conditions. Financial services fits the ICP, but has no approved customer proof yet.

**Plan type:** New segment, moving financial services to active outreach.

**Target:** Banks, insurers and asset managers. Champion: head of learning. Approver: VP of people. Trigger: company-wide AI assistant rollout. Fit check: no disqualifier met.

**Positioning for this target:** For heads of learning at financial services firms whose analysts now lean on AI assistants, CogniATion Coach makes analysts practice reasoning on real work problems, because the chatbot declines to give the answer.

**Proof by pillar:** It asks: Lena Marsh quote, public, professional services, so a gap. Practice: 72% weekly completion, all customers, public. Reasoning improves: Brightmere Advisory result, same gap.

**Route to market:** Sales-led. Phase 1 channels: email list, webinar, learning and development conference. Expect Elenchly (completion data and quiz scores, per three prospects, 2026).

**Phases:** Prepare, by January 15, 2027: close the proof gap, draft security review answers; exit test is one approved customer. First test: through March 31. Expand: [date needed].

**Targets:** Pipeline $240,000 by March 31, 2027.

**Budget and capacity:** [Budget needed from the $70,000 quarter]. One marketer, designer four days a month. Budget over $5,000 per item: Marta Ilves, three days.

**Risks:** No proof by January 15: delay the first test. Long security reviews: read pipeline, not wins.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: this segment is not yet in active outreach, and phase dates are a first pass.
- Estimate: $240,000 is the 20% customer share (internal) times the $1.2 million goal, not a forecast.
- Unknown: How much budget and sales time does this segment get?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a go-to-market plan". Read the context pack.
Task: <the new product, segment or market, the start date and the budget>.
<paste any research here, with its source>
```


===== FILE: skills/marketing-ops/audit-tool-stack.md =====

---
title: Audit the tool stack
type: skill
function: marketing-ops
owner: marketing-operations-manager
reads: [channels-and-tools, goals-and-kpis, company]
you_bring: Each tool's annual cost, renewal date, seats paid for and used, and when it was last used. With no tools yet, your budget.
also_for: [too many tools, tool costs, software renewals, martech audit, cut software spend, what tools do we need, unused seats, crm renewal]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Gives each marketing tool a keep, fix or cut decision, using costs and usage the marketer supplies.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before a renewal, during budget planning, or when you suspect you are paying for tools nobody opens. With no tools yet, use it to list which jobs need one. Do not use it to split program money across channels, which belongs to [Allocate the budget](../budget-and-planning/allocate-budget.md), or to fix tagging and naming, which belongs to [Set tracking conventions](set-tracking-conventions.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: tools by job, who has access, hard rules and approval limits.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: KPI list and definitions that depend on a tool, plus the budget.
- [[context-pack:company]]: team size, for judging seat counts.

The marketer pastes into the chat, for each tool:

- Annual cost, renewal date and notice period.
- Seats paid for and seats used, or the plan tier and actual usage.
- When it was last used, and any known problem.
- Whether its cost comes from the marketing budget.

## Procedure

1. List every tool from channels-and-tools.md and every tool the marketer adds, flagging any difference. With no tools, go to step 8. If a tool's cost is missing, write [cost needed] and leave it out of the money totals.
2. For each tool, write what depends on it: any KPI it reports, any definition that needs its data and any hard rule that needs it. Write "nothing" if nothing does.
3. Give each tool one decision by applying these rules in order, and stop at the first that fits. Something depends on it and the marketer reports a problem: Fix. Something depends on it and no problem is reported: Keep. Nothing depends on it, and another listed tool does the same job or it has gone unused for 90 days: Cut. Nothing depends on it and it is in use: Keep, and review at renewal.
4. Change a Keep to Fix when paid seats or the plan tier exceed actual usage. The fix is to drop seats or tier at renewal.
5. If usage or last-used information is missing for a tool, do not decide. Write "no decision" and ask for it in the last section. The 90-day cutoff is a starting rule the marketer can change, not a benchmark.
6. For each Cut and Fix, write the steps: what to do, what breaks or moves, who else must agree and the last date to act, which is the renewal date minus the notice period. Anyone with access to the tool in channels-and-tools.md must be told. Do not name replacement products or describe a vendor's features.
7. Calculate the money: total annual cost, savings from cuts and savings from fixes. Savings start at renewal, so show first-year savings only for the months left after that date. Compare with the budget only if the marketer confirmed the cost comes from it, and show the division.
8. List gaps: any KPI with no tool to report it, and any job with no tool. State the gap only. Do not recommend a purchase. With no tools yet, mark each job "spreadsheet for now" or "needs a tool", with must-haves, a price ceiling and the date needed, naming no product.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: no Cut removes a tool that a KPI, definition or hard rule needs, and anything above the budget approval limit in channels-and-tools.md names that approver. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder and ask in the last section. Never invent.
10. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section, including each cost you did not receive.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Bottom line
<Number of tools to keep, fix and cut, and annual savings. Max 30 words.>

## Decisions
| Tool | Annual cost | Depends on it | Decision | Reason |
<One row per tool. Decision is keep, fix, cut or no decision. Reason max 15 words.>

## Steps for cuts and fixes
| Tool | Step | Who must agree | Last date to act |
<One row per cut or fix.>

## Money
<Total annual cost, annual savings, first-year savings. Max 40 words.>

## Gaps
<Up to 3 bullets, max 20 words each, or "None". No limit with no tools yet.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every tool in channels-and-tools.md have a row?
- [ ] Do the decisions follow the rules in order?
- [ ] Does each cut leave every KPI, definition and hard rule still served?
- [ ] Is every cost supplied by the marketer or left as a placeholder?
- [ ] Does each cut or fix show a last date to act and who must agree?
- [ ] Do first-year savings start at the renewal date?
- [ ] Is the output free of named replacement products?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: all of CogniATion's costs, dates and usage are illustrative, as if pasted by the marketer on November 1, 2026. The survey tool is an illustrative addition.

**Bottom line**

Keep 4, fix 1, cut 1, no decision on 1. Annual savings of $4,200.

**Decisions**

| Tool | Annual cost | Depends on it | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CRM | $14,000 | Pipeline, SQLs | Keep | Works |
| Marketing automation and email | $9,600 | MQLs | Fix | Plan is 10,000 contacts, list is 5,400 |
| Web analytics | $0 | Organic visits, ad tracking limit | Keep | Works |
| Webinar | $2,400 | MQL definition | Keep | Works |
| CMS | $3,000 | Website | Keep | Works |
| Design | [cost needed] | Nothing | No decision | Freelancer's own tools |
| Survey tool | $1,800 | Nothing | Cut | Unused for six months |

**Steps for cuts and fixes**

| Tool | Step | Who must agree | Last date to act |
|---|---|---|---|
| Marketing automation and email | Drop contact tier | Marketer | February 1, 2027 |
| Survey tool | Export results, cancel | Marketer | December 1, 2026 |

**Money**

Total $30,800 before the fix. Annual savings $4,200: $2,400 from the tier, $1,800 from the cut. First-year savings $3,100.

**Gaps**

- Video channel has 25 scenario walkthroughs, but channels-and-tools.md lists no hosting tool.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Estimate: first-year savings cover 12 months from November 1, 2026: 8 months of the tier ($1,600, March 1 renewal), 10 of the cut ($1,500, January 1 renewal). Notice is one month.
- Unknown: Does the CRM cost come from the marketing or the sales budget?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Audit the tool stack". Read the context pack.
Task: <which tools, and the renewal or budget date behind this>.
<paste each tool's costs, renewal dates and usage here>
```


===== FILE: skills/marketing-ops/define-lead-handoff.md =====

---
title: Define the lead handoff
type: skill
function: marketing-ops
owner: marketing-operations-manager
reads: [goals-and-kpis, icp-and-segments, personas, company, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: How leads move today and where they stall, response times, sales capacity, who in sales will agree, and if sales says the leads are bad, the last 30 rejected MQLs with reasons.
also_for: [leads are bad, bad leads, junk leads, lead quality, sales complains, mql definition, sales ignores leads, leads not followed up, lead routing, demo requests go nowhere]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Sets lifecycle stages, routing rules and response times between marketing and sales.
judge: ["Would your sales lead sign these rules today or only accept them in theory?", "If every lead followed these rules would sales have better conversations or only cleaner records?", "What happens when the rules say a lead is good and sales still refuses it?", "Who looks at rejected leads each month and what changes as a result?"]
show_to: the salesperson who works these leads first, and ask which rule they would ignore
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when leads stall between marketing and sales, or when sales disputes lead quality. Do not use it for handing off the leads from one event, which belongs to [Follow up after an event](../events-and-webinars/follow-up-after-event.md), or for what happens to leads not ready for sales, which belongs to [Design a nurture sequence](../campaign-design/design-nurture-sequence.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions, MQL volume, conversion rate and SQL goal.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: fit criteria, disqualifiers, segment priority and sales cycle.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: which job titles count as buyers.
- [[context-pack:company]]: size and roles of the sales team.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who has access to the CRM and marketing automation tool.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- How leads move today, and where they stall.
- Any territory or account ownership rules.
- Current or proposed response times, and how many leads a week each sales role can take.
- Who in sales will agree to the rules.
- If sales says the leads are bad, the last 30 rejected MQLs with reasons.

## Procedure

1. If sales says the leads are bad, start here, before defining anything. Get the last 30 rejected MQLs with reasons (a starting rule), or ask sales for one reason per lead. Sort and count by the reasons in step 7. Say what the largest group points to: not ICP, the fit rules; not ready, nurture; no contact, follow-up; wrong person, contact data. To raise it without a fight, see [Three hard situations](../../guides/hard-situations.md#sales-says-the-leads-are-bad).
2. Write the lifecycle stages in this order: Lead, MQL, SQL, Won or lost, plus two side stages, Recycled and Disqualified. Each gets an entry rule, an owner and an exit rule. Copy the MQL and SQL definitions from goals-and-kpis.md word for word. If blank, and for other stages, propose a rule and label it an assumption. With no sales team, the person who sells is sales.
3. Turn the disqualifiers in icp-and-segments.md into Disqualified rules.
4. Write the routing rules, one row per trigger, in priority order. A lead asking to talk to sales comes first, and every other MQL trigger follows. Every row names exactly one owning role. Apply territory or ownership rules only if the marketer supplied them. Otherwise write [territory rule needed].
5. Set a response time for each routing row, using the marketer's figure. If none was given, write [response time to agree with sales].
6. Set what happens when the time is missed: reassign if a second person holds that role, otherwise tell that person's manager, or write [escalation owner needed].
7. Write the rejection rule. Sales may reject an MQL only with one of these reasons: not ICP, wrong person, no contact after the agreed attempts, duplicate, not ready. "Not ICP" and "duplicate" move the lead to Disqualified. "Not ready" and "no contact" move it to Recycled. "Wrong person" goes back to marketing.
8. Run the capacity check. Weekly volume today is the MQL baseline divided by the weeks in that period. Weekly volume needed is the SQL goal divided by the MQL to SQL rate, then divided by the weeks in the quarter. Show the division and label both as estimates. Flag any shortfall against the marketer's capacity figure, or record capacity as unknown.
9. List the fields the rules need: stage, stage date, owner, rejection reason, first response date. A spreadsheet can hold them.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: definitions are word for word, the sales roles exist in company.md, and the fit rules match icp-and-segments.md. Leave a visible placeholder for any blank field and ask in the last section.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section, including anything sales has not agreed to.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Rejected leads, sorted
| Reason | Count | What it points to |
<Largest first, or "Not in dispute".>

## Lifecycle stages
| Stage | Entry rule | Owner | Exit rule |
<One row per stage. Each cell max 20 words.>

## Routing rules
| Trigger | Goes to | Response time | If missed |
<Up to 6 rows, in priority order.>

## Rejection rule
<The five reasons and where each sends the lead. Max 50 words.>

## Capacity check
<Volume today, volume needed, capacity, and the gap. Max 50 words.>

## CRM fields to set up
<Up to 8 bullets, one field each.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Are the MQL and SQL definitions word for word from goals-and-kpis.md?
- [ ] Are the disqualifiers applied before a lead reaches sales?
- [ ] Is every response time supplied by the marketer or left as a placeholder?
- [ ] Does each routing row say what happens when the time is missed?
- [ ] Has someone in sales been named to agree to the rules?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: the response times are illustrative, as if proposed by CogniATion's marketer. Lead and owner rules await agreement from Tom Ferrant, head of sales. Won or lost and Recycled are left out for length.

**Rejected leads, sorted** (invented for this example)

From 30 rejected MQLs: not ICP 14, not ready 9, no contact 5, wrong person 2. The largest group points to the fit rules, so disqualifiers come first.

**Lifecycle stages**

| Stage | Entry rule | Owner | Exit rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lead | Gave contact details | Marketer | Meets MQL definition, or disqualified |
| MQL | Fits the ICP and has requested a demo, attended a webinar or downloaded two resources | Sales development representative | First call held, or rejected |
| SQL | Accepted by an account executive after a first call | Account executive | Won or lost |
| Disqualified | Fewer than 100 learners, wants a course library, or wants scores on individuals for performance reviews | Marketer | None |

**Routing rules**

| Trigger | Goes to | Response time | If missed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Demo request, ICP fit | Sales development representative | 1 business day | Head of sales told |

**Rejection rule**

Not ICP or duplicate: Disqualified. Not ready or no contact: Recycled. Wrong person: back to marketer.

**Capacity check**

About 20 MQLs a week today, 27 needed, one person. Capacity unknown.

**CRM fields to set up**

- Stage, stage date, owner, rejection reason, first response date.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Estimate: 260 / 13 = 20. 90 / 0.26 / 13 = 27.
- Unknown: How many MQLs a week can that person take?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Define the lead handoff". Read the context pack.
Task: <where leads stall and who in sales will agree>.
<paste today's routing, response times and sales capacity here>
```


===== FILE: skills/marketing-ops/review-any-output.md =====

---
title: Review any output
type: skill
function: marketing-ops
owner: marketing-reviewer
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The output in full, the skill it came from, and where it will appear.
also_for: [check my draft, proofread, fact check, is this ok to publish, review copy, check claims, before it goes out, ceo review]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Critiques any output against the context pack and the checklist of the skill it came from, without rewriting it.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before anything public, or anything with a customer name or a number, is used. Do not use it for messaging and positioning, which get [Review messaging](../positioning-and-messaging/review-messaging.md), or for a launch plan, which gets [Review launch readiness](../go-to-market-and-launches/review-launch-readiness.md). To judge results after they happen, use [Evaluate a campaign](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/evaluate-campaign.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: which numbers can be used, and where.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what it does and is not, prices, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: whether the audience fits.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: whether the output speaks to the right person.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: claims, proof and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: competitor facts, with sources and dates.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: permission status of each customer, number and quote.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice rules for public copy.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: definitions and targets.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: hard rules and who approves what.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- The output to review, in full.
- The title of the skill it came from, and its checklist if not available.
- Where it will appear: public, one-to-one sales or internal.
- Any task evidence behind the draft: exports, notes, answers.

## Procedure

1. Set the audience from the marketer's statement. If none was given, review it as public and record that as an assumption.
2. Find the quality checklist of the skill it came from. If it is not available, ask for it. If the marketer wants the review anyway, write "checklist not run" and do not improvise one.
3. Go through the output line by line and list each factual claim, name, number, quote, price, date and competitor statement.
4. Check each item against the context pack and any task evidence, and mark it supported, contradicted or not found. Show any conflict between the two. Anything not found is a problem unless it is a visible placeholder. If a file needed for a check is blank, write "could not check" and ask. Never invent.
5. Apply the permission check for the audience. Task evidence grants no permission. Every customer name, number and quote must have a status in proof-points.md that allows this use. Every company number must have a status in company.md that allows it.
6. Check the product limits in product.md, the competitor sources in competitors.md, and the hard rules in channels-and-tools.md.
7. For public copy, check the words to avoid in messaging.md and the rules in brand-voice.md.
8. Run the checklist of the skill it came from. Answer each question yes or no, with the line or fact that shows it.
9. Sort every problem. A blocker is a contradicted or unsupported fact, a permission breach, an unannounced roadmap item, a broken product limit, a breach of a hard rule or an invented placeholder fill. A fix is a word to avoid, a voice problem, a missing template field, a length breach or a failed checklist item that is not already a blocker.
10. Set the verdict. Any blocker means "Do not use yet". No blockers and at least one fix means "Fix first". Neither means "Facts and format checked". End every verdict with "Strategy not judged." Never call the output approved, since only the marketer changes its status.
11. Name the single weakest idea in the draft, the one a senior marketer would push on first. Ask it as a question for the marketer, and say why. It does not change the verdict.
12. List the approvers from channels-and-tools.md that this output needs.
13. Say what to change in each problem row, in one line. Do not rewrite the output unless the marketer asks.
14. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.
