## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Verdict
<Do not use yet, Fix first, or Facts and format checked, with the count of blockers and fixes. Then: Strategy not judged. Max 30 words.>
Weakest idea: <the single weakest idea, and why, as a question for the marketer. Max 30 words.>

## Blockers
| Where | Problem | Source | What to change |
<One row per blocker, or "None". What to change max 15 words.>

## Fixes
| Where | Problem | Source | What to change |
<One row per fix, or "None". What to change max 15 words.>

## Checklist results
| Question | Yes or no | Evidence |
<One row per question, or "checklist not run".>

## Approvals needed
<Up to 4 bullets: what needs approval, who approves and typical time.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Was every claim, name, number and quote checked?
- [ ] Was the permission check run for the stated audience?
- [ ] Does each problem name the context pack file behind it?
- [ ] Do the verdict and the problem sorting follow the rules?
- [ ] Was the real checklist of the skill it came from used, or marked as not run?
- [ ] Are the approvers named from channels-and-tools.md?
- [ ] Did the review stay a critique, with no rewrite unless asked?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: the draft below is an illustrative CogniATion search ad, reviewed as public. It was deliberately written with errors, and is not usable copy.

Draft reviewed: "Unlike Arguvia, CogniATion Coach raises IQ and makes your analysts smarter. 64 customer companies live. The manager view arrives in February."

**Verdict**

Do not use yet. 3 blockers, 1 fix. Strategy not judged.

Weakest idea: why would a head of learning care that you beat Arguvia, when the ad never mentions showing that training changed how people work?

**Blockers**

| Where | Problem | Source | What to change |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Unlike Arguvia" | Competitor name in an ad | channels-and-tools.md, hard rules | Remove the name |
| "raises IQ" | Claim about intelligence | channels-and-tools.md, hard rules; product.md, what it is not | Describe practice on reasoning, with no intelligence claim |
| "arrives in February" | Roadmap, internal only | product.md, goals-and-kpis.md | Remove the line |

**Fixes**

| Where | Problem | Source | What to change |
|---|---|---|---|
| "smarter" | Word to avoid | messaging.md | Use "reasoning" or "judgment" language |

**Checklist results**

| Question | Yes or no | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Are competitors left out if channels-and-tools.md forbids naming them? | No | "Unlike Arguvia" |

**Approvals needed**

- Ads go to the marketer, same day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the skill it came from is [Write ad variations](../paid-demand-gen/write-ad-variations.md).
- Inference: "64 customer companies" is publishable, from company.md, as of September 30, 2026.
- Unknown: What character limits apply?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Review any output". Read the context pack.
Task: <the skill it came from, and where it will appear>.
<paste the output here, in full>
```


===== FILE: skills/marketing-ops/set-tracking-conventions.md =====

---
title: Set tracking conventions
type: skill
function: marketing-ops
owner: marketing-operations-manager
reads: [channels-and-tools, goals-and-kpis, icp-and-segments]
you_bring: Current UTM values or campaign names if any exist, each platform in use, and any limits on values.
also_for: [utm, messy utms, campaign naming, link tagging, tracking links, reports do not agree, cannot track leads, source is unknown]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Sets UTM and campaign naming rules and a tracking sheet format, so the numbers can be read later.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this once before running paid or email campaigns, and again when a channel is added or reports stop agreeing. Do not use it to read the resulting data, which belongs to [Read attribution](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/read-attribution.md), or to decide which metrics to display, which belongs to [Specify a dashboard](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/specify-dashboard.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: owned and paid channels, tools and hard rules on tracking and names.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: the attribution model and the campaigns planned this quarter.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment names, for the segment codes.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- Current UTM values or campaign names, as an export from the web analytics tool, if any exist.
- The name of each ad and social platform in use, for source values.
- Any limits a tool puts on parameter values.

## Procedure

1. List every channel from channels-and-tools.md, plus any the marketer adds, with one row each in the allowed values table.
2. Copy these format rules into the output as written: lowercase only, hyphens between words, no spaces or special characters, no personal data, no customer names, no competitor names, 40 characters at most per value. Link parameters can be seen by anyone who clicks. The 40-character cap is a starting rule the marketer can change.
3. Set the parameters. Source, medium and campaign are required on every link from outside the website. Source is the site or sender. Medium comes from the allowed list only. Campaign follows the pattern in step 5. Content is required when a campaign has more than one variant. Term is used for search ads only, and holds the keyword theme.
4. Build the allowed values table. Each channel gets one medium and one source. Use the marketer's platform names for source. If none was supplied, write [platform name needed]. A new value goes into the table before it is used on any link.
5. Set the campaign pattern: year and quarter, segment code, then topic, such as [yyyy]q[n]-[segment]-[topic]. Give each segment in icp-and-segments.md a short code, and use "all" for campaigns across segments. The same name goes in the CRM, the marketing automation tool and the ad account, so one campaign has one name everywhere.
6. Write the exceptions. Never tag links between pages on the company's own website, since that restarts the visit and overwrites its source. Do not tag links in organic search results. For print and event material, use a short link or code that redirects to a tagged link, with the medium for events.
7. Write the attribution requirement. If goals-and-kpis.md reports first and last touch, the marketer must confirm that the CRM stores both on each lead. Record this as unknown until confirmed.
8. Write the tracking sheet format with these columns in this order: date, campaign, channel, source, medium, content, term, destination page, full link, owner, tested (yes or no). Add one row per link. Set the rule: no link goes live until it has been clicked and the visit appears in the web analytics tool.
9. Map old values to new ones when the marketer supplied existing values. Old data keeps its old names, and the map is used for reading across the change.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: every channel has values, every segment has a code, and hard rules on ad spend and names are reflected. If a needed field is blank, leave a visible placeholder and ask in the last section. Never invent.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Format rules
<The rules from the procedure, as up to 8 bullets.>

## Allowed values
| Channel | Source | Medium |
<One row per channel.>

## Campaign names
<Pattern, segment codes and up to 3 real campaign names. Max 60 words.>

## Parameter rules
| Parameter | Required when | Value |
<One row each for source, medium, campaign, content and term.>

## Exceptions
<Up to 5 bullets, max 25 words each.>

## Tracking sheet format
| date | campaign | channel | source | medium | content | term | destination page | full link | owner | tested |
<One filled sample row, then the go-live rule in one sentence.>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every channel in channels-and-tools.md have allowed values?
- [ ] Does every segment have a code?
- [ ] Are source, medium and campaign required on every outside link?
- [ ] Are links between the company's own pages left untagged?
- [ ] Are customer and competitor names kept out of all values?
- [ ] Is there a sample row with a complete, working link format?
- [ ] Is there a go-live rule that requires a tested link?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Assumption: source values stand in for platform names, which CogniATion's context pack leaves out. The topic, page path, content and term values are illustrative.

**Format rules**

- Lowercase, hyphens between words, no spaces or special characters.
- No personal data, customer names or competitor names.
- 40 characters at most per value.

**Allowed values**

| Channel | Source | Medium |
|---|---|---|
| Search ads | search-engine | paid-search |
| Professional social network ads | pro-social | paid-social |
| Company social page | pro-social | organic-social |
| Newsletter | newsletter | email |
| Video channel | video-channel | organic-video |
| Regional conference | ld-conference | event |

**Campaign names**

Pattern: year and quarter, segment, topic. Codes: proserv, software, finserv. Examples: 2027q1-proserv-defend-the-memo, 2027q1-all-ld-conference.

**Parameter rules**

| Parameter | Required when | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Source, medium, campaign | Every outside link | As above |
| Content | More than one variant | Variant name |
| Term | Search ads only | Keyword theme |

**Exceptions**

- No tags on links between cogniation.example pages.
- Conference print material uses a short link that redirects to a tagged link, with medium event.

**Tracking sheet format**

Sample row: 2027-01-15, 2027q1-proserv-defend-the-memo, search ads, search-engine, paid-search, defend-answers-a, critical-thinking, /professional-services, cogniation.example/professional-services?utm_source=search-engine&utm_medium=paid-search&utm_campaign=2027q1-proserv-defend-the-memo&utm_content=defend-answers-a&utm_term=critical-thinking, marketer, no.

No ad spend until the link is tested, per the tracking limit in channels-and-tools.md.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Unknown: Does the CRM store first and last touch on each lead?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Set tracking conventions". Read the context pack.
Task: <which channels, and any limits a tool puts on values>.
<paste current values and platform names here>
```


===== FILE: skills/marketing-ops/update-context-pack.md =====

---
title: Update the context pack
type: skill
function: marketing-ops
owner: marketing-operations-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: Your notes, such as a sales call, a customer email or a price change, with the date and who said it.
also_for: [update my context pack, a fact changed, new proof point, new objection, add this to the pack, record this, keep the pack current, customer said yes, price changed]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Turns pasted notes into the exact changes to make in your context pack, showing only what changes and never marking anything approved on its own.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when a note, call or email changes a fact your skills rely on. Do not use it to fill a blank pack from scratch; use [Fill the context pack](../../guides/fill-the-context-pack.md) instead. To find what has gone stale without new notes, run the monthly check in [Maintain and update](../../guides/maintain-and-update.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: company facts, and which numbers may be used where.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what it does and is not, prices, roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, fit and disqualifiers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: buyers, their problems and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: claims, pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: competitor facts, with sources and dates.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, numbers and quotes, with permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice rules.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, baselines and definitions.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, tools, limits and approvers.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- The notes, with the date and who said each thing.
- Any permission a customer or the CEO has given, in their words if possible.

## Procedure

1. Read the notes and list each separate fact they state. If a note has no date or no source, write [date needed] or [source needed] for it and ask in the last section.
2. Sort each fact. A company fact goes on in step 3. A task, deadline or decision goes under "Not added", with the workboard named as its place. An opinion from one person stays an opinion: record it with its source, or mark it "(founder belief)" if a founder said it about buyers, the market, competitors or results.
3. Find the section and field each fact belongs in, using the templates' field names. If no field fits, put it under "Not added" and say why.
4. Compare the fact with the current answer in that field. Mark it new, changed or the same. Drop anything that is the same.
5. A newer fact from the person who owns it, such as a price from whoever approves pricing, is a change. Name that person as the source. For any other contradiction, do not choose. Put both under "Conflicts to settle", with a question and your recommendation.
6. Set permission only from what the notes say. A customer's written yes, with its date, can move an item to the status they agreed to. A verbal yes is "pending" until it is in writing. No word on permission means "none". Being pleased, being quoted in sales notes, or appearing in an export is not permission.
7. Do not change a goal, target, MQL or SQL definition, or budget. Put each one under "Conflicts to settle" as a decision for the marketer, since these belong to the marketer and whoever approves them.
8. Write each change as it should read in the file: section, field name, and the full new "Answer:" line, with its source and date. Keep the rest of the answer word for word. Do not reword what you are not changing.
9. Do not return the whole context pack. A retyped pack can change facts without anyone noticing, so return only the changed fields.
10. Name which skills read each changed section, up to 3, so the marketer knows what to run again.
11. Check every change against the context pack once more: no new claim beyond the notes, no permission raised without a source, no field name altered.
12. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Notes from: <dates and sources, up to 20 words>

## Changes to make
| Section | Field | Now | Change to | Source and date | New or changed |
<one row per field. "Now" up to 15 words; write "Blank" if empty>

## Paste these lines
<For each change: the section name, the field name and the full new "Answer:" line, in a code block.>
Paste these into my-context-pack.md, save it, upload the new file and delete the old copy. This chat changes no file.

## Conflicts to settle
| Field | The pack says | The notes say | Question and recommendation |
<one row per conflict or reserved decision, or "None". Question and recommendation up to 25 words.>

## Not added
- <item, reason, and where it belongs instead, up to 20 words each, or "None">

## Skills to run again
- <changed section: up to 3 skill titles, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every change name its source and date?
- [ ] Were only changed fields returned, with no retyped pack?
- [ ] Is every permission status backed by what the notes say?
- [ ] Are contradictions and reserved decisions left for the marketer?
- [ ] Is one person's opinion recorded as an opinion, not a fact?
- [ ] Do the field names match the templates exactly?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Notes from: Tom Ferrant, January 29, 2027; Haldane Ridge Capital email, February 2, 2027.

Assumption: the notes below are invented for this example. Tom Ferrant said a managing partner asked about individual scores. He also thinks the price is too high. Haldane Ridge Capital's head of learning wrote that the firm's name may be used in sales material.

**Changes to make**

| Section | Field | Now | Change to | Source and date | New or changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| personas.md | Persona 3, objections | "Why not just tell people to check the AI's work?" | Add "Can I see how each associate scored?" | Tom Ferrant, January 29, 2027 | New |
| proof-points.md | Customer 3, permission | Pending, requested October 2026 | Sales only | Haldane Ridge Capital email, February 2, 2027 | Changed |

**Paste these lines**

```
personas.md, Persona 3, Objections
Answer: "Why not just tell people to check the AI's work?"; "Can I see how each associate scored?" (Tom Ferrant, first call, January 29, 2027)
```

**Conflicts to settle:** None.

**Not added**

- Price is too high: one opinion. Keep the note, and watch for it in win-loss.

**Skills to run again:** personas.md: Write a web page.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Unknown: does Haldane Ridge Capital's yes cover a quote or a result as well as the name?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Update the context pack". Read the context pack.
Task: update it from these notes.
<paste your notes here, with the date and who said each thing>
```


===== FILE: skills/paid-demand-gen/adjust-paid-campaigns.md =====

---
title: Adjust paid campaigns
type: skill
function: paid-demand-gen
owner: paid-media-manager
reads: [messaging, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: An ad export for the four weeks ending seven days ago and for the last seven days, paid's budget and expected SQLs, and the paid plan's stop rules.
also_for: [ads not working, cost per lead too high, should i pause this ad, paid search results, weekly ad review, wasting ad budget, cut ad spend, junk leads from ads]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Turns a pasted performance export into weekly keep, cut or change decisions for each campaign line.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this once a week while paid campaigns are running, with a current export in hand. Do not use it to judge a finished campaign, which belongs in [Evaluate a campaign](../analytics-and-campaign-evaluation/evaluate-campaign.md). If no plan exists yet, use [Plan a paid campaign](plan-paid-campaign.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:messaging]]: words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: MQL and SQL definitions, conversion rates.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: monthly budget per channel, limits, approval thresholds.

The marketer supplies: a performance export with spend and conversions per line, split into the four weeks ending seven days ago and the last seven days; paid's budget and expected SQLs; and the paid plan's target cost and stop rules. Better still, add CRM MQLs and SQLs per line.

## Procedure

1. Check the export: both date ranges, spend, conversions, no repeated lines, and totals that match the lines. If something is missing or wrong, say what to export again. Judge lines on the rolling four weeks, leaving out the last seven days, or the MQL to SQL time in goals-and-kpis.md when judging on SQLs. Use the last seven days only for rule 1 and pace.
2. Get the target cost. Use the target from the paid plan. If there is none, estimate: paid's own budget divided by the SQLs expected from paid is the cost per SQL, and that times the MQL to SQL rate is the cost per MQL. Show the arithmetic. If expected SQLs are unknown, write [expected SQLs needed] and apply only rules 1 and 6.
3. Choose the conversion to judge. Use SQLs per line if pasted, against the cost per SQL. Otherwise use MQLs, as goals-and-kpis.md defines them. Ad-tool conversions are not MQLs: judge them only against a plan target for that event, or with evidence of how many become MQLs. Without either, write [target for this conversion needed], apply only rules 1 and 6, and report tracking and spend.
4. Compute for each line: spend, conversions and cost per conversion. Use only the marketer's numbers.
5. Decide for each line, using the first rule below that matches. If the paid plan has its own stop rules, its numbers replace these multiples. These numbers and windows are starting rules, not benchmarks.

   | Rule | Condition | Decision |
   |---|---|---|
   | 1 | Tracking looks broken: clicks but no conversion data, or a sudden drop to zero | Change: fix tracking, and pause if channels-and-tools.md forbids spend without it |
   | 2 | Spend below the target cost | Keep: too little data |
   | 3 | Spend at 3 times the target cost or more, no conversions | Cut |
   | 4 | Cost per conversion above 1.5 times the target, at least 5 conversions | Change one thing |
   | 5 | Cost per conversion at or below the target | Keep |
   | 6 | Anything else | Keep: watch next week |

6. For each change, name one thing: audience, search terms, ad copy, destination page, bid, or tracking under rule 1. Pick the likely cause from the export. If the export cannot show it, propose the cheapest to test, and label that an assumption. Do not change a line changed in the last two weeks unless rule 3 applies.
7. Move cut money to the Keep line with the lowest cost per conversion, up to its plan limit. Otherwise report it as unspent. Keep totals within the monthly budget in channels-and-tools.md, and flag anything needing approval.
8. Compare spend to date with the monthly budget per channel and say whether it is on pace, over or under.
9. Write the steps the marketer takes in the ad tool, in order. Say nothing as if it were already done.
10. Check the decisions against the context pack: limits, forbidden ad types, words to avoid, budget, approval thresholds.
11. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Window judged: <dates, and the gap left out> | Judged on: <SQLs, MQLs or ad-tool conversions>

## Decisions this week
| Line | Spend | Conversions | Cost per conversion | Decision | Rule | Reason, up to 15 words |

## Changes to make
| Line | One change | What to do in the ad tool | Check in two weeks |

## Budget
Moved: <from, to, amount>
Pace: <channel, spend to date, monthly budget, on pace or over or under>
Approval needed: <approver, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every line in the export have exactly one decision and the rule behind it?
- [ ] Were lines judged on the rolling four weeks, and on SQLs when pasted?
- [ ] Do all the numbers trace back to the pasted export or show their arithmetic?
- [ ] Is the target cost from the paid plan or a labeled estimate, for the same conversion judged?
- [ ] Is each change limited to one thing per line?
- [ ] Do lines with too little data stay at Keep instead of being judged?
- [ ] Do the totals stay inside the monthly budget, with approvals flagged?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Window judged: December 21 to January 17, leaving out January 18 to 24. Judged on: MQLs.

**Decisions this week**

| Line | Spend | Conversions | Cost per conversion | Decision | Rule | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Search, critical thinking terms | $3,780 | 21 | $180 | Keep | 5 | Below the $234 target |
| Search, general AI training terms | $820 | 0 | None | Cut | 3 | Over $702 with no MQLs |
| Social, heads of learning | $3,600 | 8 | $450 | Change | 4 | Above the $351 limit |

**Changes to make**

| Line | One change | What to do in the ad tool | Check in two weeks |
|---|---|---|---|
| Social, heads of learning | Audience | Limit to professional services firms | Cost per MQL under $351 |
| Search, general terms | Pause | Pause the line | No further spend |

**Budget.** Moved: about $205 a week from general terms to critical thinking terms. Pace, last seven days: search $1,150 of $5,000 a month, social $900 of $4,000, both on pace. Approval needed: None.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the export figures and the 30 SQLs expected from paid are invented. No SQLs were pasted, so conversions are MQLs.
- Estimate: $234 is paid's $27,000 for the quarter divided by 30 SQLs, times the 26% MQL to SQL rate. $702 is three times that, $351 is 1.5 times.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Adjust paid campaigns". Read the context pack.
Task: <paid's budget and expected SQLs, and the plan's target cost and stop rules>.
<paste the export here, with CRM MQLs and SQLs if you have them>
```


===== FILE: skills/paid-demand-gen/plan-paid-campaign.md =====

---
title: Plan a paid campaign
type: skill
function: paid-demand-gen
owner: paid-media-manager
reads: [icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The goal, dates, budget, the campaign brief if there is one, and any past results or keyword figures.
also_for: [search ads plan, start running ads, ad budget, which audience to target, need more demo requests, paid social, paid campaign, stop rules]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Sets the channel, audience, budget split, tests and stop rules for one paid campaign.
judge: ["Is this audience looking for a solution right now or will you be interrupting them?", "Is the money enough to reach a result you can read before it runs out?", "If the first test fails what will you have learned for the money?", "Is the page after the click ready for someone who has never heard of you?"]
show_to: someone who has run paid ads at a similar budget, and ask which test they would drop
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before spending on a paid campaign, when you know the goal and the budget. Do not use it to plan a campaign across all channels, which belongs in [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md). Use [Allocate the budget](../budget-and-planning/allocate-budget.md) instead when the question is how much paid should get at all.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment, region, company size, disqualifiers, triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who to target and the job titles to use.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars to test as angles.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, budget, conversion rates, metric definitions.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: current paid channels and spend, limits, approval thresholds.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the goal, dates, budget, and the campaign brief if there is one. Paste past results and any keyword or audience-size figures. Without them, those fields stay unknown.

## Procedure

1. Check the gates first. List each limit in channels-and-tools.md as met, not met or unknown. If any is not met or unknown, say in the Gates section that no spend should start until it is met.
2. Set the target cost per MQL. Use the marketer's number if given. Otherwise work it out as an estimate: paid's own budget divided by the SQLs expected from paid, multiplied by the MQL to SQL rate. Show the arithmetic. If any input is blank, write [target cost needed] and ask.
3. Choose channels. Use only the current paid channels in channels-and-tools.md, unless the marketer adds one. Exclude any type of ad that file forbids.
4. Define the audience per channel. For search ads: buying problems and terms from the persona's language, with disqualifier terms as exclusions. Treat volume as unknown unless pasted. For audience-targeted ads: job title from personas.md, industry, company size and region from icp-and-segments.md. Skip segments the priority order limits to other routes.
5. Split the budget. If channels-and-tools.md shows current spend, keep that ratio, so results can be compared with history. If not, give 70% to the channel that reaches people searching for a solution and 30% to the targeted channel. Hold back 10% of each line for tests.
6. Divide each line's budget by the target cost. That is the number of MQLs the line needs, an estimate and not a forecast. If a line needs fewer than 3, drop it and move its money to the other line.
7. Plan up to 2 tests. Each changes one thing and names the winner rule: the lower cost per MQL, once each option has at least 5 MQLs. If the budget cannot reach that, run one test only and say so.
8. Set the stop rules. Line: pause when spend reaches 3 times the target cost with no MQL. Campaign: review when cost per MQL stays above 2 times the target for two weeks in a row. These multiples, and the 70/30 split, 10%, 3-MQL and 5-MQL figures above, are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks. They feed [Adjust paid campaigns](adjust-paid-campaigns.md).
9. List what must exist before launch: ad sets from [Write ad variations](write-ad-variations.md), landing page, tracking links, and any approval over the limits in channels-and-tools.md with its typical time.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: channel limits, disqualifiers, budget total, approval thresholds, definitions. Fix what contradicts it.
11. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Gates
| Limit | Met, not met or unknown | What to do |

## Campaign summary
Goal: <one goal, with number and date>
Dates: <start to end>
Target cost per MQL: <number and arithmetic>

## Channels and audience
| Channel | Audience, up to 25 words | Exclusions |

## Budget split
| Line | Total | Per month | Test money | MQLs needed |

## Tests
| Test | What changes | Winner rule |

## Stop rules
<Up to 3 rules, each with a number>

## Needed before launch
| Item | Skill or owner | Approver and typical time |

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the plan say clearly whether spend can start today?
- [ ] Does the budget split add up to the budget the marketer gave?
- [ ] Is the target cost the marketer's number, or an estimate with its arithmetic?
- [ ] Is every channel in channels-and-tools.md, with forbidden ad types excluded?
- [ ] Does each test change one thing and have a winner rule?
- [ ] Does each stop rule have a number?
- [ ] Are search volume and audience size marked unknown unless the marketer pasted them?
- [ ] Are approvals over the limit listed with time to get them?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Gates.** No ad spend without tracking in place: unknown, so confirm before February 15. No display retargeting: met.

**Campaign summary.** Goal: 58 MQLs from professional services firms by March 31, 2027. Dates: February 15 to March 31, 2027. Target cost per MQL: $234.

**Channels and audience**

| Channel | Audience | Exclusions |
|---|---|---|
| Search ads | Terms on critical thinking training and checking AI work | Course library, performance review terms |
| Professional social network ads | Heads of learning and VPs of people, consulting, accounting and law, 300 to 5,000 employees, United States, Canada, United Kingdom | Software companies |

**Budget split**

| Line | Total | Per month | Test money | MQLs needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Search ads | $7,500 | $5,000 | $750 | 32 |
| Social ads | $6,000 | $4,000 | $600 | 26 |

**Tests.** One test only, given the budget: client memo angle against keep-practicing angle. Winner: lower cost per MQL at 5 MQLs each.

**Stop rules.** Line: pause at $702 with no MQL. Campaign: review after two weeks above $468 per MQL.

**Needed before launch.** Ad sets, landing page, tracking links. Search at $7,500 and social at $6,000 in total: Marta Ilves, 3 days.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all current paid spend moves to this campaign for 1.5 months.
- Assumption: a line's campaign total counts as one item for the $5,000 limit.
- Estimate: $234 is paid's $27,000 for the quarter divided by 30 expected SQLs (illustrative), times the 26% MQL to SQL rate. 58 is $13,500 divided by $234, rounded, part of the 116 in the campaign brief.
- Unknown: search volume for these terms. Can you paste an export?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan a paid campaign". Read the context pack.
Task: <the goal, dates and budget>.
<paste the brief, past results and keyword or audience figures here>
```


===== FILE: skills/paid-demand-gen/write-ad-variations.md =====

---
title: Write ad variations
type: skill
function: paid-demand-gen
owner: paid-media-manager
reads: [personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The channel, the audience, the destination page, and the character limit for every ad field.
also_for: [ad copy, ad headlines, search ad text, ads to test, new ad creative, social ad copy, ad fatigue, demo ad]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Writes ad copy sets by angle, within the character limits the marketer supplies.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when a paid plan names the channel, audience and angles and you need copy to test. Do not use it for organic posts, which belong in [Write social posts](../content/write-social-posts.md). If channel, audience and budget are still open, use [Plan a paid campaign](plan-paid-campaign.md) first instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:personas]]: the audience's language, daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars as angles, messages by persona, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: numbers and quotes, and whether they may appear in public.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, do and do-not rules, style details.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: hard rules on ads, and who approves what.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the channel, the audience, the destination page, and the character limit for every ad field on that channel. Optional: the number of angles, and past ads with their results.

## Procedure

1. Get the character limits. If the marketer did not give them, ask and stop. If they want a draft anyway, write to [limit needed] and mark every length as unchecked. Do not use limits from memory.
2. Choose angles. Use the angles from the marketer or the paid plan. Otherwise, take one angle from each pillar in messaging.md, plus one from the persona's daily problem, up to 4 in total. Drop a pillar that has no public proof and no plain benefit to state.
3. Write 2 variations per angle. The two must differ in one thing only, such as the opening claim or the call to action, so a result can be read. Fill every field the marketer listed.
4. Apply the claim rules. Numbers only from proof-points.md with Public status, worded as that file words them, without dropping what the number measures. Customer names only with Public status. Do not name a competitor if channels-and-tools.md forbids it, even if the positioning statement names one. No internal numbers, no roadmap, no words from the avoid list.
5. Mention price only if the marketer asks. If an ad mentions price, list the pricing approver from channels-and-tools.md in the Approvals section.
6. Count characters for each field, including spaces and punctuation, and write the count next to it. Rewrite anything over the limit. Never cut a word in a way that changes the claim. Tell the marketer to confirm the counts in their ad tool.
7. Match each ad to the destination page. If the page does not carry the claim in the ad, flag it. If the destination is unknown, write [destination page needed].
8. Check the draft against the context pack: proof status, competitor rule, words to avoid, voice rules, spelling of the product name. Fix what contradicts it.
9. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels. Say which angle you would test first and why, labeled as an inference.

## Output format

Repeat the table for each angle.

```
Status: Draft

## Ad set summary
Channel: <one>
Audience: <up to 20 words>
Destination: <page>
Limits: <field and character limit, as supplied>

## Angle <number>: <angle name>
Source: <pillar or persona problem>
Proof: <item and permission status, or "None">
| Variation | Field | Copy | Characters |

## Approvals
<Approver and typical time, or "None beyond the marketer">

## Test order
<Angles in test order, one line each>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Are the character limits the ones the marketer supplied?
- [ ] Is every field within its limit, with the count shown?
- [ ] Does each pair of variations differ in only one thing?
- [ ] Is every number or customer name Public in proof-points.md and worded as written there?
- [ ] Are competitors left out if channels-and-tools.md forbids naming them?
- [ ] Is the copy free of the words to avoid and the do-not rules in brand-voice.md?
- [ ] Does the destination page back up each claim?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Ad set summary.** Channel: search ads. Audience: heads of learning at professional services firms. Destination: professional services landing page. Limits: headline 30, description 90.

**Angle 1: See reasoning improve.** Source: pillar 3. Proof: None.

| Variation | Field | Copy | Characters |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Headline | Can your analysts defend it? | 28 |
| A | Description | CogniATion Coach reports progress to you, with no individual's conversation on display. | 87 |

**Angle 2: Finished is not changed.** Source: head of learning's daily problem. Proof: None.

| Variation | Field | Copy | Characters |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Headline | Did the training change work? | 29 |
| A | Description | Show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it. | 77 |

**Angle 3: People keep practicing.** Source: pillar 2. Proof: 72% weekly session result, Public.

| Variation | Field | Copy | Characters |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Headline | Practice people keep doing | 26 |
| A | Description | 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week in Q3 2026. | 77 |

**Approvals.** None beyond the marketer.

**Test order.** Angle 1 against 3, then 2.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: variation B is cut here to save space.
- Assumption: the marketer supplied these limits.
- Inference: angle 1 first, because the destination page cites client memos and analysts.
- Inference: the 22% Brightmere Advisory result stays on the landing page, because 90 characters cannot hold what it measures.
- Unknown: is the landing page live?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write ad variations". Read the context pack.
Task: <the channel, audience, destination page, and character limit for every ad field>.
```


===== FILE: skills/partner-and-channel-marketing/build-partner-kit.md =====

---
title: Build a partner kit
type: skill
function: partner-and-channel-marketing
owner: partner-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The partner's type and name, whether it refers or sells, who receives referrals, and any commercial terms to include.
also_for: [reseller materials, referral program, partner enablement, channel partner, partner leads, partners selling it wrong, reseller pitch, agency partner kit]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Builds the messages and materials a partner needs to refer or sell the product accurately.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when a partner will refer or sell your product and needs words they can use without calling you. Do not use it for a single joint campaign, which belongs in [Plan co-marketing with a partner](plan-co-marketing.md). For a page your own sales team hands to a buyer, use [Write a sales one-pager](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-sales-one-pager.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: the public name and the numbers that may be published.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what it does, what it is not, pricing, and roadmap limits.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: who to send over, disqualifiers, triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: messages and objections by role.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: one-sentence description, pillars, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: proof the partner may repeat.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice for the copy, logo rules.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the partner's type and name, whether it refers or sells, who receives referrals, and any commercial terms to include. If the context pack holds nothing about partners, these facts come from the marketer or stay as placeholders.

## Procedure

1. Set the kit type from the partner's role. Refer: the partner makes an introduction and leaves the sale to you. Sell: the partner quotes and closes. If the role is not given, ask. A Sell kit adds pricing. A Refer kit leaves it out unless the marketer asks.
2. Write who to send over, as signs a partner can spot in conversation: company type, size, situation and triggers from icp-and-segments.md. Write who not to send over from the disqualifiers and what the product is not. Reword any term on the avoid list.
3. Write the short description: 30 words at most, built from the one-sentence description and one pillar.
4. Write three fit questions for the partner to ask, each based on a trigger or a daily problem from personas.md.
5. Copy the message from messaging.md for each role the partner will meet.
6. Write up to three objections and answers, from personas.md. Answers use only features in product.md and Public proof.
7. List proof the partner may repeat: Public items and publishable company numbers only. A partner passes material along outside any one-to-one sale, so leave out Sales only items. If nothing qualifies, write [public proof needed].
8. Write the do-not-say list: what the product is not, integrations it does not have, roadmap items, internal numbers, and any claim about competitors. Competitor questions go to you, not the partner.
9. Write the referral steps: what the partner sends, to whom, what happens next. If unknown, write [referral contact needed] or [response time needed].
10. Write the ready-to-send text in brand voice: one introduction email and one social post. Leave price out unless the marketer asks and the approver is listed.
11. List the naming and logo rules from brand-voice.md, and the approvals the kit needs from channels-and-tools.md.
12. Check the kit against the context pack: permission status, avoid list, roadmap, what the product is not, hard rules. Fix what contradicts it.
13. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels.

## Output format

Everything above the last section goes to the partner. The last section stays with the marketer.

```
Status: Draft

## Kit type
<Refer or sell, and the partner>

## Short description
<Up to 30 words>

## Who to send over
Yes: <up to 4 signs>
No: <up to 4 signs>
Questions to ask: <3>

## Messages by role
| Role | Message, up to 20 words |

## Objections
| Objection | Answer, up to 30 words |

## Proof you may repeat
<Item, source date. Up to 5>

## Do not say
<Up to 6 items>

## How to refer
<Up to 4 numbered steps>

## Ready-to-send text
Introduction email: <up to 120 words>
Social post: <up to 60 words>

## Name and logo rules
<Up to 4 rules>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Could a partner decide who to send over without calling you?
- [ ] Is every proof item Public, with no Sales only or Internal only items?
- [ ] Are all numbers publishable in company.md or Public in proof-points.md?
- [ ] Does the do-not-say list cover what the product is not and the roadmap?
- [ ] Are competitor claims left out?
- [ ] Are the email and post within their word limits and in brand voice?
- [ ] Are the referral contact and response time filled in or visibly marked?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Kit type.** Refer. Partner: [partner name needed], an AI rollout consultancy.

**Short description.** CogniATion Coach is an AI chatbot that won't give you the answer, so you learn to find it. Learning leaders see progress by team.

**Who to send over.** Yes: knowledge-work companies, 300 to 5,000 employees, at least 100 people to train, after an AI assistant rollout or a client complaint about poor reasoning. No: fewer than 100 learners, wants a course library, wants scores on individuals for performance reviews. Questions to ask: Do junior staff check what the AI assistant says? Has a client complained about poor reasoning? Who owns learning and development?

**Messages by role.** Head of learning: show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it.

**Objections.** "Will people feel they are being watched?" Learning leaders see progress by team and never see an individual's conversation.

**Proof you may repeat.** 64 customer companies as of September 30, 2026. Median of 9 days from signed contract to first live session, 31 rollouts, January to September 2026. Lena Marsh quote, Brightmere Advisory.

**Do not say.** Not a general AI assistant, course library or learning management system. Not for hiring or performance decisions. No claim about raising intelligence. No future features. No competitor claims.

**How to refer.** Email [referral contact needed]. CogniATion replies in [response time needed].

**Ready-to-send text.** Email: Your junior staff have answers from the AI assistant. Can they defend them? CogniATion Coach makes people practice reasoning on real work problems. Want an introduction?

**Name and logo rules.** CogniATion, spelled exactly that way. The Coach on second mention. Logo on plain backgrounds only.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the partner refers and is a consultancy.
- Unknown: who at CogniATion receives referrals?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Build a partner kit". Read the context pack.
Task: <the partner's type and name, whether it refers or sells, who receives referrals, and any terms>.
```


===== FILE: skills/partner-and-channel-marketing/plan-co-marketing.md =====

---
title: Plan co-marketing with a partner
type: skill
function: partner-and-channel-marketing
owner: partner-marketing-manager
reads: [product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The partner's name, its audience, what it can contribute, each side's budget, and the rules on sharing leads.
also_for: [joint webinar, joint campaign, co-branded content, partner campaign, share leads with a partner, partner event, pipeline through partners, cross promotion]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Plans a joint campaign with one partner, including the split of work, lead handling and approvals.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when one named partner has agreed to discuss a joint campaign and you need a plan to take to them. Do not use it for an ongoing referral program, which belongs in [Build a partner kit](build-partner-kit.md). For a campaign you run alone, use [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product is not, and integrations that exist, so nothing joint is overstated.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments and disqualifiers to test the partner's audience against.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who the campaign speaks to, and where they get information.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: proof and its permission status.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals, MQL definition, conversion rates.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels, tools, approvers and typical times.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the partner's name, what it sells, its audience, what it can contribute, any agreed terms, each side's budget, and the rules on sharing leads. If the context pack holds nothing about the partner, these facts come from the marketer or stay unknown.

## Procedure

1. Test the fit and give a verdict. Go: the partner's audience overlaps a priority segment, it does not sell what the product does, and it can bring one channel the company lacks. Fix first: one test is unknown. No-go: the partner competes with the product or its audience is mostly disqualified.
2. If no-go, stop and give the reason. If fix first, name the question that settles it and plan on the labeled assumption.
3. Write one joint goal using the MQL definition from goals-and-kpis.md, with a date. Record a different partner goal alongside it, without merging the two.
4. Choose one joint offer that neither side could make alone, such as a webinar, guide or event session. Do not imply an integration or joint product unless product.md lists it.
5. Write the split of work. Each task has one owner, one due date and one cost owner. If the marketer's capacity is unknown, write [capacity needed].
6. Write the lead rules: who collects the form, what consent wording appears, who may contact a lead and when, and how the other side learns the result. If no consent wording was given, write [consent wording needed, check with counsel]. Leads go to both sides only if the form says so.
7. Set the overlap rule for a lead already open in either side's CRM. Default: the existing owner keeps it, labeled as an assumption until both sides agree.
8. List approvals on both sides, using channels-and-tools.md and [partner approver needed]. Each side approves its own name, logo and claims. Use only Public proof in joint material.
9. Build the timeline backward from the start date, leaving room for the longest approval on each side.
10. Set targets with arithmetic shown, using rates from goals-and-kpis.md. If the partner's audience size is unknown, write [audience size needed] and give no registration target.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: what the product is not, disqualifiers, proof status, words to avoid, internal numbers. Fix what contradicts it.
12. List everything assumed, estimated, inferred or unknown in the last section, using those labels.

## Output format

The plan is written to be shared with the partner, so it holds no internal numbers.

```
Status: Draft

## Verdict
<Go, fix first or no-go, with the reason, up to 30 words>

## Joint campaign
Partner: <name, what it sells>
Goal: <one goal, with number and date>
Audience: <segment, persona>
Offer: <up to 25 words>

## Split of work
| Task | Owner | Due | Who pays |

## Lead rules
Collects: <side and form>
Consent wording: <text or placeholder>
Follow-up: <who, how soon>
Overlap: <rule>
Reporting back: <what, when>

## Approvals
| Item | Our approver and time | Partner approver and time |

## Timeline
| Date | Milestone |

## Targets
| Metric | Target | How worked out |

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the verdict rest on the partner's audience and product, not on enthusiasm?
- [ ] Does every task have one owner, one due date and one cost owner?
- [ ] Are lead collection, consent, follow-up and overlap all written down?
- [ ] Do both sides appear in the approvals table?
- [ ] Does the plan avoid implying an integration that product.md does not list?
- [ ] Is every fact about the partner from the marketer, or labeled?
- [ ] Does joint material use only Public proof and no internal numbers?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Verdict.** Fix first. Audience overlap with professional services firms is unconfirmed.

**Joint campaign.** Partner: [partner name needed], a learning and development association that sells memberships and events. Goal: [MQL target needed] by March 31, 2027. Audience: professional services firms, head of learning and development. Offer: joint webinar on showing that training changed how people reason.

**Split of work**

| Task | Owner | Due | Who pays |
|---|---|---|---|
| Webinar setup and registration | CogniATion | [date needed] | CogniATion |
| Invite to association members | Partner | [date needed] | Partner |
| Follow-up to registrants | CogniATion | [date needed] | CogniATion |

**Lead rules.** Collects: CogniATion, on its webinar tool. Consent wording: [consent wording needed, check with counsel]. Follow-up: CogniATion, [response time needed]. Overlap: existing owner keeps the lead. Reporting back: registrant and SQL counts after the webinar.

**Approvals**

| Item | Our approver and time | Partner approver and time |
|---|---|---|
| Lena Marsh quote | Already Public | Not needed |
| Partner name and logo | [approver needed] | [partner approver needed] |

**Timeline.** [dates needed, once the partner confirms].

**Targets.** Webinar attendees who fit the ICP count as MQLs per goals-and-kpis.md. Registration target: [audience size needed].

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the partner is an association. No partner is named in the context pack.
- Inference: heads of learning would listen, because personas.md lists learning and development associations as an information source.
- Assumption: the existing lead owner keeps overlaps, until both sides agree.
- Unknown: how many of the association's members work at professional services firms?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Plan co-marketing with a partner". Read the context pack.
Task: <the partner, what it sells, its audience and contribution, each side's budget, and lead-sharing rules>.
```


===== FILE: skills/positioning-and-messaging/build-messaging-framework.md =====

---
title: Build a messaging framework
type: skill
function: positioning-and-messaging
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: A settled positioning statement, plus any new proof with its permission status.
also_for: [key messages, value proposition, what do we say, sales tells a different story, website messaging, message house, elevator pitch, value props]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Turns a positioning statement into pillars, proof per pillar, messages by persona and words to use and avoid.
judge: ["If a prospect read only the first pillar would they know why to keep reading?", "Would your sales team say these words on a call or rewrite them first?", "Does each persona message answer what that person worries about or just restate the product?", "Which pillar would you drop first if you could only say two things?"]
show_to: a salesperson who ran a call this week, and ask which message they would never say out loud
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this once the positioning statement is settled, or when pillars have drifted from what sales says. Do not use it without a statement; run [Write a positioning statement](write-positioning-statement.md) first. For finished page copy, use [Write a web page](../content/write-web-page.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:messaging]] – statement, current pillars, words to use and avoid
- [[context-pack:product]] – features, what it is not, known weak spots
- [[context-pack:personas]] – roles, what each is measured on, objections
- [[context-pack:competitors]] – dated facts, where each wins and loses
- [[context-pack:proof-points]] – evidence and permission status
- [[context-pack:company]] – which company numbers may be published
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]] – voice traits and style rules
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]] – hard rules on competitor names

The marketer supplies: a new positioning statement, if it differs from messaging.md, and any new proof with its permission status.

## Procedure

1. Read the positioning statement. If it is blank and none is pasted, stop and ask for it, or point to the skill "Write a positioning statement".
2. Split the statement into separate claims: the lead value, the reason to believe and each difference. Add the current pillars from messaging.md. Keep at most 6 candidates.
3. Keep a candidate only if all four are true: product.md supports it without crossing "What it is not" or "Known weak spots", it reaches what at least one persona is measured on, at least one alternative cannot say it as well, and it can be stated in 8 words or fewer.
4. Rank what is left by the number of personas it reaches, then by its best proof, in this order: Public, Sales only, Internal only, none. On a tie, keep messaging.md order (a starting rule). Take the top three as pillars. If only two survive, write two. Never pad.
5. For each pillar, write why it differs: the alternative it beats and the dated fact or field behind that. If it beats only the status quo, say so.
6. For each pillar, list proof from proof-points.md and publishable numbers from company.md, each with its permission status. Exclude Pending and None. Proof that appears only in messaging.md has no permission status, so replace it with a placeholder such as [proof permission needed] and add it to the unknowns.
7. Mark each pillar for public use. Ready means it has at least one Public proof item. Otherwise write "Not ready" and the reason.
8. For each persona in personas.md, in file order, write one message of 20 words at most that uses their words and speaks to what they are measured on. Reuse the current line if it still passes step 3.
9. For each persona, take the first objection and answer it in 25 words at most, using only product facts and permitted proof. If the context pack has no answer, write [answer needed].
10. Copy "Words to use" and "Words to avoid" unchanged. Propose up to 3 additions to each, with a reason: use words come from persona quotes, avoid words come from what the product is not.
11. Keep competitor names out of the one-sentence description, pillars and persona messages, because that copy may reach ads. Names belong only in "Why it differs".
12. Rewrite pillars and messages to match the voice traits and rules in brand-voice.md.
13. Check the draft against the context pack: no contradiction with product.md, no word from "words to avoid", no internal number, no roadmap item, every proof status correct. Fix what fails.
14. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not find in the last section, each with its label.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Core
Positioning statement: <copied unchanged>
One-sentence description: <15 words at most, no competitor names>

## Pillars
Pillar 1: <8 words at most>
Why it differs: <25 words at most>
Proof: <item, source file, permission status; 3 items at most>
Public use: <Ready, or Not ready and why>
(Repeat for pillars 2 and 3. Three pillars at most.)

## Messages by persona
For each persona, in file order:
<Persona>: <message, 20 words at most>
Objection: <first objection from the persona file>
Answer: <25 words at most, or [answer needed]>

## Words to use
<Current list>. Proposed: <up to 3, with reason>

## Words to avoid
<Current list>. Proposed: <up to 3, with reason>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does each pillar trace back to the positioning statement?
- [ ] Does each pillar have proof with a permission status, or a visible gap?
- [ ] Is any pillar marked "Ready" without Public proof?
- [ ] Does every persona in the context pack have one message?
- [ ] Are the objection answers built from product facts, with gaps left as placeholders?
- [ ] Are competitor names limited to "Why it differs"?
- [ ] Is every message free of "words to avoid", internal numbers and roadmap items?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Core**

One-sentence description: CogniATion Coach won't give you the answer, so you learn to find it.

**Pillars**

Pillar 1: It asks, it does not answer. Why it differs: beats the status quo only, telling staff to check what the AI assistant says. Proof: Lena Marsh quote, proof-points.md, Public. Public use: Ready.

Pillar 2: People keep practicing. Why it differs: beats the status quo only, where earlier tools went unused. Proof: 72% of assigned learners practiced at least weekly, Q3 2026, proof-points.md, Public. Public use: Ready.

Pillar 3: You can see reasoning improve. Why it differs: three prospects described Elenchly's reports as completion data and quiz scores (calls, 2026). Proof: Brightmere Advisory's 22% rise in client memo reasoning scores, proof-points.md, Public. Public use: Ready.

**Messages by persona**

Head of learning: show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it. Objection: "People won't make time for another training tool." Answer: sessions run about 15 minutes, and 72% of assigned learners practiced at least weekly in Q3 2026.

VP of people: build judgment in junior staff, with no individual's conversation ever on display. Objection: "Is this another AI tool I have to defend to legal?" Answer: not a psychometric test, not for hiring or performance decisions. Leaders never see an individual's conversation.

**Words to use**

Reasoning, judgment, assumptions, practice, scenario. Proposed: none.

**Words to avoid**

Current list, unchanged. Proposed: none.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: messaging.md is current. Condensed: positioning statement and two personas left out.
- Assumption: description trimmed from messaging.md to fit 15 words.
- Inference: Pillars 1 and 2 beat only the status quo, from competitors.md.
- Unknown: What can we tell legal about uploaded documents?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Build a messaging framework". Read the context pack.
Task: <any new positioning statement, and new proof with permission status>.
```


===== FILE: skills/positioning-and-messaging/review-messaging.md =====

---
title: Review messaging
type: skill
function: positioning-and-messaging
owner: marketing-reviewer
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The text to review, unless it is messaging.md, and whether its use is public, sales only or internal.
also_for: [does our message hold up, is our messaging clear, claims check, can we say this, messaging sounds like everyone, sales ignores the messaging, differentiation check, website sounds generic]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Critiques messaging or positioning claim by claim for accuracy, proof and differentiation, and gives a verdict.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this on a positioning statement, a messaging framework or a set of claims before anyone builds on them. Do not use it on finished copy such as a page, an email or an ad; use [Review any output](../marketing-ops/review-any-output.md) instead. This skill critiques and does not rewrite; to produce new messaging, use [Build a messaging framework](build-messaging-framework.md).

## Inputs

From the context pack:

- [[context-pack:messaging]] – the text under review if none is pasted, and words to avoid
- [[context-pack:product]] – features, what it is not, known weak spots, roadmap rule
- [[context-pack:proof-points]] – evidence and permission status
- [[context-pack:competitors]] – dated facts, where each wins and loses
- [[context-pack:personas]] – what each persona is measured on
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]] – the target and its disqualifiers
- [[context-pack:company]] – which company numbers may be published
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]] – hard rules and approvers

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the text to review, if it is not messaging.md, and the intended use: public, sales only or internal.

## Procedure

1. Take the pasted text, or messaging.md if nothing is pasted. If both are blank, ask for the text.
2. Set the intended use from the prompt. If it is missing, treat the text as public, the strictest case, and record that as an assumption.
3. Split the text into numbered claims. A claim is any statement a buyer could check or dispute. Quote each one exactly, at most 20 words.
4. Run the accuracy test on each claim: does product.md support it, and does it stay clear of "What it is not", "Known weak spots" and roadmap items? A claim stronger than the fact fails. A claim with no fact in product.md is marked unknown, not false.
5. Run the proof test: is there an item in proof-points.md, or a publishable number in company.md, that backs this exact claim, with a permission status that allows the intended use? Proof that measures something else fails. An item with no entry in proof-points.md fails for public use and is listed as unknown. A plain capability that product.md supports needs no proof item.
6. Run the differentiation test: put each named competitor's name in the claim. If competitors.md suggests the sentence would still be true of all of them, mark "true of all". If of some, mark "true of some" and name them. If competitors.md is silent, write "cannot tell" and list it as unknown, without guessing.
7. Run the rules test: words from "words to avoid", competitor claims with no source and date, competitor names in text meant for ads, a target that conflicts with the disqualifiers, and a message that misses what its persona is measured on.
8. Rate each failure. Blocker: false, contradicts the context pack, or uses proof not permitted for the intended use. Major: a claim stronger than the fact, no proof, proof that measures something else, or "true of all" on a pillar. Minor: wording, or any other differentiation result short of "cannot tell".
9. Set the verdict. Any blocker: "Do not use". No blocker and at least one major: "Fix first". Otherwise: "Facts and proof checked". End every verdict with "Strategy not judged."
10. Name the single weakest idea in the text, the one a senior marketer would push on first. Ask it as a question for the marketer, and say why. It does not change the verdict.
11. For each finding, name the evidence by file and field, and give a fix direction in 20 words at most. Do not rewrite the claim unless the marketer asks.
12. List the claims that passed all tests, so the marketer knows what to keep.
13. Check the review against the context pack: every finding cites a real field, and competitor statements stay within competitors.md.
14. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not verify in the last section, each with its label.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Verdict
<Do not use, Fix first or Facts and proof checked>, for <intended use>. Strategy not judged. <Reason, 40 words at most>
Weakest idea: <the single weakest idea, and why, as a question for the marketer, 30 words at most>

## Findings
| # | Claim | Test failed | Severity | Evidence | Fix direction |
(most severe first, 12 rows at most)

## Claims that passed
- <claim; 6 items at most>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Was every claim tested, not only the weak-looking ones?
- [ ] Does each finding cite a file and field?
- [ ] Does the verdict follow the severity rule?
- [ ] Was proof checked for what it actually measures, not only whether it exists?
- [ ] Was permission checked against the stated use?
- [ ] Are competitor statements limited to what the competitors file holds?
- [ ] Did the review stay a critique, with no rewriting unasked?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

**Verdict**

Do not use, for public. Strategy not judged. One claim promises a view of individuals that the product rules out, and two say more than the proof shows.

Weakest idea: why lead with "a chatbot built for practice", when Arguvia can say it too and your buyer's worry is showing that training worked?

**Findings**

| # | Claim | Test failed | Severity | Evidence | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "Leaders can see how each person reasons" | Accuracy | Blocker | product.md: progress by team, never an individual's conversation | Claim team-level progress only |
| 2 | "Better reasoning in your first two weeks" | Proof | Major | proof-points.md: median of 9 days measures first live session, not reasoning | Claim only what 9 days shows |
| 3 | "Every learner practices every week" | Proof | Major | proof-points.md, Result 2: 72% of assigned learners, Q3 2026 | Use the actual figure |
| 4 | "A chatbot built for practice" | Differentiation | Minor | True of some: competitors.md lists Arguvia as a debate practice chatbot | Say which alternative this beats |

**Claims that passed**

- "It asks, it does not answer"
- "Brightmere's analysts raised client memo reasoning scores 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners"

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: public use, because none was stated.
- Assumption: the failed claims come from an illustrative draft pasted for this example, not from messaging.md.
- Inference: Finding 4 rests on Arguvia's category alone.
- Unknown: Does Arguvia also decline to give answers? competitors.md is silent, so "It asks" is marked cannot tell.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Review messaging". Read the context pack.
Task: <public, sales only or internal use>.
<paste the text here, unless it is messaging.md>
```


===== FILE: skills/positioning-and-messaging/write-positioning-statement.md =====

---
title: Write a positioning statement
type: skill
function: positioning-and-messaging
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points]
you_bring: The target segment if it is not your first priority, plus any new evidence and customer quotes with source and date.
also_for: [what category are we, we sound like everyone, how are we different, differentiation, explain what we do, why us, new segment positioning, ceo asks what we are]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a positioning statement for one target, the reasoning behind it and what would break it.
judge: ["Would a buyer who has never heard of you repeat this to a colleague?", "Could your top competitor say the same sentence?", "Is this why customers really chose you or why you wish they had?", "Which deals would you now walk away from because of this and would your CEO accept that?"]
show_to: your sales lead, and ask which line a buyer would argue with
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you have no positioning statement, enter a new segment, or reach the quarterly positioning review. Do not use it to write pillars and persona messages; use [Build a messaging framework](build-messaging-framework.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: category, features, limits, weak spots
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: priority order, situation, triggers
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the champion and their measures
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: the current statement and words to avoid
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: alternatives, the status quo, dated facts
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status

The marketer supplies: the target segment, if not the first priority, and new evidence and customer quotes, with source and date.

## Procedure

1. If the category, the ICP or the competitors are blank, ask before drafting. If the marketer wants a draft anyway, leave a visible placeholder such as [category needed]. Never invent.
2. Pick one target. Use the segment the marketer names, otherwise the first in the priority order in icp-and-segments.md, or ask. Use the champion in personas.md, or ask.
3. List the alternatives: every competitor in competitors.md plus the status quo. A pasted competitor claim with no source and date stays out and is listed as unknown.
4. For each alternative, write what the product does that the alternative does not. Keep a difference only if product.md lists the capability, competitors.md backs the gap with "Where they lose" or a dated fact, and the difference does not touch "What it is not" or "Known weak spots".
5. Turn each difference into one value tied to what the target persona is measured on. Drop differences that reach no measure and keep at most 4 values.
6. Score each value out of 3, one point each: it has proof with status Public in proof-points.md, it answers a situation or trigger in icp-and-segments.md, and competitors.md records no alternative that can also claim it. Pending and None items count as no proof. So does proof found only in messaging.md.
7. Choose the lead value: the highest score. Break a tie with the value closest to the first item the persona is measured on. If the top score is 1, say under "What would break it" that the position is weak.
8. Compare two or three categories: product.md's plus others buyers use, from competitors.md or customer words. Say what buyers would compare each with and whether the lead value wins there. Keep product.md's unless another fits better, and say why.
9. Write the statement in the exact form shown in the template. The reason to believe must be a fact from product.md. Use at most two "Unlike" sentences, favoring differences that rest on dated facts.
10. Write the one-sentence description with no competitor names.
11. Fill in the reasoning table and name the segments and buyer types the statement gives up.
12. Name up to 3 things that would make it false or weak, such as a missing permission.
13. Mark the result Hypothesis if no customer quote, in proof-points.md or pasted notes, backs the lead value and the category.
14. Compare with the current statement. Same target and lead value: write "Keep the current statement" and list wording edits only. Otherwise say what changed and why. If there is none, write "No current statement".
15. Check the draft against the context pack: no claim crosses "What it is not", no word comes from "words to avoid", every competitor claim has a source and date, and only Public proof appears as public.
16. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not find in the last section, each with its label.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Evidence: <"Backed by" and the quotes, or "Hypothesis" and what is missing>

## Category comparison
| Category | Compared with | Lead value wins? |
(2 or 3 rows)

## Positioning statement
For <target persona at target company type> who <situation>, <product> is <category> that <lead value>, because <reason to believe>. Unlike <alternative>, it <difference>. Unlike <alternative>, it <difference>.
(max 80 words)

## One-sentence description
<max 15 words>

## Reasoning
| Value | Beats | Proof and permission | Score |
(max 4 rows, cells max 15 words)
Why this value leads: <max 30 words>
Who this gives up: <max 30 words>

## What would break it
- <max 3 items, 20 words each>

## Change from the current statement
<max 40 words>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the statement name one target, not several?
- [ ] Is the reason to believe a fact from the product file, not a hope?
- [ ] Could a named alternative not honestly say the same thing?
- [ ] Does every proof item show its permission status, with nothing pending or unapproved counted as proof?
- [ ] Does it say who the statement gives up?
- [ ] Were categories compared, and is the result marked Hypothesis if no customer quote backs it?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Evidence: Hypothesis. The lead value rests on a measured result, and no customer quote names this category.

**Category comparison**

| Category | Compared with | Lead value wins? |
|---|---|---|
| Critical thinking training software | Elenchly | Yes |
| Course library | Elenchly | No, buyers expect courses |

**Positioning statement**

For heads of learning at professional services firms who worry that junior staff accept AI answers without checking them, CogniATion Coach is critical thinking training software that shows whether people's reasoning improves, because it scores reasoning against a five-point rubric and reports progress by team. Unlike Elenchly, it is practice, not a course. Unlike Arguvia, it uses the customer's own scenarios and reports to the learning leader.

**One-sentence description**

CogniATion Coach makes people practice reasoning and shows learning leaders what improved.

**Reasoning**

| Value | Beats | Proof and permission | Score |
|---|---|---|---|
| See reasoning improve | Elenchly, Arguvia | Brightmere 22% result: Public | 3 |
| Asks, does not answer | Status quo | Lena Marsh quote: Public | 2 |
