Why this value leads: the champion is measured on proof that training changed behavior, and this scores highest.

Who this gives up: software and financial services, and buyers wanting one course library.

**What would break it**

- Elenchly adds reports on reasoning, removing the difference.
- Brightmere withdraws approval, leaving no Public reasoning result.

**Change from the current statement**

Narrowed to professional services. Leads with visible progress, not practice.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: professional services is the target, as first in the priority order.
- Unknown: Do Elenchly's reports show more than completion data and quiz scores?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a positioning statement". Read the context pack.
Task: <the target segment, if not the first priority>.
<paste new evidence and customer quotes here>
```


===== FILE: skills/pr-and-comms/draft-holding-statement.md =====

---
title: Draft a holding statement
type: skill
function: pr-and-comms
owner: pr-and-communications-manager
reads: [company, product, messaging, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: What is confirmed and by whom, when it started, who is affected, what is being done, who is asking, and the next update time.
also_for: [outage statement, data breach, crisis communication, bad press, angry customer post, journalist calling, public complaint, incident response]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces the first statement for an incident, with Q&A, an escalation check and a time for the next update.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this in the first hours of an incident, when people are asking and the facts are incomplete: an outage, a data problem, a safety event, a public complaint. Do not use it once the facts are known and you are announcing a fix or a change; use [Write a press release](write-press-release.md) instead. It does not replace a lawyer when step 2 says to call one.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: public name, who may speak, press contact.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does and is not.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: words to avoid and promises the incident contradicts.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: tone and things never to say.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and owned channels.

The marketer supplies: what happened as far as confirmed, who confirmed it, when it started, who is affected, what is being done, who is asking, and the next update time.

## Procedure

1. Sort every fact from the prompt into two lists: confirmed (with who confirmed it) and not yet known. Anything without a named source goes in not yet known. If the prompt does not say what is confirmed, ask before drafting.
2. Run the escalation check. If the incident involves injury or death, personal or customer data, a regulator, police, a legal threat, or a claim about product safety, write "Stop: get a lawyer and the CEO before anything goes out" at the top, and still draft for their review.
3. Set the mode. Choose "reactive, send only when asked" when few customers are affected and no one outside has noticed. Choose "proactive" when customers are affected or a public report exists. In proactive mode, affected customers hear before anyone else.
4. Write the statement in four parts, in this order: what we know, who is affected as far as we know, what we are doing, when we will next update. Use only the confirmed list.
5. Do not state a cause, a fix time, an amount of data or a number of customers unless it is on the confirmed list. Do not say "no customer data was affected" unless a named person has checked. Do not blame a person, a supplier or a customer.
6. Write without marketing: no proof points, no customer names, no product benefits and no claims about reliability. A product feature that might limit the effect, such as a backup or offline mode, goes in only if someone has confirmed it worked during this incident.
7. Set the next update time from the prompt. If there is none, leave [next update time needed]. Never promise one the marketer did not give.
8. Write the customer note: the same facts, plus what the customer should do now. If nothing, say so.
9. Write 5 to 7 Q&A items, covering the hardest questions first: what happened, who is affected, is data safe, whose fault is it, when will it be fixed. Where the honest answer is unknown, answer "We do not know yet" and say when that will change.
10. Fill the escalation table from company.md and channels-and-tools.md. If a needed field is blank, or no approver is listed for incidents, or the usual approval time is too slow, leave a visible placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: every word against words to avoid and brand-voice.md, and every statement against the confirmed list. Remove anything that fails.
12. List everything you assumed, and everything not yet known, in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft, not for release until approved
Escalation check: <"Stop: get a lawyer and the CEO" and the reason, or "No trigger found">
Mode: <Reactive or proactive, and one line why>

## Holding statement
<80 words maximum, the four parts from step 4>

## Customer note
<60 words maximum. Same facts, plus what to do now>

## Q&A
Q: <question>
A: <answer, 30 words maximum>
<5 to 7 pairs, hardest first>

## Do not say
- <Up to 5 tempting lines to avoid, each with the reason>

## Escalation
| Job | Person | When |
|---|---|---|
| Approves the statement | <name or placeholder> | <before release> |
| Speaks to press | <name or placeholder> | <when> |
| Handles press inquiries | <contact> | <when> |
| Tells customers | <name or placeholder> | <before public release> |
Update this statement when: <what would change it>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is every statement backed by a fact with a named source?
- [ ] Did you run the escalation check, and act on any trigger?
- [ ] Is the statement free of cause, fix time and scope unless confirmed?
- [ ] Is it free of blame, jokes, marketing claims and customer names?
- [ ] Is there a time for the next update, from a real person?
- [ ] Do affected customers hear before the public?
- [ ] Does each Q&A answer say "We do not know yet" where that is the truth?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft, not for release until approved

Escalation check: No trigger found, on the facts given

Mode: Proactive, because customers are affected

**Holding statement**

Some learners cannot start practice sessions in CogniATion Coach. We do not yet know the cause or which customers are affected. We are working to restore access. We will update you by [next update time needed].

**Customer note**

Some of your learners cannot start CogniATion Coach sessions. We are working on it. Please ask them to try again after our next update. Next update by [next update time needed].

**Q&A**

Q: Is learner data safe?

A: We do not know yet. [Named person to confirm before this changes]

Q: Whose fault is it?

A: We do not know the cause yet, and we will say when we do.

**Do not say**

- "No learner's conversation or score was affected." No named person has checked.

**Escalation**

| Job | Person | When |
|---|---|---|
| Approves the statement | Marta Ilves, CEO | Before release, same day |
| Speaks to press | Marta Ilves, CEO (assumption) | When asked |
| Handles press inquiries | press@cogniation.example | Now |
| Tells customers | [name needed] | Before public release |

Update this statement when: the cause or scope is confirmed.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the incident itself is invented for this example.
- Assumption: the CEO speaks to press, because company.md names no one for incidents.
- Unknown: who tells customers, and who can confirm whether learner data is affected?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Draft a holding statement". Read the context pack.
Task: <what is confirmed and by whom, when it started, who is affected, what is being done, who is asking, next update time>.
```


===== FILE: skills/pr-and-comms/write-media-pitch.md =====

---
title: Write a media pitch
type: skill
function: pr-and-comms
owner: pr-and-communications-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The type of journalist or outlet, what they have covered recently, and any news hook or date.
also_for: [press coverage, pitch a journalist, get media attention, trade press, earned media, story idea, pr outreach, reporter email]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces one short pitch and angle for one type of journalist or outlet, using only public proof.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this to offer one story to one type of journalist or outlet, such as a trade editor covering corporate learning. Do not use it for a formal announcement to everyone; use [Write a press release](write-press-release.md) instead. Run it once per type of outlet, because an angle that fits two kinds of reader usually fits neither.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: who may speak on what, press contact, publishable numbers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does and is not, and whether any roadmap item is cleared.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: the segment this reader cares about and what triggers buying there.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the problems and language of the people the outlet writes for.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customers, results and quotes, with permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: tone and things never to say.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves outside communication.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the type of journalist or outlet, what that person has covered recently with links or titles if known, the journalist's name if known, and any news hook or date.

## Procedure

1. Name the reader of the outlet in one line: their job and what they worry about. Take this from personas.md if the outlet writes for one of the personas. If it does not, ask the marketer who reads it.
2. Write three candidate angles, one line each, stated as a story about the reader's problem, not about the company. Discard any angle that needs a fact not in the context pack or the prompt.
3. Pick one angle using this order: it has public proof, then it connects to a hook the marketer supplied, then it fits the highest-priority segment in icp-and-segments.md. State the pick and why in one line. List the rejected angles.
4. Write the subject line: under 10 words, naming the topic, with no "exclusive" unless the marketer says it is.
5. Write the opening: one sentence linking to the journalist's recent coverage, only if the marketer supplied it. Otherwise leave [recent coverage needed]. Never guess what a journalist wrote or cares about.
6. Write the pitch body: the story in two sentences, then one to three proof items, each public in proof-points.md or publishable in company.md, with its date or sample.
7. Write the offer: exactly what the journalist can have, such as an interview or data. Name a company leader only for the topics company.md gives them. If the best speaker is outside those topics, say so as an open question. A public quote does not mean a customer agreed to an interview, so label any customer interview "to be confirmed with the customer".
8. Close with one clear question the journalist can answer yes or no. No follow-up pressure.
9. Do not name competitors, mention roadmap items or attach pricing. If a needed context pack field is blank, leave a visible placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: every name and number against its permission, every word against words to avoid, every claim against what the product is not. If the approvals table has no line for media pitches, say who you assumed approves it.
11. List everything you assumed, and every placeholder still open, in the last section.

This skill finds no journalists and sends nothing. The marketer chooses the recipient and sends the pitch.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Reader: <who reads this outlet, one line>
Angle: <one sentence>
Why this angle: <one line>
Rejected angles: <up to 2, one line each>

## Pitch
Subject: <under 10 words>

<Greeting, with the journalist's name or [name needed]>

<Opening, one sentence on their coverage, or [recent coverage needed]>

<Story, two sentences>

<Proof, 1 to 3 items, one line each, with date or sample>

<Offer, one or two sentences>

<One yes-or-no question>

<Marketer's name, press contact>

Total pitch length: 150 words maximum, not counting the subject.

## Approvals needed
- <Approver, what they approve>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is the angle about the reader's problem, not about the company?
- [ ] Is the pitch 150 words or fewer?
- [ ] Is the reference to the journalist's work supplied by the marketer, or a visible placeholder?
- [ ] Is every proof item public or publishable, with its date or sample?
- [ ] Is it clear what the journalist can have, and is any customer interview marked as unconfirmed?
- [ ] Is the pitch free of competitor names, roadmap items and words to avoid?
- [ ] Could the journalist answer the closing question with one word?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Reader: editors at a trade publication on corporate learning, writing for heads of learning and development.

Angle: how a head of learning can show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it.

Why this angle: it has public proof, and professional services, where Brightmere Advisory sits, is the top-priority segment.

Rejected angles: junior staff accepting AI answers unchecked, no public number behind it. Time to first live session, weaker for this reader.

**Pitch**

Subject: Showing that training changed how people reason

Hi [name needed],

[recent coverage needed]

The heads of learning we talk to can show who finished a course, but not whether it changed how people work. Many have rolled out AI assistants and worry that junior staff now accept answers without checking them.

Proof: analysts at Brightmere Advisory, a professional services firm using CogniATion Coach, raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners. 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week in Q3 2026, across all customers.

We can share a quote from Lena Marsh, Brightmere's head of learning, with an interview to be confirmed with the customer. Anika Sole, our VP product, can explain how the Coach questions employees instead of answering them.

Would a conversation about this be useful for your readers?

[Marketer's name], press@cogniation.example

**Approvals needed**

- Marta Ilves, CEO, this pitch

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the outlet is invented for this example, and its readers match the head of learning in personas.md.
- Inference: Anika Sole fits, because company.md gives her how the Coach works.
- Unknown: who should speak on measuring reasoning? company.md gives leaders no such topic.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a media pitch". Read the context pack.
Task: <the type of journalist or outlet, recent coverage, name if known, and any news hook or date>.
```


===== FILE: skills/pr-and-comms/write-press-release.md =====

---
title: Write a press release
type: skill
function: pr-and-comms
owner: pr-and-communications-manager
reads: [company, product, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The news in one sentence, the release date or embargo, and any quote text the speaker has approved.
also_for: [announcement, funding news, news release, leadership hire, milestone news, pr announcement, wire release, announce a release]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a press release with approved quotes only and visible placeholders for anything not yet cleared.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when something has actually changed and you want a formal announcement: a release, a funding round, a milestone, a leadership hire. Do not use it to reach one specific journalist; use [Write a media pitch](write-media-pitch.md) instead. If the news fails the test in step 1, use [Write a blog post](../content/write-blog-post.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: public name, headquarters, one-line description, who may be quoted on what, press contact, and which numbers are publishable, internal or forbidden.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does, what it is not, and whether any roadmap item is cleared.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: one-sentence description and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: customer names, results and quotes, with permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: style details and things never to say.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves a press release, and how long it takes.

The marketer supplies in the prompt: the news in one sentence, the release date or embargo, any quote text the speaker has approved, and any facts about the news that the context pack lacks.

## Procedure

1. Test the news. Write one sentence saying what changed, for whom, and as of when. If you cannot, or if nothing changed, say "Weak news" in the status line and recommend a blog post, then draft anyway only if the marketer asked for a release regardless.
2. Check clearance. If the news is a product or feature that product.md does not clear for public mention, mark the status line "Not cleared for public mention" and ask who can clear it. If the prompt contains a number company.md lists as internal or forbidden, leave it out and flag it.
3. Write the headline: under 12 words, sentence case, saying what happened. No exclamation points and no adjectives about the company.
4. Write the lead paragraph: who, what, when, where, in under 50 words, opening with the dateline from the headquarters in company.md.
5. Write the body in third person, even if brand-voice.md uses "we", because other people will reuse the text. Note this under assumptions. Keep every number with its date or sample as proof-points.md or company.md states it.
6. Add quotes. Use a customer quote only word for word and only with public permission in proof-points.md. For a company leader, use quote text only if the marketer supplied it as approved. Otherwise leave [quote from name, on topic, approval needed]. Never write a quote for anyone. Keep each leader to the topics company.md gives them.
7. Do not name competitors. Do not mention pricing unless it is the news, and then list the pricing approver from channels-and-tools.md.
8. Add the boilerplate from company.md, using only facts and publishable numbers from there, and the press contact.
9. Fill the approvals block from channels-and-tools.md. If a needed field in the context pack is blank, leave a visible placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section. Never invent.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: every name, number and quote against its permission, every word against words to avoid, every product claim against what the product is not. Fix anything that fails.
11. List everything you assumed, every placeholder still open, and every approval still needed in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: <Draft, plus "Weak news" or "Not cleared for public mention" if either applies>
Release: <date and time, or "Embargoed until <date>", or [release date needed]>

## <Headline, under 12 words>

<City, state, date> – <Lead paragraph, under 50 words>

<Body, 2 to 3 paragraphs, 150 words maximum in total>

"<Quote>" <Name, title, company>
<One or two quotes, each word for word or a placeholder>

## About <public company name>
<Boilerplate, 50 words maximum>

## Press contact
<Contact from company.md>

## Approvals needed
- <Approver, what they approve, typical time>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the headline say what happened, with no praise of the company?
- [ ] Can you point to what changed, and when?
- [ ] Is every quote word for word, approved for public use, or a visible placeholder?
- [ ] Does every number appear in company.md as publishable or in proof-points.md as public, with its date or sample?
- [ ] Is every leader quoted only on the topics company.md gives them?
- [ ] Is the release free of roadmap items, competitor names and words to avoid?
- [ ] Are the approver and the time they need listed, with the release date allowing for it?

## Example (fictional company)

Status: Draft

Release: [release date needed]

**CogniATion Coach reaches 28,000 learners at 64 companies**

Austin, Texas, [date] – CogniATion said today that 28,000 learners at 64 customer companies had active seats on CogniATion Coach, its critical thinking training software, as of September 30, 2026.

Learners have completed more than 2.3 million practice sessions as of the same date. The Coach is a chatbot that questions employees about a work problem instead of answering it, then scores their reasoning against a rubric. Learning leaders see progress by team, and never see an individual's conversation.

"My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory

[Quote from Marta Ilves, CEO, on company direction, approval needed]

**About CogniATion**

CogniATion makes a specialized AI chatbot that trains employees to think through problems instead of asking for answers. Founded in 2021, it is based in Austin, Texas.

**Press contact**

press@cogniation.example

**Approvals needed**

- Marta Ilves, CEO, this release, 2 days

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: third person is used despite "we" in brand-voice.md, because other people will reuse the text.
- Assumption: the 2.3 million sessions were completed by those 28,000 learners. company.md gives both numbers but does not link them. Confirm.
- Inference: the Lena Marsh quote needs no new customer approval, because proof-points.md marks it public, approved June 2026.
- Unknown: is a learner count news to the press, or would a blog post do? Step 1 is close to "Weak news".

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a press release". Read the context pack.
Task: <the news in one sentence, the date or embargo, approved quotes, and facts the context pack lacks>.
```


===== FILE: skills/product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/request-customer-proof.md =====

---
title: Request customer proof
type: skill
function: product-marketing-and-sales-enablement
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [proof-points, icp-and-segments, messaging, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The claim or segment that needs proof, where it will appear, and who you know at the customer.
also_for: [customer quote, customer testimonial, logo permission, permission to use a customer name, reference customer, need proof for a claim, ask a customer for a number]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Writes the ask to a customer for a quote, number or logo, with who to ask, the message, the permission wording and the entry for proof-points.md.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when a claim needs a customer quote, number or logo that you do not have permission to use. Do not use it to write the full story; use [Write a case study](write-case-study.md) once you have notes. To learn what customers think rather than clear what they said, use [Write an interview guide](../segmentation-icp-and-personas/write-interview-guide.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: what is on file, permission status, the format of an entry.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segment priority.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and which claims lack cleared proof.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice for the message.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves customer items, how long it takes, hard rules.

The marketer supplies the claim or segment, where the proof would appear, and the date it is needed. Optional: customers and contacts to consider, anything a customer said word for word, and any number with its period, sample and who measured it.

## Procedure

1. Name the gap: one claim, one segment, one intended use. If none is given, list the pillars in messaging.md with no Public proof for the top segment, and ask which comes first.
2. List what proof-points.md holds for the gap. If a Public item covers the use, say no request is needed and stop. If a request is Pending, write one reminder rather than a second ask, and keep the item out of all drafts.
3. Choose who to ask, up to 3 customers, ranked by same segment, then a result on file, then a known contact (a starting rule). Use only names from proof-points.md or the marketer. Ask whether any has an open dispute or renewal.
4. Name who can approve, since a company name, number or logo may need someone senior: [customer approver needed] if unknown. Name the sender, or [sender needed].
5. Choose the smallest ask that fills the gap, up to 3 items (a starting rule). Prefer widening an existing permission, then an exact quote, a number, a logo. Approval for one item does not cover the next.
6. Set the exact text, in the customer's own words or number. Never write a quote and offer it as theirs. With no words on file, ask one question they can answer themselves. Give each number's period, sample and who measured it, or [period needed].
7. Write the message in at most 120 words: the exact text, where it would appear, a reply date, and that saying no changes nothing. Offer no discount or favor unless the marketer supplies one. The marketer sends it.
8. Write the permission wording as a reply they can copy: item, credit, uses, logo and who is approving. Only a written reply counts. The wording is a starting point for any legal review on either side.
9. Write the proof-points.md entries in that file's format: now, if yes, if no. The current status holds until the written reply arrives. Use only the status values the file defines.
10. Set the dates: send, one reminder after 5 business days (a starting rule), and the approval route from channels-and-tools.md. If the needed date leaves less than the typical time, say so.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: permission status, internal-only numbers, words to avoid, brand voice, hard rules.
12. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Gap: <claim, segment, intended use>

## On file
| Item | Status | Covers this use? |

## Who to ask
| Rank | Customer and person | Who approves | Why them | Sender |

## The ask
<Each item: exact text or number, credit, where it would appear>

## Message
Subject: <50 characters at most>
Body: <120 words at most>

## Permission wording
"<reply they can copy, up to 60 words>"

## Entry for proof-points.md
Now: <item, current status, then "pending, requested <month and year>">
If yes: <"Permission: <status>, approved <month and year> by <name, title>">
If no: <what stays, or "Permission: none">

## Route and dates
| Date | Step | Owner |

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is every customer and person from proof-points.md or the marketer?
- [ ] Is every quote the customer's own words, and every number given with its period and sample?
- [ ] Does the message say that saying no changes nothing?
- [ ] Does the permission wording cover item, credit, uses, logo and approver?
- [ ] Does the entry keep the current status until a written reply arrives?
- [ ] Do the dates allow the typical approval time?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Gap:** Pillar 3, you can see reasoning improve, software companies, website.

**On file**

| Item | Status | Covers this use? |
|---|---|---|
| Corvane Software result and quote | Sales only, no logo | No, not online |

**Who to ask**

| Rank | Customer and person | Who approves | Why them | Sender |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Corvane Software, Sam Oduya, VP engineering | [customer approver needed] | Same segment, proof on file | [sender needed] |

**The ask:** public use of Quote 2 and Result 3, credit as already approved. Logo, yes or no.

**Message**

Subject: Could we use your words on our website?

Body: Sam, our sales material carries this quote from you, approved in August: "Managers argue about the assumption now, not about who is senior." May we also use it on our website, with the result that 120 engineering managers went from 2.6 to 3.4 out of 5 in 12 weeks? If you say no, nothing changes. Could you reply by November 18?

**Permission wording:** "Yes, CogniATion may use this quote and result on its website and in ads, credited to me by name and title. Logo: yes or no. I can approve this for Corvane Software."

**Entry for proof-points.md**

Now: Quote 2 and Result 3. Permission: sales only, approved August 2026. Public use: pending, requested November 2026.

If yes: Permission: public, approved [month and year] by Sam Oduya, VP engineering.

If no: Stays sales only.

**Route and dates:** November 4, send. November 11, reminder. Then Marta Ilves, CEO. Allow 2 weeks.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all dates, and that the website is the use.
- Unknown: has Haldane Ridge Capital answered the October request? It stays out of all drafts until then.

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Request customer proof". Read the context pack.
Task: draft the request for <the claim or segment that needs proof>, for use on <where it will appear>, needed by <date>.
```


===== FILE: skills/product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-battlecard.md =====

---
title: Write a battlecard
type: skill
function: product-marketing-and-sales-enablement
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [competitors, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, proof-points, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The competitor's name, today's date, and any new sourced material or lost-deal notes with dates.
also_for: [rival keeps appearing in deals, competitor comes up on calls, sales needs help against a competitor, how to beat a rival, objection handling, competitive sheet, losing to a competitor, talk track]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Produces a short internal sheet for sales against one named competitor, using only sourced and dated facts from competitors.md.
judge: ["Could a salesperson find the answer in the ten seconds they have on a call?", "Does it help win on what buyers care about or only list what the competitor lacks?", "Would someone who used to work at this competitor call it fair?", "What should a salesperson say when the buyer already prefers the competitor?"]
show_to: the salesperson who last lost to this competitor, and ask what the card gets wrong
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when sales needs a sheet for deals against one named competitor already in competitors.md. Do not use it to research or update what you know about the competitor; use [Write a competitor profile](../competitive-intelligence/write-competitor-profile.md) instead. For a page a buyer can read, use [Write a sales one-pager](write-sales-one-pager.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:competitors]]: facts with source and date, where they win and lose, our answer.
- [[context-pack:product]]: features, what it is not, known weak spots, price, roadmap rules.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: disqualifiers, to know when to walk away.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: objections and who to direct questions to.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approver and hard rules.

The marketer supplies: the competitor's name, today's date, and any new sourced material or lost-deal notes, each with its source and date checked. Work only from what is pasted.

## Procedure

1. Find the competitor in competitors.md. If it is not there and the marketer has pasted nothing, stop and ask for a competitor profile first. For what buyers do today, use the status quo entry and the same template.
2. Copy each known fact with its source and date. Write absences as "not found in [source]", never as "does not have". Give what buyers said as a report, with the count.
3. Mark any fact checked more than 6 months before today's date as Stale and tell sales to verify it before using it. Six months is a starting rule the marketer can change.
4. Use nothing about the competitor from memory, even if you recognize the name. Any claim sales wants but competitors.md does not support goes under Unknown.
5. Write "Where they win" honestly, from competitors.md. Add a "When to walk away" line from the ICP disqualifiers and the competitor's strengths.
6. Write "Where we win": at most 3 lines, each tied to a pillar and to a sourced fact about them. Nothing beyond product.md. List our own weak spots from product.md under "Where we are exposed", with what sales says when asked.
7. Write 3 to 5 questions for sales to ask the buyer, starting from "Our answer" in competitors.md. Each question must point at a gap in the sourced facts, not at a guess.
8. Write at most 3 things the buyer will say, and an answer of at most 30 words each. Use objections from personas.md and the marketer's notes. If there is no basis for an answer, write [answer needed from sales].
9. Choose proof. Use Public and Sales only items, keeping limits such as no logo. Mark each as usable in one-to-one conversations. Internal only, Pending and None never appear. Do not use internal numbers from company.md.
10. If you compare prices, use only prices in product.md and sourced competitor prices, label the math as an estimate, and flag the approver for any pricing mention.
11. If a context pack field is blank, leave a placeholder in square brackets and ask in the last section.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: every competitor line has a source and date, permission status, what the product is not, words to avoid. Add "Internal, sales use only, not for ads or buyers" at the top, because hard rules may ban competitor names in ads.
13. List everything assumed, estimated or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Internal, sales use only, not for ads or buyers
Status: Draft
Competitor: <name and category>
Facts last checked: <latest and oldest date>

## Where they win
<Max 2 lines>

## When to walk away
<Max 2 lines>

## Where we win
<Max 3 lines, each tied to a pillar>

## Where we are exposed
<Max 2 lines, each with what sales says, max 25 words>

## Known facts
| Fact | Source | Date checked | Stale? |

## Questions to ask the buyer
<3 to 5 numbered questions>

## What the buyer will say
| They say | Answer (max 30 words) |

## Proof to use
| Item | Permission | Limit |

## Approval
<Approver and typical time>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every statement about the competitor have a source and a date from competitors.md or the marketer's paste?
- [ ] Are absences written as "not found", not as facts?
- [ ] Is it honest about where they win and where we are exposed?
- [ ] Does it say when to walk away?
- [ ] Is each proof item allowed for one-to-one use, with its limits shown?
- [ ] Is it free of internal numbers and roadmap items?
- [ ] Is it marked as not for ads or buyers, with the approver named?

## Example (fictional company)

Internal, sales use only, not for ads or buyers

**Status:** Draft

**Competitor:** Arguvia, low-cost debate practice chatbot for individuals

**Facts last checked:** October 2026

**Where they win:** Small teams and individuals wanting something today at a low price.

**When to walk away:** Fewer than 100 learners.

**Where we win:** You can see reasoning improve: team reports and custom scenarios, against generic topics and no team reports found.

**Where we are exposed:** Price. Say: "Fine for one person practicing. Ask how they would show leadership that it worked." No reports below team level. Say: "No individual's conversation is on display."

**Known facts**

| Fact | Source | Date checked | Stale? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lists $9 per user per month | Pricing page | October 2026 | No |
| Generic debate topics only | Help center | October 2026 | No |
| Team reports not found | Help center | October 2026 | No |

**Questions to ask the buyer**

1. How would you show leadership that it worked?
2. Do you need your own scenarios, or will generic topics do?
3. Who needs to see team progress?

**What the buyer will say**

| They say | Answer |
|---|---|
| "It is cheaper." | Yes, $108 per user a year against our $180. Ask what leadership would see. |

**Proof to use**

| Item | Permission | Limit |
|---|---|---|
| "Managers argue about the assumption now, not about who is senior." Sam Oduya, VP engineering, Corvane Software | Sales only | One-to-one, no logo |

**Approval:** Tom Ferrant, 2 days. Anika Sole for the price, 1 day.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Estimate: $108 is $9 times 12 months. Our $180 excludes the $5,000 setup.
- Unknown: Has Arguvia won where the buyer needed team reports?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a battlecard". Read the context pack.
Task: <the competitor's name and any new sourced notes, with dates>.
```


===== FILE: skills/product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-case-study.md =====

---
title: Write a case study
type: skill
function: product-marketing-and-sales-enablement
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [product, messaging, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The customer's name, the interview notes or transcript, the interview date, and where it will appear.
also_for: [customer story, success story, customer proof, testimonial write-up, reference customer, customer result, website proof, sales proof]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Turns interview notes into a case study, starting with a permission check on the customer, each number and each quote.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you have notes or a transcript from a customer interview and want a case study. Do not use it to find patterns across many customers; use [Synthesize customer research](../segmentation-icp-and-personas/synthesize-customer-research.md) instead. For a short item without a customer story, use [Write a sales one-pager](write-sales-one-pager.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does and is not, to check the customer's words against it.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: permission status for the customer, each result and each quote.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice and rules for public copy.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves a customer story, and how long it takes.

The marketer supplies: the customer's name, the interview notes or transcript, the date of the interview, and where the case study will appear. Web, email or social means public. One-to-one means sales hands it to a named buyer. If not stated, treat it as public.

## Procedure

1. Run the permission check before writing anything. Look up the customer in proof-points.md and compare its status with the intended use:
   - Public: continue.
   - Sales only: continue only for one-to-one use. If the use is public, stop and offer to draft a one-to-one version instead. Keep limits such as no logo.
   - Pending, None, Internal only or not listed: stop. Return the permission check and the request list. Write the story only if the marketer says the copy is for the customer's own approval. In that case, mark the status "Customer approval copy, not for circulation".
2. Check each result and each quote separately. A customer's name permission does not cover a new number or quote. For each item in the notes:
   - Listed in proof-points.md with a fitting status: use as written there.
   - Not listed: keep it in the permission check as "Needs customer approval", and use it in the draft inside a marked placeholder such as [needs approval: quote from notes]. Never present it as cleared.
3. Sort the notes into situation, why they chose, rollout and result. Use only what the notes or proof-points.md say. If a section has nothing behind it, write a placeholder such as [before-state detail needed] and ask the marketer for it.
4. Do not tidy quotes beyond removing filler words. Never write a quote the customer did not say. If the wording changes meaning, ask.
5. Check every product statement in the customer's words against product.md. If the customer describes something the product does not do, leave it out and flag it under Unknown.
6. Write the headline: what happened, in at most 12 words, naming the result or the job. Do not write how we feel about it.
7. Write the sections, keeping the customer as the subject: at most 80 words each for situation, why they chose, rollout and result. Give each number's period and sample where the notes have them.
8. Check the draft against the context pack: permission status, brand voice, words to avoid, what the product is not.
9. List the approval route from channels-and-tools.md for this customer and any pricing mention. A case study goes through this route even if the customer's name is already public, because the story is new. Items already approved in proof-points.md need no new customer approval.
10. List every assumption, inference and gap in the last section. Leave any blank field as a placeholder in square brackets, and never invent a detail to complete a story.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Customer: <name>
Intended use: <Public or one-to-one>

## Permission check
| Item | proof-points.md status | Fits this use? | Action |

## Case study (400 words maximum; this total governs if the field limits add up to more)
Headline: <max 12 words>
At a glance: <industry, size, result, max 25 words>
The situation: <max 80 words>
Why they chose: <max 80 words>
The rollout: <max 80 words>
The result: <max 80 words, with period and sample>
Quote: <exact words, name, title, company>
Next step: <max 15 words>

## Approval route
| Step | Approver | Typical time |

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Was the permission check done first, and does it cover the customer, each number and each quote?
- [ ] Does each item's status fit the intended use?
- [ ] Are quotes the customer's own words?
- [ ] Is every detail in the story found in the notes or proof-points.md?
- [ ] Are items without approval shown as marked placeholders?
- [ ] Is every number shown with its period and sample where known?
- [ ] Is the customer's approval route listed, then the internal approver?
- [ ] Is it free of hype and of words on the avoid list?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Customer:** Brightmere Advisory

**Intended use:** Public

**Permission check**

| Item | Status | Fits? | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| Name and logo | Public, June 2026 | Yes | Use |
| Lena Marsh quote | Public, June 2026 | Yes | Use as written |
| 22% client memo reasoning result | Public, June 2026 | Yes | Use as written |
| Details from interview notes | Not listed | Not yet | Needs customer approval |

**Case study**

**Headline:** Brightmere Advisory's analysts raised client memo reasoning scores 22% in one quarter

**At a glance:** Professional services, 400 learners. Analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026.

**The situation:** [Before-state detail needed from interview notes]

**Why they chose:** [Reason needed from interview notes]

**The rollout:** [Detail needed from interview notes]

**The result:** Brightmere Advisory's analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners.

**Quote:** "My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory

**Next step:** Start an 8-week paid pilot for up to 40 learners.

**Approval route**

| Step | Approver | Typical time |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Brightmere Advisory, new story and details | 2 weeks for both steps |
| 2 | Marta Ilves, CEO | Included above |

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: no interview notes were supplied for this example, so sections wait on them. Any learner's conversation or score in the notes stays out, per the hard rule.
- Unknown: Can you paste the interview notes, how many analysts does the 22% cover, and who at Brightmere signs off on the new details?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a case study". Read the context pack.
Task: <the customer, the interview date, and where it will appear>.
<paste the notes or transcript here>
```


===== FILE: skills/product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-sales-deck.md =====

---
title: Write a sales deck
type: skill
function: product-marketing-and-sales-enablement
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The segment and buyer role for the first call, and whether the deck stays one-to-one or may be posted.
also_for: [pitch deck, first call slides, talk track, sales presentation, slides for sales, intro deck, what to say on a first call]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Writes a first-call deck outline, slide by slide, with a talk track and proof chosen by where the deck will be used, and no design work.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when sales needs slides and a talk track for a first call with one kind of buyer in one segment. Do not use it for a page to hand over; use [Write a sales one-pager](write-sales-one-pager.md) instead. For a named competitor, use [Write a battlecard](write-battlecard.md). For how the slides look, use [Write a design brief](../brand-and-design-briefs/write-design-brief.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: publishable numbers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: what it does and is not, price, pilot, roadmap rules.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: reasons to buy, and triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, persona messages, words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: only what buyers do today, and why they stay.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice and rules.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies the segment, the persona, the call length, and the use. One-to-one means sales presents it or sends it to a named buyer. Open means posted online or sent to a list. If not stated, treat it as open use.

## Procedure

1. Choose one segment and one persona. If none is named, ask before drafting. If several are named, ask which comes first. Do not blend them.
2. Set the proof allowed. Open use takes Public items only. One-to-one use takes Public and Sales only items, keeping any limit in the entry, such as no logo. Internal only, Pending and None never appear. Prefer proof from the same segment, then the same persona (a starting rule). The talk track follows the same rule as the slides.
3. Use these 8 slides in order, for a 30-minute call that leaves half the time for the buyer (a starting rule; for a shorter call, cut slide 6 first):
   - 1 Why we are talking: the segment's trigger.
   - 2 The problem: the persona's daily problem, in their words.
   - 3 What you do today: from competitors.md, with no competitor names.
   - 4 What it does: from product.md only.
   - 5, 6 and 7: one pillar each, in the order in messaging.md.
   - 8 Next step: the pilot or trial in product.md, or [next step needed].
4. Write each slide: a headline of at most 10 words, at most 3 lines of at most 12 words each, and a line naming what it shows, such as a report, with [screenshot needed] where one is missing. Give no layout, color or type direction.
5. Write a talk track for each slide in at most 60 words, as a person would say it. It adds to the slide and does not read it out.
6. Add one question about the buyer's situation on slides 1, 2, 3 and 7.
7. Attach proof to slides 5 to 7. If a pillar has no allowed proof, use a product fact and say so, or write a placeholder such as [progress proof needed]. Leave out any number not in proof-points.md or marked publishable in company.md.
8. Write the backup, shown only if asked: up to 3 objections from personas.md in file order, each answered from the context pack, or [answer needed from sales]. Include price only if product.md allows it and the marketer asked, copied as written. Do not promise a feature or mention a roadmap item.
9. Check the draft against the context pack: what the product is not, words to avoid, brand voice, permission status, hard rules, including anything shown in a screenshot.
10. List the approvals from channels-and-tools.md: sales material, any pricing mention, any customer item not yet approved. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Segment: <one>
Persona: <one>
Use: <One-to-one or open>
Call length: <minutes>

## Slides
Slide <number>: <slide name>
Headline: <max 10 words>
On the slide: <up to 3 lines, max 12 words each>
Shows: <content only, max 10 words>
Talk track: <max 60 words>
Question: <one, on slides 1, 2, 3 and 7 only>
<repeat for all 8 slides>

## Backup (only if asked)
- <Objection>: <answer, max 25 words>
Price: <as written in product.md, or omit this line>

## Draft notes (not on the slides)
- Proof used: <item, permission status, slide, any limit>
- Left out: <item, and why>
- Approvals needed: <item, approver, typical time>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does it speak to one segment and one persona, in 8 slides?
- [ ] Does every proof item, on a slide or in the talk track, fit the stated use and keep its limits?
- [ ] Is every product statement backed by product.md, with no roadmap items?
- [ ] Are competitor names absent from the slides and the talk track?
- [ ] Is the deck free of layout, color and type direction?
- [ ] Are approvers listed for sales material and any price?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Segment:** Professional services firms

**Persona:** Head of learning and development

**Use:** One-to-one

**Call length:** 30 minutes

**Slides** (three of 8 shown)

**Slide 3: What you do today**

Headline: The workshop ends, and nobody knows what changed

On the slide: An annual workshop. Telling staff to check the AI assistant.

Shows: Text only

Talk track: Firms like yours often run a workshop once a year, then ask staff to check what the AI assistant says. It seems free, and earlier training tools went unused.

Question: What happened after your last workshop, and how would you know?

**Slide 7: You can see reasoning improve**

Headline: Show that reasoning improved, team by team

On the slide: Brightmere Advisory: up 22% on client memo reasoning in one quarter. Corvane Software: 2.6 to 3.4 out of 5 in 12 weeks.

Shows: Team-level report, [screenshot needed]

Talk track: Brightmere's partners did the measuring, in 2026. Corvane is a software company, so treat it as a second reference. You see progress by team, never one person's conversation.

Question: What would your partners need to see?

**Slide 8: Next step**

Headline: An 8-week paid pilot for up to 40 learners

**Draft notes**

- Proof used: Brightmere, Public, slide 7. Corvane, Sales only, no logo, slide 7.
- Left out: price, not asked for. Roadmap, not cleared.
- Approvals needed: deck, Tom Ferrant, 2 days.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: sales presents this live or sends it to a named buyer.
- Unknown: which team report can sales show?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a sales deck". Read the context pack.
Task: <the segment, the persona, the call length, and whether use is one-to-one or open>.
```


===== FILE: skills/product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/write-sales-one-pager.md =====

---
title: Write a sales one-pager
type: skill
function: product-marketing-and-sales-enablement
owner: product-marketing-manager
reads: [company, product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The segment, the person it is for, and whether sales will hand it over or it goes online.
also_for: [sales sheet, leave behind, sales collateral, product sheet, flyer, handout for prospects, sales asked for something, demo follow up]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Writes one page for one segment or persona that sales can hand to a buyer, with proof chosen by where the page will be used.
judge: ["Would a salesperson hand this over in a meeting or leave it in a folder?", "If the buyer forwarded this to their boss with no note would the boss see why it matters?", "Is the headline about the buyer's problem or about your product?"]
show_to: a salesperson, and ask which line they would cross out before sending it
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when sales needs one page to hand to one kind of buyer in one segment. Do not use it to handle a named competitor; use [Write a battlecard](write-battlecard.md) instead. For a page on the website, use [Write a web page](../content/write-web-page.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:company]]: publishable numbers and public name.
- [[context-pack:product]]: features, what it is not, price, pilot, roadmap rules.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: why this segment buys, and triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: what the buyer is measured on, daily problem, objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, proof, messages by persona, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: only what buyers do today, and why they stay.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: voice, rules, style details.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: approvers and hard rules.

The marketer supplies: the segment, the persona, and how the page will be used. Use one-to-one means sales hands or sends it to a named buyer. Open use means anywhere online or ungated. If not stated, treat it as open use.

## Procedure

1. Choose one segment and one persona. If none is named, ask before drafting. If the marketer names several, ask which one comes first. Do not blend them.
2. Set the proof allowed. Open use takes Public items only. One-to-one use takes Public and Sales only items, keeping any limit in the entry, such as no logo. Internal only, Pending and None never appear. Prefer proof from the same segment, then the same persona (a starting rule). If a number is not listed in proof-points.md or marked publishable in company.md, leave it out.
3. Write the headline: what happens for this buyer, in at most 10 words. Use the persona's message from messaging.md as the starting point.
4. Write the problem in the buyer's terms, from the persona's daily problem and the segment's reason to buy.
5. Write what the product does for this buyer in at most 40 words, from product.md only.
6. Write three benefit lines, one per pillar. Each has a claim, a proof line and the proof's permission status in the draft notes. If a pillar has no allowed proof, write a placeholder such as [progress proof needed], or use a product fact from product.md and say so.
7. Answer up to two of the persona's objections from personas.md, in file order. If the context pack has no answer to one, write [answer needed from sales]. Do not write an answer that promises a feature. Do not name competitors. Address what buyers do today instead.
8. Include price only if product.md says it may be published and the marketer asked for it. Copy the figures as written.
9. Write one next step, using the pilot or trial in product.md if there is one. Use a placeholder for the sales contact unless the marketer supplies it.
10. Leave a placeholder in square brackets for any blank context pack field this page needs, and ask about it in the last section.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: what the product is not, words to avoid, brand voice, permission status for the stated use. Check that the page body stays within 250 words.
12. List the approvals from channels-and-tools.md: sales material, any pricing mention, any customer item not yet approved in proof-points.md. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Segment: <one>
Persona: <one>
Use: <One-to-one or open>

## Page (250 words maximum in total; this total governs if the field limits add up to more)
Company: <public name and one-line description from company.md>
Headline: <max 10 words>
The problem: <max 40 words>
What it does: <max 40 words>
Benefits:
- <Pillar claim, max 8 words>. <Proof, max 25 words>
- <Pillar claim, max 8 words>. <Proof, max 25 words>
- <Pillar claim, max 8 words>. <Proof, max 25 words>
Customer quote: <exact approved quote, name, title, company, or placeholder>
Common questions (up to two):
- <Objection>: <answer, max 25 words>
Price: <as written in product.md, or omit this line>
Next step: <max 20 words, with contact or placeholder>

## Draft notes (not on the page)
- Proof used: <item, permission status>
- Approvals needed: <item, approver, typical time>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does it speak to one segment and one persona, on one page?
- [ ] Does every proof item have a permission status that fits the stated use?
- [ ] Are quotes exact, with name and title as approved?
- [ ] Is every product statement backed by product.md, with no roadmap items?
- [ ] Is every word on the avoid list absent, and is the voice right?
- [ ] Are competitor names absent from the page?
- [ ] Are approvers listed for sales material and any price?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Segment:** Professional services firms

**Persona:** Head of learning and development

**Use:** Open

**Page**

**Company:** CogniATion makes a specialized AI chatbot that trains employees to think through problems instead of asking for answers.

**Headline:** Show that training changed how your analysts reason

**The problem:** Everyone finishes the course, and nobody can show it changed how they work. Partners worry that junior staff accept AI answers without checking them, in work that reaches clients.

**What it does:** CogniATion Coach questions employees about a work problem instead of answering it, then scores their reasoning against a rubric. You see progress by team, never an individual's conversation.

**Benefits:**

- It asks, it does not answer. The Coach asks for assumptions, evidence and alternatives, and declines to give the answer.
- People keep practicing. 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week in Q3 2026.
- You can see reasoning improve. Brightmere Advisory's analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners.

**Customer quote:** "My analysts stopped asking the bot for the answer and started defending their own." Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory

**Common questions:**

- "People won't make time for another training tool": Sessions take about 15 minutes, and 72% of assigned learners completed at least one each week in Q3 2026.
- "How would I show the leadership team that it worked?": Team-level reports show how scores move, with no individual's conversation shown.

**Next step:** Start an 8-week paid pilot for up to 40 learners. [Sales contact needed]

**Draft notes**

- Proof used: all Public; the first benefit is a product fact.
- Approvals needed: Tom Ferrant, 2 days.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Unknown: Which sales contact goes on the page?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a sales one-pager". Read the context pack.
Task: <the segment, the person it is for, and whether use is one-to-one or open>.
```


===== FILE: skills/segmentation-icp-and-personas/build-market-segmentation.md =====

---
title: Build a market segmentation
type: skill
function: segmentation-icp-and-personas
owner: insights-analyst
reads: [icp-and-segments, product, channels-and-tools, goals-and-kpis]
you_bring: A customer export with industry, size, contract value, days to close and status, plus won and lost deals if you have them.
also_for: [which market to target, market segments, who should we sell to, target market, rank segments, focus on one segment, too many markets, where is demand]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Splits the market into segments and ranks them from data the marketer supplies.
judge: ["Would you actually market differently to these segments or are they only different labels?", "Can you reach the top segment through a channel you already have?", "Did the top segment win on buying evidence or because its data was easier to find?", "What will you stop doing for the segments at the bottom?"]
show_to: your sales lead, and ask whether the top segment matches the deals they would chase first
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill to split your market into segments and rank them, usually once a quarter or before a new go-to-market plan. Do not use it to set company-level fit rules; use [Define the ICP](define-icp.md) instead. To describe the people inside a segment, use [Write a persona](write-persona.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: current segments, priority order, sales cycle and triggers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: "What it is not" and known weak spots, which can rule a segment out.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: channels that already reach a segment.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: goals and events that already point at a segment.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- A customer export with industry, size, contract value, days to close and whether the customer is still active.
- A deal export with won and lost deals, if available.
- Any market size figures, each with its source. Without them, market size stays unknown.

## Procedure

1. Read the inputs. If neither the marketer's data nor the segments in icp-and-segments.md exist, ask for one of them and stop. If only the context pack exists, proceed and mark every rank as provisional.
2. Group the companies by shared need and way of buying. Keep two groups separate only if they differ on what triggers the purchase, who signs or how long the deal takes. Merge them if they differ only by industry label. End with 3 to 6 segments.
3. Apply the product gate. If a segment's main need matches an item under "What it is not" in product.md, move it to "Not served" with the reason, and do not score it.
4. Score Need for each remaining segment. Give 3 if an outside party forces the purchase, such as a regulator or the buyer's own customer. Give 2 if the driver is a recurring cost the buyer feels. Give 1 if the purchase is optional.
5. Score Win ease by comparing segments on days to close and price pressure. Give 3 to the best segment, 1 to the worst and 2 to any in between.
6. Score Retention the same way, from active and churned customers. Give 3 to the best, 1 to the worst and 2 to any in between.
7. Score Reach. Give 3 if a channel in channels-and-tools.md or an event in goals-and-kpis.md already targets the segment. Give 2 if it is reachable through inbound or new spend alone. Give 1 if no route is known.
8. When data for a score is missing, give it 1, write "unknown" beside it and mark the segment's rank provisional. Do not infer a score from general knowledge of an industry.
9. Add the scores and rank the segments. Break ties by Retention, then Win ease. The scoring scale and the segment count are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks. Market size is shown but not scored, because it is often unavailable and a large segment you cannot win is no use.
10. Write the recommendation: the top segment gets first call on effort, the second gets a defined smaller share of effort, and the rest get inbound only or nothing. Say which.
11. Check the draft against the context pack: compare the rank with the priority order in icp-and-segments.md and list every difference with the data behind it. Keep internal numbers marked internal.
12. List everything you assumed, estimated, inferred or could not find in the last section, including each unknown score and missing market size.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Data used: <sources and row counts, one line>

## Ranked segments
| Rank | Segment | Need | Win ease | Retention | Reach | Total | Share of customers | Market size |
<one row per segment, 3 to 6 rows; scores 1 to 3; "unknown" beside any defaulted score; market size with source, or "unknown">

## Segment definitions
- <segment name>: <shared need, way of buying and how to recognize a company in it, up to 50 words each>

## Not served
- <segment and reason from product.md, up to 25 words each, or "None">

## Recommendation
<where effort goes first, second and not at all, up to 80 words>

## Changes to icp-and-segments.md
- <field, current text, proposed text, data behind it, up to 40 words each, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every score trace to pasted data or a named context pack file, or say "unknown"?
- [ ] Are segments split by need and way of buying, not by industry label alone?
- [ ] Is every scored segment's main need something the product can meet?
- [ ] Is market size either sourced or marked unknown?
- [ ] Is every difference from the current priority order listed with its data?
- [ ] Do the totals add up, and do ties follow the stated rule?
- [ ] Are internal numbers marked internal, so this output does not get reused in public copy?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Data used:** icp-and-segments.md, product.md and goals-and-kpis.md only, no export pasted. All ranks provisional.

**Ranked segments**

| Rank | Segment | Need | Win ease | Retention | Reach | Total | Share of customers (internal) | Market size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Professional services | 3 | 3 | 1 unknown | 3 | 10 | 45% | unknown |
| 2 | Financial services | 2 | 1 | 3 | 2 | 8 | 20% | unknown |
| 3 | Software companies | 1 | 2 | 1 unknown | 2 | 6 | 35% | unknown |

**Segment definitions**

- Professional services: consulting, accounting and law. Junior staff's reasoning reaches clients. Fastest sales cycle.
- Financial services: banks, insurers and asset managers. Analysts' judgment carries risk. Longest security review.
- Software companies: buy to build decision-making in new managers. Most price-sensitive.

**Not served:** Buyers wanting a course library, which CogniATion Coach is not.

**Recommendation:** Professional services first, with the February campaign. Financial services second, about a quarter of effort (assumption). Software companies inbound only.

**Changes to icp-and-segments.md:** None. The rank matches the current priority order.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Inference: professional services Need 3 treats a client complaint, a trigger in icp-and-segments.md, as outside pressure.
- Inference: professional services Reach 3 comes from the campaign in goals-and-kpis.md. The conference names no segment, so earns no credit.
- Inference: Win ease and Retention come from notes in icp-and-segments.md, with security reviews ranked worse than price pressure.
- Unknown: what are days to close and retention by segment, and any sourced market size?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Build a market segmentation". Read the context pack.
Task: <any market size figures, with sources>.
<paste the customer and deal exports here>
```


===== FILE: skills/segmentation-icp-and-personas/define-icp.md =====

---
title: Define the ICP
type: skill
function: segmentation-icp-and-personas
owner: insights-analyst
reads: [icp-and-segments, product, company, goals-and-kpis]
you_bring: A customer export with one row per customer, lost deals with reasons, notes on why customers succeeded or left, and any research findings.
also_for: [ideal customer, who to target, wrong leads, junk leads, bad fit customers, who to disqualify, target accounts, customers who churn]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Defines fit criteria and disqualifiers from customer data.
judge: ["Do your best customers really look like this or is it who you wish you sold to?", "Does this describe companies that stay and grow or only companies that were easy to close?", "If you had to pick ten accounts today would this make the choice easy?", "Are there enough companies like this to reach your goals?"]
show_to: whoever talks to customers after the sale, and ask which of these companies they would not sign again
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill to define which companies buy, succeed and stay, and which to turn away, from your customer data. Do not use it to rank groups of companies against each other; use [Build a market segmentation](build-market-segmentation.md) instead. The ICP describes companies, so for the people inside them use [Write a persona](write-persona.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: the current company type, size, region, situation, disqualifiers and triggers.
- [[context-pack:product]]: "What it is not" and known weak spots, which become disqualifiers.
- [[context-pack:company]]: internal churn and contract value, as a comparison for the customer data.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: the MQL definition, which depends on the ICP.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- A customer export with one row per customer: industry, employees, sites, team size, region, contract value, days to close, start date, and whether the customer is active or churned.
- A list of lost deals with the reason, if available.
- Any notes on why a customer succeeded or left, and any findings from Synthesize customer research.

## Procedure

1. Read the inputs and count the customer rows. With 20 or more rows, mark criteria as "Data". With fewer than 20 rows or no export, work from pasted findings first, then the context pack, flag where they disagree and follow the findings. Mark every criterion "Hypothesis". Say which applies on the first line of the output.
2. Sort the customers into two groups. "Good outcome" means active for at least 12 months with no known dispute over value. "Poor outcome" means churned, or active under 12 months with a known problem. Leave out customers too new to judge, and report how many.
3. Compare the groups on each attribute, including what they used before. Keep an attribute as a fit criterion only if at least 60% of the good-outcome group shares the value, and the share in the poor-outcome group is at least 20 points lower. These cutoffs, and the 20 rows and 12 months above, are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks.
4. State each criterion as a test someone can apply from public information or one sales call, such as "at least 100 people to train". Reject criteria that require judgment, such as "innovative culture".
5. Write the disqualifiers from three sources: any item under "What it is not" in product.md that a buyer needs, any known weak spot that caused a churn or a loss, and any attribute shared by most of the poor-outcome group. Give the reason for each.
6. List the buying triggers from icp-and-segments.md, plus any trigger appearing in at least 3 customer notes.
7. Write the scoring rule. Tier A meets every criterion and has a trigger. Tier B meets every criterion with no trigger. Tier C misses exactly one criterion. Out misses two or more, or has any disqualifier.
8. Check the draft against the context pack: compare each criterion with icp-and-segments.md, flag every change, and say whether the MQL definition in goals-and-kpis.md still works. Where a needed field is blank, write a placeholder such as [region needed] and ask in the last section.
9. List everything you assumed, estimated, inferred or could not find in the last section, including customers excluded.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Evidence basis: <"Data" with row count and number excluded, or "Hypothesis", one line>

## ICP in one sentence
<company type, size, region and situation, up to 40 words>

## Fit criteria
| Criterion | Test | Evidence | Basis |
<3 to 6 rows; Basis is Data or Hypothesis; Evidence is a count from the export, a count of sources or a named context pack file>

## Disqualifiers
- <disqualifier and reason, up to 25 words each, 2 to 6 items>

## Buying triggers
- <trigger and source, up to 20 words each, 2 to 6 items>

## Scoring rule
| Tier | Test | What sales and marketing do |
<4 rows: A, B, C, Out>

## Changes to the context pack
- <file, field, current text, proposed text, up to 40 words each, or "None">

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the ICP describe companies, with no people or job titles in the criteria?
- [ ] Can each criterion be tested from public information or one sales call?
- [ ] Is each criterion marked Data or Hypothesis, with Data backed by a count?
- [ ] Does each disqualifier give a reason tied to the product or to poor outcomes?
- [ ] Does the scoring rule put every company in exactly one tier?
- [ ] Are all proposed changes to the context pack listed, and none made silently?
- [ ] Would turning away the Out tier be acceptable to sales, if you asked them today?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Evidence basis:** Hypothesis. No customer export pasted, so this works from the context pack.

**ICP in one sentence:** Knowledge-work companies of 300 to 5,000 employees in the United States, Canada and the United Kingdom that have rolled out general AI assistants and have someone responsible for learning and development.

**Fit criteria**

| Criterion | Test | Evidence | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Company type | Consulting, accounting, law, software or financial services | icp-and-segments.md | Hypothesis |
| Size | 300 to 5,000 employees | icp-and-segments.md | Hypothesis |
| People to train | At least 100 | icp-and-segments.md, product.md | Hypothesis |
| Region | United States, Canada or United Kingdom | icp-and-segments.md | Hypothesis |
| AI rollout | General AI assistants already rolled out to staff | icp-and-segments.md | Hypothesis |

**Disqualifiers**

- Fewer than 100 learners: below the product's minimum.
- Wants a course library: CogniATion Coach is not one.
- Wants scores on individuals for performance reviews: the Coach is not for performance decisions, and reports stop at team level.
- Proposed: wants to screen job candidates, because the Coach is not for hiring decisions.

**Buying triggers:** company-wide AI assistant rollout, client complaint about poor reasoning, new head of learning, annual training budget planning.

**Scoring rule:** A meets all criteria with a trigger. B meets all, no trigger. C misses one. Out misses two, or has a disqualifier.

**Changes to the context pack:** Add the hiring disqualifier to icp-and-segments.md.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Inference: the proposed disqualifier comes from "What it is not" in product.md.
- Unknown: can you paste the customer export, so criteria can move from Hypothesis to Data?
- Unknown: should companies needing training in other languages be Out, given the English-only product?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Define the ICP". Read the context pack.
Task: <why customers succeeded or left, if known, plus any research findings>.
<paste the customer export and lost deals here>
```


===== FILE: skills/segmentation-icp-and-personas/synthesize-customer-research.md =====

---
title: Synthesize customer research
type: skill
function: segmentation-icp-and-personas
owner: insights-analyst
reads: [personas, icp-and-segments, messaging, proof-points]
you_bring: The research itself, and for each source the date, company type, role and how the person was chosen.
also_for: [customer interviews, survey results, voice of customer, interview notes, what customers say, call notes, customer feedback, make sense of research]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Turns interview notes, survey exports and call notes into findings with evidence.
---
