**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill to turn interview notes, survey exports or call notes into findings, each backed by evidence you can check. Do not use it for patterns across won and lost deals from a CRM export; use [Analyze wins and losses](../competitive-intelligence/analyze-win-loss.md) instead. To turn the findings into a buyer description, run [Write a persona](write-persona.md) next.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:personas]]: current objections and daily problems, to test against the findings.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments and triggers, to tag each source and compare.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to use, to see which ones the research supports.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: permission statuses, to flag quotes that are not approved for use.

The marketer pastes into the chat:

- The research itself: interview notes, transcripts, survey results or call notes.
- For each source, if known: date, company type, role and how the person was chosen.
- The question the research was meant to answer, if there was one.

## Procedure

1. If no research text was pasted, ask for it and stop. Do not write findings from the context pack alone.
2. Number each source S1, S2 and so on, one per interview, call or survey. Record its type, date, role and segment. Write "unknown" for anything missing.
3. Pull excerpts word for word. Keep every quote exactly as written, and never tidy or combine quotes. Mark notes taken by an interviewer as "notes", not as the person's own words.
4. Tag each excerpt with one theme type: trigger, problem, objection, alternative considered, outcome or language.
5. Group excerpts into findings. Each finding is one plain claim about what buyers do, want or fear, in a full sentence. Do not write findings about what the company should do.
6. Count the sources behind each finding, not the mentions. Rate it Strong with 3 or more sources, Moderate with 2, Weak with 1. For surveys, give the question wording and the number of responses, and report counts rather than percentages under 30 responses. These counts are starting rules the marketer can change.
7. Report contradictions and silences: where sources disagree, show both sides, and note which segments or roles are missing from the sample. Say if the sample was chosen in a way that skews it, such as only current customers.
8. Rank findings by strength, then by how directly they answer the research question. Keep at most 7. Move the rest to the excerpt list.
9. Write one implication for each Strong or Moderate finding, naming the next skill to run. For a Weak finding, the only allowed implication is what to ask next.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: for each finding, say whether personas.md, icp-and-segments.md or messaging.md confirms it, contradicts it or is silent. Propose changes, but do not make them.
11. Mark quotes for use. A quote is internal only unless proof-points.md shows it with a permission status that allows the use. To use one publicly, the marketer must get approval first.
12. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not determine in the last section, including gaps in the sample.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Sources: <number and type of sources, date range, one line>
Sample gaps: <roles, segments or outcomes missing, up to 30 words>

## Findings
| ID | Finding | Strength | Sources | Context pack |
<up to 7 rows; finding up to 25 words; Sources lists IDs; Context pack is confirms, contradicts or silent, with the file name>

## Evidence
For each finding:
F<number>
- S<number>: "<quote, up to 30 words>" or notes: <paraphrase, up to 30 words>
<up to 3 excerpts per finding>

## Contradictions
- <what disagrees and which sources, up to 30 words each, or "None">

## Implications
- F<number>: <what follows and which skill to run next, up to 30 words>

## Proposed changes to the context pack
- <file, field, proposed text and the finding behind it, up to 40 words each, or "None">

## Quote permissions
- <quote reference and status from proof-points.md, or "internal only, no permission on file">

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every finding list the sources behind it, counted by source and not by mention?
- [ ] Is every quote word for word, with notes marked as notes?
- [ ] Are Weak findings limited to what to ask next?
- [ ] Are contradictions and sample gaps stated rather than smoothed over?
- [ ] Does each finding say what the context pack does with it?
- [ ] Is every quote marked with its permission status before anyone uses it?
- [ ] Are findings about buyers, with recommendations kept in the implications?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Sources:** 5 sources: four persona entries (S1 to S4) and one approved customer quote (S5), standing in for pasted notes.

**Sample gaps:** no lost deals or churned customers, and no software or financial services sources.

**Findings**

| ID | Finding | Strength | Sources | Context pack |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Leaders doubt the reasoning behind confident junior work. | Strong | S2, S3, S4 | Confirms icp-and-segments.md, situation |
| F2 | Buyers doubt staff will use another tool, especially one withholding answers. | Moderate | S1, S4 | Confirms messaging.md, pillar 2 |
| F3 | People leaders fear staff will feel watched. | Weak | S2 | Confirms messaging.md, VP message |

**Evidence**

F1

- S2: "The partners keep telling me juniors can't think without the chatbot."
- S3: "I read work that sounds confident and hasn't been thought through."
- S4: "I get the draft back with 'why?' written on it, and I don't know how to answer."

F2

- S1: "People won't make time for another training tool."
- S4: "Why would I use a bot that won't give me the answer?"

**Contradictions:** S4 doubts a bot that withholds answers, while S5 says analysts "started defending their own."

**Implications**

- F1: lead the professional services campaign with pillar 3, using [Write a campaign brief](../campaign-design/write-campaign-brief.md).
- F2: test it in the head of learning entry, using [Write a persona](write-persona.md).
- F3: ask VPs of people what would make staff feel watched.

**Proposed changes to the context pack:** None.

**Quote permissions:** S1 to S4: internal only, no permission on file. S5: public, approved June 2026.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: no research batch exists for CogniATion, so personas.md and proof-points.md stand in.
- Unknown: can you paste real interview notes?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Synthesize customer research". Read the context pack.
Task: <the question the research was meant to answer>.
<paste the notes or survey results here>
```


===== FILE: skills/segmentation-icp-and-personas/write-interview-guide.md =====

---
title: Write an interview guide
type: skill
function: segmentation-icp-and-personas
owner: insights-analyst
reads: [icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, product, proof-points, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: What you need to learn, and a list of customers or lost deals to choose from if you have one.
also_for: [customer interviews, lost deal interviews, talk to customers, interview questions, why we lost deals, voice of the customer, recruit customers for research]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: Writes the questions, recruiting list and invitation for customer or lost-deal conversations, plus a note-taking format that feeds Synthesize customer research.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this before you talk to customers, prospects or buyers from lost deals, when you have a question and no notes yet. Do not use it once you have notes; use [Synthesize customer research](synthesize-customer-research.md) instead. For patterns in a CRM export of won and lost deals, use [Analyze wins and losses](../competitive-intelligence/analyze-win-loss.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, fit criteria and triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: roles, objections and daily problems, to test.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, to keep out of the questions.
- [[context-pack:product]]: limits and roadmap rules, so the interviewer promises nothing.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: permission status of customers and quotes.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: hard rules.

The marketer supplies the research question, the kind of conversation (customer, prospect or lost deal) and minutes per conversation. Optional: a list with only name, title, company, segment, outcome, date and account owner.

## Procedure

1. Fix one research question and the decision it informs. If there are several, ask which comes first. If there is none, ask before drafting.
2. Set the sample: at least 5 conversations per group, a group being one kind of conversation in one segment (a starting rule). Synthesize customer research rates a finding Strong at 3 sources, and five leaves room for that.
3. Build the recruiting list from the pasted list and proof-points.md only. Never invent a name. With no list, write the selection criteria and rows of [name needed]. Rank by fit with the ICP, then a purchase or loss in the last 6 months (a starting rule), then a group the context pack says little about. Flag anyone with an open deal or dispute. Name who makes each introduction, or [account owner needed].
4. Write the invitation in at most 80 words: why you are asking, the length, no sales pitch, and that nothing is quoted or named without written approval. For lost deals, add that you are not reopening the deal. Mention an incentive only if the marketer approved one. Nothing is sent; outreach comes back to the marketer.
5. Write at most 10 open questions for 30 minutes, in proportion for other lengths (a starting rule). Ask about what happened, not what the person would do. Tag each with one theme type from Synthesize customer research, and cover all six: trigger, problem, objection, alternative considered, outcome, language. For lost deals, ask what they chose and what has happened since.
6. Check the draft against the context pack: each question tests an entry in personas.md, icp-and-segments.md or messaging.md without quoting it. No pillar wording, product name or competitor name appears until the person brings it up. Nothing breaks a hard rule.
7. Write the interviewer rules: no pitching, no promises about features or roadmap, ask "why?" and then wait. The person who sold the deal does not run the conversation (a starting rule).
8. Write the note-taking format, one block per conversation, in the fields Synthesize customer research uses. The person's own words go in quotation marks. Anything else is marked notes.
9. Set the next steps: paste all blocks into [Synthesize customer research](synthesize-customer-research.md). Every quote stays internal until cleared with [Request customer proof](../product-marketing-and-sales-enablement/request-customer-proof.md).
10. List everything assumed or unknown in the last section, including any group the sample leaves out.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft
Research question: <one, up to 25 words>
Decision it informs: <up to 20 words>
Conversations: <number, kind, minutes each>

## Recruiting list
| Rank | Person, title and company | Group | Why them | Who introduces |

## Invitation
Subject: <50 characters at most>
Body: <80 words at most>

## Interview guide
Opening: <what the interviewer says, up to 40 words>
| # | Question | Theme type | What it tests |
<up to 10 rows>

## Interviewer rules
- <up to 5 lines>

## Note-taking format
Source: S<number>
Type and date: <customer or lost deal, date>
Role and segment: <role, segment>
How chosen: <list rank or introduction>
Excerpts: <one per line>
- <theme type>: "<word for word>" or notes: <paraphrase>
Quote status: internal only, unless proof-points.md says otherwise

## Next steps
<When to paste the notes, and into which skill, up to 25 words>

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Is there one research question, tied to one decision?
- [ ] Does every name on the list come from the marketer or the context pack?
- [ ] Do the questions ask about past events, with no pillar wording?
- [ ] Are all six theme types covered?
- [ ] Does the invitation say that nothing is quoted without written approval?
- [ ] Does the note format separate word-for-word quotes from notes?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Research question:** Why do professional services firms move past the annual workshop, or stay with it?

**Decision it informs:** Which pillar leads the February 15 campaign.

**Conversations:** 5 customers and 5 lost deals, 30 minutes each.

**Recruiting list**

| Rank | Person, title and company | Group | Why them | Who introduces |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Lena Marsh, head of learning, Brightmere Advisory | Customer | Same segment | [account owner needed] |
| 2 | [name needed] | Lost deal | Lost in the last 6 months | [account owner needed] |

**Invitation**

Subject: 30 minutes on how you chose training

Body: [First name], we are trying to understand how firms decide on training for how people reason. Could I ask you about it for 30 minutes? I will not sell you anything, and nothing gets quoted or named without your written approval.

**Interview guide** (three of 10 questions)

| # | Question | Theme type | What it tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What happened that made you look at this? | Trigger | Triggers in icp-and-segments.md |
| 2 | What did you do before, and what happened after? | Alternative considered | What buyers do today |
| 3 | Tell me about the last time you had to show training worked. | Problem | Head of learning objection |

**Next steps:** After the tenth conversation, paste all notes into Synthesize customer research.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: Lena Marsh is willing to talk.
- Unknown: can you paste lost deals from the CRM?
- Unknown: the sample leaves out software and financial services. Is that acceptable?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write an interview guide". Read the context pack.
Task: write the guide for <customer or lost-deal> conversations, to answer <research question>. Any list of names is pasted below.
```


===== FILE: skills/segmentation-icp-and-personas/write-persona.md =====

---
title: Write a persona
type: skill
function: segmentation-icp-and-personas
owner: insights-analyst
reads: [personas, icp-and-segments, messaging, proof-points, product]
you_bring: The role, the segment if it differs, and any evidence such as interview quotes, call notes or sales comments.
also_for: [buyer persona, who is the buyer, target audience, decision maker, buying committee, what the buyer cares about, buyer objections, customer profile]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-02
summary: One buying persona with job, objections and language.
judge: ["Could you name a real person who fits this and have you spoken to them?", "Does this change what you would write tomorrow or could any marketer have guessed it?", "Would this person describe their problem in these words or are the words yours?", "What does this person need to show their boss before they can say yes?"]
show_to: a customer in this job or a salesperson who sells to them, and ask what sounds wrong
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this skill to write or update one buying persona: a person in the buying decision, described by role, job, objections and language. Do not use it for raw research notes; use [Synthesize customer research](synthesize-customer-research.md) first and bring the findings here. To define which companies to target, use [Define the ICP](define-icp.md) instead.

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:personas]]: the existing entry for this role, its measures, daily problem, objections and information sources.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, triggers and the buying process.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: the message for this persona, words to use and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: proof that answers objections, with permission status.
- [[context-pack:product]]: features and limits, so that answers to objections stay true.

The marketer states in the prompt:

- The role, and the segment if the persona differs by segment.
- Any evidence to add: interview quotes, call notes, research findings or sales comments.

## Procedure

1. Pick one role. If the marketer names several, write the first and list the rest under open questions. If the role is missing from personas.md and no evidence was pasted, ask for evidence. If the marketer wants a draft anyway, mark every field "Hypothesis".
2. Tag each field by source: "Pack" for the context pack, "Pasted" for the marketer's evidence, "Hypothesis" for anything else. When pasted evidence contradicts the context pack, show both and flag the conflict rather than choosing one.
3. Set the buying role, using exactly one of these: champion (pushes the purchase), approver (says yes for the department or team), signer (controls the money), daily user (works in the product), blocker (can stop it, such as IT or legal).
4. Write the job from what the person is measured on. Use the measures in personas.md. If that field is blank, write [measures needed] and ask in the last section. Leave out age, hobbies, family, invented names and any trait that does not change what you would say to this person.
5. List the triggers that bring this person into the decision, using only icp-and-segments.md or pasted evidence.
6. List up to 4 objections, in the person's own words where a source has them. Do not write new quotes. A paraphrase must be labeled as a paraphrase.
7. Answer each objection with one true statement from product.md, then one proof point from proof-points.md. A persona is an internal output, but its answers get reused, so use public proof only by default. If no public proof fits, write [proof needed], and name any sales-only item that could fit in one-to-one material without quoting it. Never use pending or none items. Where the product has a real limit, say so.
8. Build the language section: words this person uses, taken from quotes and sources only, and words to avoid, taken from messaging.md, plus any the evidence shows they dislike.
9. Write one message for this persona. Use the line from messaging.md if there is one. Write a new one only if the evidence supports a change, and label it "Proposed".
10. Check the draft against the context pack: test each answer against "What it is not" and known weak spots in product.md, and each proof point against its permission status.
11. List everything you assumed, inferred or could not find in the last section, including each field tagged Hypothesis.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Persona
Role and title: <up to 12 words> | Source: <Pack, Pasted or Hypothesis>
Buying role: <champion, approver, signer, daily user or blocker>
Segment: <segment name or "All">

## Job
Measured on: <up to 25 words> | Source
Daily problem: <one quote or paraphrase, up to 30 words> | Source

## Triggers
- <trigger, up to 15 words, 1 to 4 items> | Source

## Objections and answers
| Objection | Answer | Proof and permission |
<1 to 4 rows; answer up to 30 words>

## Language
Words they use: <up to 10, from sources only>
Words to avoid: <from messaging.md and evidence>

## Message
<one sentence, up to 25 words, marked "Proposed" if new>

## Where they get information
- <source, up to 10 words, 1 to 4 items> | Source

## Assumptions and open questions
- Assumption: <what you took as true, and why>
- Estimate: <the number, and how you worked it out>
- Inference: <what you concluded, and from which inputs>
- Unknown: <what you need from the marketer, as a question>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the persona cover one role only?
- [ ] Is every field tagged with its source?
- [ ] Is every quote traceable to a source, with no written-up quotes?
- [ ] Does every proof point carry a permission status that allows this use?
- [ ] Do the answers to objections stay within what the product does?
- [ ] Are demographics and invented personal details left out?
- [ ] Are all words from the "words to avoid" list kept out of the message?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Persona**

Role and title: head of learning and development, reports to the VP of people | Source: Pack

Buying role: champion

Segment: Professional services firms

**Job**

Measured on: participation in programs, manager feedback, proof that training changed behavior | Source: Pack

Daily problem: "Everyone finishes the course, and nobody can show me it changed how they work." | Source: Pack

**Triggers:** company-wide AI assistant rollout, client complaint about poor reasoning | Source: Pack

**Objections and answers**

| Objection | Answer | Proof and permission |
|---|---|---|
| "People won't make time for another training tool." | Sessions take about 15 minutes and use work problems. | 72% of assigned learners completed at least one session each week, Q3 2026, public |
| "How would I show the leadership team that it worked?" | Reports show progress on six thinking skills by team, and stop at team level. | Brightmere Advisory's analysts raised their average score on client memo reasoning by 22% in one quarter of 2026, as measured by Brightmere's partners, public |

**Language**

Words they use: finishes, course, changed how they work, leadership team

Words to avoid: revolutionary, world-class, brain training, unlock your potential, smarter, superintelligent, IQ

**Message:** Show that training changed how people reason, not only that they finished it.

**Where they get information:** peers in other companies, learning and development associations, conferences | Source: Pack

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the triggers above are the two most relevant to this role, picked from the list in icp-and-segments.md.
- Unknown: does this persona change in financial services, where the security review runs longest?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write a persona". Read the context pack.
Task: <the role, and the segment if it differs>.
<paste quotes, call notes, findings or sales comments here>
```


===== FILE: skills/seo/audit-seo-from-exports.md =====

---
title: Audit SEO from exports
type: skill
function: seo
owner: seo-specialist
reads: [product, icp-and-segments, messaging, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: At least one search console or crawl export, with dates of any site changes and the pages that matter most.
also_for: [website traffic dropped, organic traffic down, seo problems, site audit, pages not ranking, technical seo, website not found in search, lost rankings]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Turns pasted search console and crawl exports into a ranked list of fixes with evidence and owners.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this monthly, or after a site change, when you have search console or crawl exports and need to know what to fix first. Do not use it to plan new topics; use [Build a keyword map](build-keyword-map.md) instead. To rebuild one page after the audit, use [Write an SEO page brief](write-seo-page-brief.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product is and is not.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, to decide which pages matter most.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for replacement titles and descriptions.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: the organic KPI and its baseline.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who can change what on the site.

The marketer pastes at least one export. The search console export needs page, query if available, clicks, impressions, CTR and average position, for two equal periods with dates. The crawl export needs URL, status code, indexability, canonical, title, meta description, inlinks and click depth. Also useful: dates of site changes and a list of the pages that matter most.

## Procedure

1. Inventory the data: file, date range, row count and columns. If a row count is a round limit, such as exactly 1,000, flag it as possibly truncated. If no export is pasted, stop and ask. Any check whose columns are missing goes under Not checked.
2. Tag each URL as ICP page or Other. It is an ICP page if the marketer listed it, or if its topic matches a segment or the product. Label topic matches as an inference. If icp-and-segments.md is blank, write [ICP needed] and ask in the last section.
3. Run these checks in order. Every finding names URLs and quotes numbers from the export. The cutoffs are starting rules the marketer can change, not benchmarks.
   - Blocked: status 4xx or 5xx, noindex, or a canonical pointing elsewhere.
   - Lost ground: clicks down 25% or more between periods, from at least 20 clicks.
   - Near win: average position 4 to 15, with impressions in the top quarter of the export.
   - Weak snippet: position 10 or better with CTR below this export's median for its band (1 to 3, or 4 to 10), or a title over 60 characters, or a meta description missing or over 155.
   - Competing pages: two or more URLs each holding at least 10% of a query's impressions.
   - Structure: an ICP page with 2 inlinks or fewer, or click depth over 3.
   - Off target: impressions driven by queries for something product.md says the product is not.
4. Rate impact. High: an ICP page with a Blocked, Lost ground or Near win finding. Medium: an ICP page with any other finding, or an Other page that is Blocked. Low: everything else.
5. Rate effort from who can make the change, using channels-and-tools.md. Low: one page, in the CMS, by the marketer. Medium: a page rewrite. High: engineering, or a change across the site.
6. Rank fixes in this order: High impact and Low effort, High and Medium, Medium and Low, High and High, Medium and Medium, then the rest. Keep the top 10. The rest go under Parked with a reason.
7. Write each fix: what to change, on which URL, who does it, and the number to check in next month's export. For a title or description problem, draft the replacement within the limits and the brand voice.
8. State causes only as far as the data shows. Label a cause as an inference and name the inputs, or write "cause unknown". Do not mention search engine updates or competitor moves unless the marketer supplied them.
9. Compare with the organic KPI only if the marketer pasted the same measure. Clicks in a search console export are not the same as visits in a web analytics tool. If the KPI is blank, write [organic KPI needed].
10. Check the draft against the context pack: replacement titles use no word to avoid, promise only what product.md supports, and owners match channels-and-tools.md.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## SEO audit: <site>, <periods compared>

Bottom line: <biggest problem and first fix, 2 sentences at most>
Data used: <each export, dates, row count, 40 words at most>

## Prioritized fixes
Limit: 10 rows, ranked.
| # | Fix | URL | Check | Evidence from export | Impact | Effort | Who | Next-month test |

## Draft titles and descriptions
- <URL>: <title, 60 characters at most> / <description, 155 characters at most>

## KPI comparison
<one line: KPI, baseline, current figure and source, or [figure needed]>

## Parked
- <URL or issue>: <reason, 15 words at most>

## Not checked
- <check>: <missing column or export>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every finding quote a URL and a number from your exports?
- [ ] Do the rankings follow the impact and effort rules, with nothing moved by feel?
- [ ] Are checks with missing data listed under Not checked, not skipped silently?
- [ ] Is every cause labeled as an inference or marked unknown?
- [ ] Does each fix name an owner who has access, per channels-and-tools.md?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**SEO audit: cogniation.example, September and October 2026**

Bottom line: The professional services page is blocked from search. Remove the noindex first.
Data used: search console by page (400 rows), crawl (210 URLs).

**Prioritized fixes**

| # | Fix | URL | Check | Evidence from export | Impact | Effort | Who | Next-month test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Remove noindex | /professional-services | Blocked | Noindex, 0 clicks | High | Low | Marketer | Impressions above 0 |
| 2 | Rewrite title | /critical-thinking-training | Near win | Position 7.2, 4,100 impressions, CTR 0.9%, band median 2.1% | High | Low | Marketer | CTR above band median |
| 3 | Add three inlinks | /scenario-library | Structure | 1 inlink, depth 4 | Medium | Low | Marketer | 4 inlinks |

**Draft titles and descriptions**

- /critical-thinking-training: Critical thinking training that asks instead of answers / CogniATion Coach questions employees about work problems, declines to give the answer and scores their reasoning. You see progress by team.

**KPI comparison**

Organic visits from ICP pages: baseline 9,400, Q3 2026. Current: [figure needed].

**Parked**

- /company-news: Other page, low impact.

**Not checked**

- Competing pages and Off target: no query by page export.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all URLs, exports and figures are invented for this example.
- Inference: /professional-services is an ICP page, because its topic matches the first-priority segment in icp-and-segments.md.
- Unknown: was the noindex deliberate?
- Unknown: what is the web analytics figure for organic visits?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Audit SEO from exports". Read the context pack.
Task: <the two periods compared>.
<paste the search console and crawl exports here>
```


===== FILE: skills/seo/build-keyword-map.md =====

---
title: Build a keyword map
type: skill
function: seo
owner: seo-specialist
reads: [product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: A keyword export with volume, its source and date, and a list of your existing pages.
also_for: [keyword research, seo plan, seo topics, which keywords, website content plan, organic traffic, what pages do we need, content gaps]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Clusters keywords from a pasted export, sets intent and priority, and maps each cluster to one page.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you have a keyword export and need to decide which page targets which cluster. Do not use it for one page you have already chosen; use [Write an SEO page brief](write-seo-page-brief.md) instead. To fix pages from performance data, use [Audit SEO from exports](audit-seo-from-exports.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: what the product does and is not.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: segments, priority order and disqualifiers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: who has which problem, and their words.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars and words to avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: competitor names and dated facts, for comparison pages.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: how often the marketer publishes.

The marketer pastes a keyword export (keyword, monthly volume, difficulty if available, plus the export's source and date) and a list of existing pages (URL and title).

## Procedure

1. Stop and ask if no keyword list is pasted. Never supply volumes, difficulty or rankings yourself. If the list has no volume column, write "unknown" in the Volume field and continue. If the page list is missing, write [page check needed] in the Action field and ask in the last section.
2. Clean the list. Merge duplicates and close variants under the highest-volume variant. Count rows in and rows kept.
3. Exclude. Move a keyword to the excluded table, with its reason, if it names something product.md says the product is not, matches a disqualifier, targets a region outside the ICP, or is about jobs, salaries or free downloads with no link to buying. High volume does not save it.
4. Set one intent per keyword:
   - Buy: software, pricing, vendor, demo, "best" plus the category.
   - Compare: "vs", "alternative", a competitor's name.
   - Solve: how to, checklist, template, exercise.
   - Learn: what is, meaning, definition.
   If the wording fits two, take the one closer to buying.
5. Cluster. Two keywords go together only if they share an intent and one page could fully answer both. Name the cluster after its highest-volume keyword.
6. Assign a segment and a persona. Use the segment whose name or buying reason matches the keyword. If two match, take the one higher in the priority order. If none matches, write "All". Use the persona whose job or daily problem matches, or the first listed on a tie (a starting rule). If segments or the priority order are blank, write [segment priority needed] and ask in the last section.
7. Assign one page and one action per cluster:
   - Update: an existing page has the same topic and intent.
   - Consolidate: two or more existing pages cover the cluster. Name the one to keep.
   - New: no page exists. Page type follows intent: Buy gets a product or solution page, Compare a comparison page, Solve a guide or blog post, Learn a blog post.
   - Hold: a Compare cluster names a competitor and competitors.md has no dated fact about it.
8. Set the tier. Tier 1: Buy or Compare in the first or second priority segment, or All. Tier 2: Solve in those segments, or Buy or Compare in any other segment. Tier 3: everything else. Within a tier, sort by volume, high to low, with unknown last. Break ties by lower difficulty.
9. Pick the next three briefs: the first three rows that are not Hold. Say how long they take at the publishing rate in channels-and-tools.md, labeled as an inference.
10. Check the draft against the context pack: each kept cluster needs a page that product.md and messaging.md can support, with no roadmap item and no word to avoid in its name or slug. Exclude any that fail.
11. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section. Say that volumes come from the marketer's export and were not verified.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## Keyword map: <product>, <export date>

Source: <export source and date the marketer named>
Rows in: <n>. Rows kept: <n>. Clusters: <n>.

## Clusters
Limit: 30 rows.
| # | Cluster | Intent | Volume | Segment | Persona | Action | Page | Tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | <primary keyword> | <intent> | <n or unknown> | <segment or All> | <persona> | <action> | <URL, or page type and slug> | <tier> |

## Keywords by cluster
- <primary keyword>: <the other keywords, comma separated, 9 at most>

## Excluded keywords
| Keyword | Volume | Reason, with file name |

## Next three briefs
1. <cluster>: <why it comes first, 25 words at most>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does every volume come from the pasted export, or say "unknown"?
- [ ] Does each cluster map to exactly one page, and each page to one cluster?
- [ ] Did keywords for what the product is not go to the excluded table, even at high volume?
- [ ] Does every tier follow the rule and the priority order in icp-and-segments.md?
- [ ] Does every Compare cluster that names a competitor have a dated fact in competitors.md, or sit on Hold?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**Keyword map: CogniATion Coach, assumed export**

Source: assumed export.
Rows in: 8. Rows kept: 5. Clusters: 3.

**Clusters**

| # | Cluster | Intent | Volume | Segment | Persona | Action | Page | Tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | critical thinking software for consultants | Buy | 390 | Professional services | Head of learning | New | Solution page, /consulting-firms | 1 |
| 2 | train analysts to check ai | Solve | 720 | Financial services | Head of learning | New | Guide, /check-ai-answers | 2 |
| 3 | new manager decision exercises | Solve | 260 | Software companies | Analyst or new manager | New | Blog post, /new-manager-decisions | 3 |

**Keywords by cluster**

- critical thinking software for consultants: critical thinking training for consultants
- train analysts to check ai: teach analysts to question ai

**Excluded keywords**

| Keyword | Volume | Reason, with file name |
|---|---|---|
| pre-employment critical thinking test | 1,300 | Not for hiring, product.md |
| ai memo writer | 880 | Will not write the memo, product.md |
| learning and development jobs | 2,400 | No link to buying |

**Next three briefs**

1. critical thinking software for consultants: first-priority segment, closest to buying.
2. train analysts to check ai: second-priority segment, and AI assistant rollouts trigger buying.
3. new manager decision exercises: this segment is served through inbound only.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: all keywords, volumes and slugs are invented.
- Assumption: no existing page covers these clusters.
- Inference: three pages take about six weeks at about two blog posts a month, from channels-and-tools.md.
- Unknown: can solution pages ship alongside the blog schedule?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Build a keyword map". Read the context pack.
Task: <the export's source and date>.
<paste the keyword export and page list here>
```


===== FILE: skills/seo/write-seo-page-brief.md =====

---
title: Write an SEO page brief
type: skill
function: seo
owner: seo-specialist
reads: [product, icp-and-segments, personas, messaging, competitors, proof-points, brand-voice, goals-and-kpis, channels-and-tools]
you_bring: The topic and primary keyword, the titles and headings of the top results, the volume, and URLs for internal links.
also_for: [seo brief, rank for a keyword, brief a writer, blog brief, page brief, website content brief, search traffic, outrank a competitor page]
version: 0.1.0
updated: 2026-11-01
summary: Produces a brief for one page targeting one topic, ready to hand to the content lead.
---

**Draft only.** The model does not know your market. It produces a draft, plan, analysis or review, and you decide what to use.

## When to use

Use this when you have chosen one topic and one page and need a brief the content lead can write from. Do not use it to choose topics; use [Build a keyword map](build-keyword-map.md) instead. To write the page itself, use [Write a web page](../content/write-web-page.md) or [Write a blog post](../content/write-blog-post.md).

## Inputs

- [[context-pack:product]]: features and limits.
- [[context-pack:icp-and-segments]]: the segment, its buying reason and its triggers.
- [[context-pack:personas]]: the reader's daily problem and objections.
- [[context-pack:messaging]]: pillars, words to use and avoid.
- [[context-pack:competitors]]: dated facts, if the page compares.
- [[context-pack:proof-points]]: evidence and permission status.
- [[context-pack:brand-voice]]: rules for title and description.
- [[context-pack:goals-and-kpis]]: the conversion that counts, current campaigns.
- [[context-pack:channels-and-tools]]: who approves what.

The marketer supplies the topic and primary keyword. The skill works only from what is pasted, so the marketer also pastes the titles and headings of the top results, the volume, and any URLs available for internal links. Without them, those fields say unknown.

## Procedure

1. Confirm the scope is one topic and one page. If the request holds two intents, brief the one closer to buying and name the other under the last section.
2. Set the intent: Buy, Compare, Solve or Learn. If search results are pasted, use the intent most of them serve. If not, infer it from the keyword's wording and label it as an inference.
3. Set the page type by intent: Buy gets a product or solution page, Compare a comparison page, Solve a guide or blog post, Learn a blog post.
4. Choose one reader: the persona whose daily problem the keyword addresses. If two fit, take the one closer to the buying decision for that segment. If personas.md is blank, ask before drafting.
5. Write the angle in one sentence: what this page says that the pasted results do not, tied to one pillar. With no results pasted, base it on the pillar and mark the gap as unknown.
6. Write the title tag (60 characters at most), meta description (155 at most), H1 and slug. The primary keyword goes in the title, H1 and slug. Follow brand-voice.md.
7. Write the outline: 4 to 8 H2 headings. The first answers the search query directly. For each, state the question it must answer, the keyword it carries if any, and the proof it uses.
8. Choose proof from proof-points.md. A public page uses only items marked public. Leave out sales only, internal only, pending and none, and do not name those customers in the brief. Use a claim from messaging.md that names a customer only if proof-points.md shows permission for that claim. If a section has no usable proof, write [proof needed].
9. Write the do-not-say list: limits from product.md, the words to avoid, and any roadmap item. A Compare page names a competitor only with a dated fact from competitors.md. Any pricing mention goes to the approver in channels-and-tools.md.
10. Choose the page's one action. Buy and Compare pages ask for a demo or the pilot in product.md. Solve and Learn pages offer the next page to read, plus a smaller demo link. Add 3 to 5 internal links from the pasted URLs only, or write [internal link needed: topic].
11. Set the length from the median of the pasted results, or write [length needed]. Name the handoff: the content lead, the skill to run and the approver.
12. Check the draft against the context pack: every feature, number, name and word. Fix anything that contradicts it.
13. List every assumption, estimate, inference and unknown in the last section.

## Output format

```
Status: Draft

## SEO page brief: <topic>

Primary keyword: <keyword>, volume <n and source, or unknown>
Secondary keywords: <up to 6>
Intent: <Buy, Compare, Solve or Learn>
Page type: <type>. Action: <New or Update, with URL>
Reader: <persona, segment>
Angle: <one sentence, 30 words at most>
Pillar: <one pillar from messaging.md>
Page action: <one action>

## Search details
Title tag: <60 characters at most>
Meta description: <155 characters at most>
H1: <70 characters at most>
Slug: <proposed>
Length: <range and basis, or [length needed]>

## Outline
| H2 | Must answer | Keyword | Proof, with permission status |
<4 to 8 rows>

## Do not say
- <up to 6 items>

## Internal links
- <URL or [internal link needed: topic]>

## Competing pages
- <what the pasted results cover and miss, up to 5 lines, or unknown>

## Handoff
To: content lead. Skill: <skill title>. Approver: <from channels-and-tools.md>

## Assumptions and open questions
- <Assumption, Estimate, Inference or Unknown>: <one item, unknowns as questions>
```

## Quality checklist

- [ ] Does the brief cover one topic, one reader and one page action?
- [ ] Are the title tag and meta description inside their limits?
- [ ] Is every proof item marked public in proof-points.md for a public page?
- [ ] Does the page promise only what product.md says the product does?
- [ ] Could the content lead write the page from this brief without asking you anything?

## Example (fictional company)

**Status:** Draft

**SEO page brief: measuring critical thinking training**

Primary keyword: measure critical thinking training, volume unknown
Intent: Solve. Page type: guide. Action: New
Reader: head of learning and development, professional services
Angle: Completion data shows who finished, while rubric scores on real work scenarios show whether reasoning changed.
Pillar: You can see reasoning improve
Page action: Read the next page, plus a demo link

**Search details**

Title tag: How to measure critical thinking training | CogniATion
Meta description: Course completions show who finished. Learn how to score reasoning on real work scenarios and show your leadership team what changed.
H1: How to measure critical thinking training
Slug: /measure-critical-thinking-training
Length: [length needed]

**Outline**

| H2 | Must answer | Keyword | Proof, with permission status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Measure reasoning, not completions | How do I measure this training? | measure critical thinking training | Five-point rubric on six thinking skills, product.md |
| What a measured result looks like | Has anyone shown a change? | None | Brightmere Advisory analysts, 22% on client memo reasoning, 2026, public |
| How to report without watching individuals | Will people feel watched? | None | Team-level reports, product.md |
| How to keep people practicing | Will people make time? | None | 72% of assigned learners, a session each week, Q3 2026, public |

**Do not say**

Roadmap items, reports below team level, claims about intelligence or hiring, any word to avoid.

**Handoff**

To: content lead. Skill: Write a blog post. Approver: the marketer.

**Assumptions and open questions**

- Assumption: the guide format and topic.
- Inference: the intent is Solve, from the keyword's wording.
- Unknown: no search results pasted. What do the top results cover?

## How to run it

```
Use the skill "Write an SEO page brief". Read the context pack.
Task: <the topic and primary keyword>.
<paste top result titles and headings, volume and URLs here>
```
